The unchanged support arc: exact extrema and normals\n\nFor 0<=theta<=theta_sp set\n\n    e_sp(theta)=(cos theta,-sin theta),\n    t_sp(theta)=(-sin theta,-cos theta),\n    R_sp(theta)=c_sp exp(-kappa theta),\n    v_sp(theta)=R_sp(theta)e_sp(theta)=(p(theta),-q(theta)),\n    n_sp(theta)=e_sp(theta)+kappa t_sp(theta).\n\nLet K_sp be the convex hull of 0 and this compact arc. Direct differentiation gives\n\n    p'=-R_sp(kappa cos theta+sin theta)<0,\n    q'= R_sp(cos theta-kappa sin theta).\n\nThus epsilon<=p<=c_sp and q>=0 for every kappa>0. Also\nq/p=tan theta<=1/epsilon. The maximum of q is at the endpoint if\nkappa<=epsilon, and otherwise at theta_c=arctan(1/kappa). This proves\nthe exact formula (2). In the latter case qmax_sp>1. The maximum\nis1 at delta=0, since theta_sp<pi/2. Moreover\n<n_sp(theta),0>=0<R_sp(theta). Arbitrary nonnegative ordinary words and a uniform displacement\n\nFix an ordered ordinary block alpha_1,...,alpha_m, with alpha_j>=0.\nSet r_m=v_m=v_sp(theta_sp)=(epsilon,-1). Work backward. Given\nr_j=(u_j,-y_j), with projected arc point v_j=v_sp(theta_j), let\nalpha=alpha_j. If\n\n    y_j<=kappa[u_j+(alpha-1)c_sp],                       (10)\n\nput v_(j-1)=v_sp(0) and r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v_sp(0).\nOtherwise choose the unique theta in (0,theta_j] solving\n\n    <r_j,e_sp(theta)-kappa^(-1)t_sp(theta)>/R_sp(theta)\n       =1-alpha,                                       (11)\n\nand set v_(j-1)=v_sp(theta), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v_(j-1).\nThese are exact implicit definitions with proved existence and\nuniqueness, not executed root searches or numerical assumptions.\n\nWe prove backward by induction that v_j=Pi_(K_sp)(r_j), that\n\n    u_j>=epsilon,  y_j>=1,  y_j/u_j<=1/epsilon,          (12)\n\nand that before a cap, including the initial endpoint, there is t_j>=0\nsuch that\n\n    r_j=R_sp(theta_j)[(1+t_j)e_sp(theta_j)\n                                  +kappa t_j t_sp(theta_j)]. (13)\n\nAt j=m these statements hold with t_m=0. In case (10), put\nmu=u_j+(alpha-1)c_sp. Then mu>=y_j/kappa>0, and\n\n    r_(j-1)-v_sp(0)=mu n_sp(0)+(0,kappa mu-y_j).\n\nBoth coefficients of these supporting normals are nonnegative. This\nproves projection at the cap. It also gives\ny_(j-1)<=kappa(u_(j-1)-c_sp), so every earlier backward step is capped.\n\nIf (10) fails, the current point cannot already be capped. The left\nside of (11) is1 at theta_j by (13), and is\n(u_j-y_j/kappa)/c_sp<1-alpha at theta=0. Its derivative is\n\n    (kappa+kappa^(-1))(u_j cos theta+y_j sin theta)/R_sp(theta)>0.\n\nThere is exactly one root in (0,theta_j]. Since u_j,y_j are positive,\n(13) shows that the polar angle of r_j is at least theta_j; therefore\n<r_j,t_sp(theta)>=0 at the root. Equation (11) says precisely that\nr_j+(alpha-1)v_sp(theta) is a nonnegative multiple of n_sp(theta).\nThis proves projection and (13) at the preceding index. Finally,\nadding alpha(p,-q), with p>=epsilon and 0<=q/p<=1/epsilon, preserves\n(12). This also covers alpha=0 without deleting that physical entry.\n\nLet\n\n    P=sum_j alpha_j p(theta_(j-1)),\n    Q=sum_j alpha_j q(theta_(j-1)), S=sum_j alpha_j.\n\nThen r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q), P<=c_sp S, and P>=epsilon Q. We next\nbound Q independently of both the length and the size of the steps.\n\nFor two consecutive uncapped angles theta_old<=theta_new, put\nDelta=theta_new-theta_old. Taking the t_sp(theta_old) component in\nthe exact relation r_old=r_new+alpha v_sp(theta_old) gives\n\n    t_old=B(Delta)+A(Delta)t_new,\n    A(Delta)=exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),\n    B(Delta)=exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta/kappa.            (14)\n\nBoth coefficients are nonnegative. With d=1-kappa^2, direct\ndifferentiation gives\n\n    A'-(d/kappa)A\n       =-(1+kappa^(-2))exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta<=0.\n\nThus A<=exp(d Delta/kappa), for all kappa>0. If kappa<1 then\nB<=Delta/kappa and 1+dB<=exp(d Delta/kappa). Consequently\n\n    1+d t_old<=exp(d Delta/kappa)(1+d t_new).\n\nTelescoping from the endpoint gives\nt_j<=[exp(d(theta_sp-theta_j)/kappa)-1]/d<=T_sp.\nIf kappa>=1, A<=1 and B<=Delta/kappa, so (14) instead gives\nt_j<=(theta_sp-theta_j)/kappa<=T_sp. No sign reversal in a\nnegative-d inequality is used. Hence 0<=Q=y_0-1<=H_sp, as asserted.\n\nTwo more bounds repair precisely the places that lost kappa<=epsilon.\nAt every uncapped index, (13) gives\n\n    y_j-q_j=R_sp(theta_j)t_j(sin theta_j+kappa cos theta_j)>=0. (15)\n\nThe same inequality holds at a cap because q_j=0. If the horizontal\nnormal is nonnegative, u_j-p_j>=0. Otherwise theta_j>theta_c,\np_j<=p(theta_c)=kappa qmax_sp, and u_j>=epsilon. At a cap u_j>=p_j.\nTherefore the uniform bound is\n\n    u_j-p_j>=-N_sp. Otherwise\nqmax_sp decreases in epsilon, and\n\n    qmax_sp<= exp(kappa arctan kappa)/sqrt(1+kappa^2).\n\nThe logarithmic derivative of the right side in kappa is arctan kappa,\nso for kappa<=1 it is at most exp(pi/4)/sqrt(2)<2. One elementary\nverification of the last strict inequality uses pi<4 and\nexp(1)<=5/2+2/9=49/18<2sqrt(2), obtained by bounding the factorial\nseries from its third term by a geometric series of ratio1/4.\nIt follows that N_sp<2<=1/epsilon. For b>=1+epsilon^(-2), take any positive\nsigma such that\n\n    sigma(P+epsilon b)<=1,\n    sigma[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=1.                  (17)\n\nDefine\n\n    z_circ=(1,0)-sigma(epsilon+P,-1-Q),\n    U=1-sigma(P+epsilon b)>=0, Y=sigma Q,\n    V_fin={0,v_sp(0),v_0,...,v_m},\n    phi(z)=sigma max_(v in V_fin)<v,z-z_circ>.           (18)\n\nThe finite convex hull of V_fin is contained in K_sp and includes\nevery v_j. The projection inequalities from Section3 therefore hold\non this finite hull. At x_j=z_circ+sigma r_j and z_j=x_j-sigma v_j,\nthe plane with slope sigma v_j is active in phi. Indeed, for every\nv in V_fin, <r_j-v_j,v-v_j><=0. Thus sigma v_j belongs to the\nsubdifferential of phi at z_j. The proximal minimizer of phi at x_j\nis exactly z_j, and grad(env_1 phi)(x_j)=sigma v_j.\n\nFor every nonzero v=(p,-q) in V_fin, p>0 and 0<=q<=p/epsilon. Hence\n\n    <v,-z_circ>\n       =-p+sigma[p(epsilon+P)+q(1+Q)]\n       <=-p+sigma p[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=0.\n\nIt follows that phi=0 on {z_1<=0,z_2>=0}. At (U,Y),\n\n    <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>=sigma[(1-b)epsilon p+q]<=0.         (19)\n\nSo phi(U,y)=0 whenever y>=Y. Both statements remain true of its\nnonnegative envelope, whose zero set is exactly that of phi.\n\nThe local trajectory starts at (1,0), follows every actual ordinary\nstep, and ends that block at (U+sigma epsilon b,Y), with gradient\nsigma(epsilon,-1). The step b then reaches (U,Y+sigma b). Physical\nfirst coordinates decrease and second coordinates increase throughout;\nin particular x_(j),1>=U and 0<=x_(j),2<=Y before the checkpoint.\nThis fact uses only p>0, q>=0, not the sign of a normal.\n\nFor the ONE-envelope global assembly we additionally need bounds at\nthe local proximal points. Monotonicity of backward y gives\n1<=y_j<=1+Q. Together with (15),\n\n    0<= (z_j)_2=sigma[1+Q-y_j+q_j]<=sigma(1+Q). Similarly, by (16),\n\n    (z_j)_1-U=sigma[epsilon(b-1)+u_j-p_j]\n       >=sigma[epsilon(b-1)-N_sp]>=0                  (21)\n\nwhenever b>=B_sp. This is the sole additional threshold used by that\nassembly. The source-format sum assembly uses physical points only\nand consequently needs (19), but not (21).\n\n## 5. One static global envelope and the physical trajectory\n\nUse (4) and the finite planes (18) with sigma_i=1/M_i. Conditions\n(17) hold. In coordinates 0,...,k set\n\n    Phi_i(z)=D_i^2 phi_i((z_i-ell_i)/D_i,z_(i+1)/D_i), i<k,\n    delta_tail=D_k/(1+2s_k),\n    Phi_k(z)=delta_tail max{z_k-ell_k,0},\n    J_sp(z)=max_(0<=i<=k) Phi_i(z),\n    f(x)=min_z [J_sp(z)+||x-z||^2/2].                  (22)\n\nThis is a specified finite maximum of affine planes, including 0.\nIt is nonnegative and J_sp(0)=0. Its proximal minimizer is unique,\nbecause the objective in z is coercive and strictly convex.\n\nHere are the needed envelope properties with their proofs. Write\nz=prox(x) and g=x-z; the minimizing condition is g in partial J_sp(z).\nFor another input x', the two subgradient inequalities give\n||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>, so the gradient candidate is 1-Lipschitz.\nFor an increment w, using z as a trial point gives\nf(x+w)<=f(x)+<g,w>+||w||^2/2. Using the actual proximal point z'\nand the subgradient inequality at z gives\n\n    f(x+w)>=f(x)+<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2.\n\nThese estimates prove differentiability with gradient g, and the\nglobal lower supporting inequality proves convexity. The function is\neverywhere finite, has minimum0, and has 1-Lipschitz gradient.\n\nAt the start of block i the claimed physical state is\n\n    x_j=ell_j+D_j U_j for j<i,\n    x_i=ell_i+D_i,\n    x_j=0 for j>i.                                    (23)\n\nStart from e_0 and use the local trajectory of Section4 in coordinates\ni,i+1. At a candidate GLOBAL proximal point of that local trajectory,\nthe current Phi_i has its stated subgradient. The preceding component\nhas first input U_(i-1), and second input at least\nell_i/D_(i-1)>=Y_(i-1), by (21), so is0. Earlier pieces remain in\ntheir post-checkpoint zero regions. The next component has first\ninput at most0 by (20) and ell_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i), and second\ninput0. All later components, including the terminal hinge, also lie\nin their pre-activation zero regions. Thus Phi_i attains the maximum\nat the candidate proximal point, even in a zero-value tie. Its stated\nsubgradient is a subgradient of J_sp, proving that the local gradient\nis the actual global gradient.\n\nAfter the checkpoint, coordinate i freezes at ell_i+D_i U_i, while\ncoordinate i+1 is\n\n    D_i(Y_i+sigma_i b_i)=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1).\n\nThis proves (23) by induction through the entire supplied physical\nword. No component or constraint is discarded at a transition.\n\nDuring the terminal block, let u be the sum of its already executed\nsteps; 0<=u<=s_k. Its moving coordinate is\nell_k+D_k-u delta_tail. The proximal coordinate is at least ell_k,\nsince D_k-(u+1)delta_tail>=0. All old pieces vanish there. The actual\ngradient is delta_tail e_k at every terminal-block query and at the\nunqueried terminal point. Therefore\n\n    f(x_n)=delta_tail D_k-(s_k+1/2)delta_tail^2\n          =D_k^2/[2(1+2s_k)].                          (24)\n\nFor ALL ordered pairs of samples a,b, include the terminal and any\nminimizer, and write g_a=grad f(x_a), z_a=x_a-g_a, f_a=f(x_a).\nThe construction gives g_a in partial J_sp(z_a) and\nf_a=J_sp(z_a)+||g_a||^2/2. Exact cancellation then yields\n\n    Q_ab=f_a-f_b+<g_a,x_b-x_a>+||g_a-g_b||^2/2\n        =J_sp(z_a)-J_sp(z_b)+<g_a,z_b-z_a><=0.          (25)\n\nFor a minimizer g_star=0 and J_sp(z_star)=0, so no extra argument or\nomitted optimum row is needed. Zeros give equal actual jets and also\nsatisfy (25). The Gram matrix is that of the explicit real vectors\nin R^(k+1), so it is positive semidefinite. There is no independent\noracle branch, reset function, or unverified interpolation premise.\n\n## 6. Exact closest radius for the single-envelope realization\n\nSet the first two coordinates of x_star to\n\n    (x_star)_0=1-sigma_0(epsilon+P_0),\n    (x_star)_1=sigma_0(1+Q_0)=ell_1,\n\nand all later coordinates to0. The first component is at its center,\nthe next has first input0 and second input0, and every later component\nis in its pre-activation zero region. The terminal hinge also vanishes.\nThus J_sp(x_star)=0. Nonnegativity and attainment in (22) show that\nargmin f={J_sp=0}.\n\nBoth endpoint planes of the first component have value0 at x_star.\nMoreover\n\n    e_0-x_star\n      =[(P_0-epsilon Q_0)/c_sp] sigma_0(c_sp,0,0,...)\n         +(1+Q_0) sigma_0(epsilon,-1,0,...).\n\nBoth coefficients are nonnegative by P_0>=epsilon Q_0. Every point\nz of {J_sp=0} has nonpositive inner product with each active endpoint\nslope after subtracting x_star. Hence\n<e_0-x_star,z-x_star><=0. Expanding ||e_0-z||^2 proves that x_star\nis the unique closest minimizer and gives the radius in (5).\nCombining with (24) proves the exact payoff (6).\n\nFor epsilon<=1 and nonnegative P,Q,\n\n    sqrt((epsilon+P)^2+(1+Q)^2)\n       <=P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon<=M.\n\nAlso M_i<=c_sp s_i+max{epsilon b_i,A_sp}. These two estimates give\n(7). For k=0, use J_sp(z)=delta max{z,0}, delta=1/(1+2s_0),\nstarting at1. Its nearest minimizer is0, and the same terminal\ncalculation gives exactly 1/(1+2s_0).\n\n## 7. Source-format realization, with its radius accounted for\n\nFix the same independent parameters and now suppose only\nb_i>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)). For each i<k run Section3 on its actual\nordinary steps divided by2. In this section P_i,Q_i refer to those\nHALF-step blocks, so P_i<=c_sp s_i/2 and Q_i<=H_sp. Use Section4\nwith local selected step beta_i=b_i/2 and\n\n    sigma'_i=1/[c_sp s_i/2+epsilon b_i/2+A_sp].\n\nBoth conditions (17) hold because\nP_i+epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon<=c_sp s_i/2+A_sp.\nLet phi'_i, z'_circ,i, U'_i, Y'_i be the objects in (18) for these\ninputs, and put f_i=env_1 phi'_i. Define\n\n    G_i=sigma'_i beta_i=b_i/[c_sp(a_sp+s_i)+epsilon b_i],\n    D'_0=1, ell'_0=0,\n    D'_(i+1)=D'_i G_i,\n    ell'_(i+1)=D'_i Y'_i,\n    delta'=D'_k/(1+s_k).\n\nThe single source-format objective is\n\n    F(x)=(1/2)sum_(i<k) (D'_i)^2\n               f_i((x_i-ell'_i)/D'_i,x_(i+1)/D'_i)\n          +(1/2)Hub_(delta')(x_k-ell'_k),              (26)\n\nwhere Hub_delta(z) is0 for z<=0, z^2/2 for 0<=z<=delta, and\ndelta z-delta^2/2 for z>=delta. This is the same spiral mechanism\nassembled as a sum of local envelopes instead of a global envelope.\n\nEach local term is convex and 1-smooth on its two coordinates. Summing\ntheir quadratic upper inequalities with coefficient1/2, and adding\nthe terminal term, counts each coordinate at most twice. Thus\n\n    F(x+w)<=F(x)+<grad F(x),w>+||w||^2/2.\n\nTo see directly that this proves a 1-Lipschitz gradient in this convex\ncase, apply the inequality to V(z)=F(z)-<grad F(y),z> at x and\nw=-grad V(x). The minimum of V is at y, so\n\n    F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>\n       >=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.                  (27)\n\nAdding the exchanged inequality and using Cauchy--Schwarz gives the\nclaimed Lipschitz bound. The function is nonnegative and F(0)=0 by\nthe zero regions and ell'_i>=0.\n\nAt the start of block i its frozen coordinates are\nell'_j+D'_j U'_j for j<i, its current coordinate is ell'_i+D'_i,\nand its later coordinates are0. Along the local physical path,\nx_i>=ell'_i+D'_i U'_i>=ell'_i, and\n0<=x_(i+1)<=D'_i Y'_i=ell'_(i+1) before the checkpoint. Every old\nlocal objective is therefore in its post-checkpoint zero region, and\nevery future one is in its pre-activation zero region. Each has zero\ngradient there because it is differentiable and nonnegative. Only the\ncurrent term has a nonzero gradient. Its coefficient1/2 converts the\nactual physical step h_t to the prescribed local step h_t/2. The\ncheckpoint leaves current coordinate ell'_i+D'_i U'_i and next\ncoordinate ell'_(i+1)+D'_(i+1). This proves the actual GD trajectory\nby induction, using no bound on a horizontal PROXIMAL coordinate.\n\nIn the terminal block the physical gradient is delta'/2. The final\ncoordinate above ell'_k is D'_k-delta' s_k/2>=delta'. Substitution in\nthe terminal term gives\n\n    F(x_n)=(D'_k)^2/[4(1+s_k)].                        (28)\n\nThe initial point is e_0 and 0 is an actual minimizer with distance1.\nSince D'_k=product_i G_i, (28) proves the exact payoff (8) about0.\nAt k=0 simply use Hub_(1/(1+s_0))/2 from initial1; (28) holds with\nD'_0=1. For every ordered pair of actual samples, including optimum\nand terminal points, (27) with x=x_b and y=x_a is exactly Q_ab<=0.\nThus the second realization also retains every original directed row.\n\nHere is an exact radius formula for (26); it does not replace the\nsimple main radius (5). Write the two coordinates of z'_circ,i as\nc_(i,1) and c_(i,2). The zero set of phi'_i is exactly\n\n    z_1<=c_(i,1),\n    epsilon(z_1-c_(i,1))-(z_2-c_(i,2))<=0. They are sufficient because every\nother (p,-q) equals a nonnegative combination of (c_sp,0) and\n(epsilon,-1): its coefficients are (p-epsilon q)/c_sp and q.\nThe envelope has the same zero set. Since (26) is a sum of nonnegative\nterms, its full minimum set is the following EXPLICIT polyhedron:\n\n    C_rad={x: x_i<=zeta_i,\n               epsilon x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon zeta_i-D'_i c_(i,2), i<k;\n               x_k<=ell'_k},\n    zeta_i=ell'_i+D'_i c_(i,1).                        (30)\n\nIt contains0. Let A_rad be the (2k+1)-by-(k+1) matrix whose first\ntwo rows for index i are e_i^T and (epsilon e_i-e_(i+1))^T, with\nright sides zeta_i and epsilon zeta_i-D'_i c_(i,2); the final row\nis e_k^T with right side ell'_k. Let t_rad be this right-side column.\nFor a subset S of linearly independent rows, write\n\n    A_S=(A_rad)_S, r_S=A_S e_0-(t_rad)_S,\n    K_S=A_S A_S^T, lambda_S=K_S^(-1)r_S.\n\nInclude the empty subset with value0. Then the EXACT squared radius is\n\n    rho_source^2=dist(e_0,C_rad)^2\n       =max_{S independent, lambda_S>=0} r_S^T K_S^(-1)r_S. (31)\n\nThis is a finite algebraic expression determined by the given blocks.\nIt is not an executed active-set search or a numerical optimization.\n\nFor completeness, here is a proof of (31). For any feasible x and\nany lambda>=0 on S, set w=A_S^T lambda. Then\n<w,e_0-x>>=lambda^T r_S, and completing a square gives\n||e_0-x||^2>=2lambda^T r_S-||w||^2. At lambda=lambda_S the right\nside equals r_S^T K_S^(-1)r_S. This proves the lower bound for every\nentry of the displayed maximum. Let x_* be the projection onto the\nnonempty closed polyhedron C_rad, and w_*=e_0-x_*. For every direction\nd with nonpositive dot product with every active row, x_*+td is\nfeasible for sufficiently small t>0; thus <w_*,d><=0. A finitely generated cone is\nclosed: from any nonnegative representation one can successively\nremove a linear dependence while keeping coefficients nonnegative,\nuntil only independent generators remain; it is consequently a\nfinite union of closed images of orthants under injective linear\nmaps. If w_* were outside this cone, subtracting its projection on\nthe cone would give a direction d with nonpositive dot product with\nevery generator but <w_*,d>>0, a contradiction. Write\nn=2^m-1 for the physical length, b_j=1+a*c^j for the level-j step,\nand S_j=sum H_j. Thus tau=1 is the original diagnostic word; every\nother tau defines a new word, with no transfer of its old witnesses.\n\nFor clarity, the benchmark is exactly\n\n C_0=B_0=1,\n Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n C_n=max_(0<=i<n) Omega(C_i,C_(n-i-1)),\n B_n=max_(0<=i<n) G(C_i,B_(n-i-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nEvery split is retained. Accepted04219554e41d9750 gives\n\n B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m -> Z_1>0,\n lambda^(-j)B_(2^j-1)<=Z_1\n       <=lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(2^j))+3/100)^2.             (1.1)\n\nSection7 below obtains, solely from that fact's existing certificate,\n\n                    59/25 < Z_1 < 237/100.            (1.2)\n\nFor 0<kappa<1 and epsilon>0, define the following scalar quantities:\n\n Theta=arctan(1/epsilon),\n chi=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(kappa*Theta),\n d=1-kappa^2,\n T_sp=(exp(d*Theta/kappa)-1)/d,\n A=epsilon+chi*(1+(1+kappa)*T_sp)/epsilon.                (1.3)\n\nThe frozen geometry is claimed only for\n0<kappa<=epsilon<=1/2. Outside that range, (1.3) remains a definition\nof numbers and supplies no geometric theorem here.\n\nFor a legal checkpoint decomposition with ordinary block sums s_i,\nselected steps b_i, and terminal ordinary sum s_tail, define\n\n F5 = product_i [(b_i-1)/(chi*s_i+max(epsilon*b_i,A))]^2\n                         /(1+2*s_tail),\n F5a= product_i [b_i/(chi*s_i+2*A+epsilon*b_i)]^2\n                         /[2*(1+s_tail)].             (1.4)\n\nThese are exactly frozen(5) and frozen(5a) with the minimal stated\na_sp=2A/chi. Empty selections mean F5=1/(1+2*S_m) and\nF5a=1/[2*(1+S_m)]. We do not replace either terminal factor.\n\nThere is ONE checkpoint-selection rule throughout this document:\n\n R_m=max{A/epsilon, 1+2/epsilon^2},\n J_m=max{1,ceil(log(R_m/a)/log c)}.                    (1.5)\n\nIf J_m>=m, take the empty selection. Otherwise take ALL first-occurrence\nrecords at levels j=J_m,J_m+1,...,m-1. No optimality among checkpoint\nsets is asserted. The ceiling and exceptional prefix are part of the\nrule, not adjustable errors.\n\nWe analyze exactly TWO scaling regimes:\n\n I. kappa=K/m, epsilon=E/m, with fixed 0<K<=E.\n    For sufficiently large m these are in the frozen valid domain.\n\n II. The same scaling with fixed 0<E<K, interpreted only as a scalar\n     calculation and a CONDITIONAL target for a future geometric theorem.\n     Section9 states an abstract cost/radius interface for that theorem.\n\nAll asymptotic claims keep tau,K,E fixed as m increases. In either\nregime restrict initially to integers m>max{tau,2K,2E,1}; the exact\nfinite product identities also hold at any other depth at which the\ndisplayed definitions and stated parameter domain apply.\n\nDefine constants\n\n nu=pi/(2*log lambda), gamma=4+2*sqrt(2), q_rc=2*gamma,\n theta=nu/K.\n\nThe joint scalar conclusion is\n\n If K<=nu:\n   lim B_n F5 = lim B_n F5a = (Z_1/2)*exp(-tau).       (1.6)\n\n If K>nu:\n   lim B_n F5 = lim B_n F5a\n     =Z_1/(2*lambda)*exp[-tau\n           +(q_rc*tau-pi*K-2*E)*(1-nu/K)].              (1.7)\n\nIn the first case the selection is eventually empty. In the second,\nk=m-J_m is the exact checkpoint count and k/m->1-theta>0. The\nderivation controls all growing products; in fact the logarithmic\nerror in (1.7) is O(log(m)/m), with constants depending on the fixed\ntau,K,E. Equations(1.6),(1.7) are limits of the chosen SCALAR\ncertificates divided by 1/B_n, not limits or upper bounds for W_n.\n\nSpecific consequences proved below:\n\n * In Regime I, at tau=1, BOTH limits are strictly below1/2 for EVERY\n   fixed admissible K,E. A nonempty selected chain has limit below1/2\n   throughout 0<tau<=1. This is not a statement about all checkpoint\n   patterns, parameters varying beyond these scalings, or actual W_n.\n * In Regime I the explicit choice K=E=2nu has limit\n   Z_1/(2lambda)*exp[lambda^2*tau-(pi+2)*nu].\n   It exceeds5369/4250>1 for every fixed tau>=2.\n * In Regime II the fixed choice K=14/5,E=1/100 has limit strictly\n   above1001/1000 for every fixed tau>=1. Thus a geometry with precisely\n   these effective costs and a sufficiently small total additional\n   radius/transfer loss would reach the benchmark on this family.\n   The current frozen construction does NOT supply that geometry.\n\n## 2. Exact masses and every physical index\n\nLet\n\n zeta=a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)], v=c(c-2).\n\nThen 0<zeta<1, because with x=tau/m in(0,1),\n\n c(c-2)=1+gamma*x+lambda^2*x^2>1>beta.\n\nThe recurrence S_(j+1)=2S_j+1+a*c^j, S_0=0, gives exactly\n\n S_j=zeta*c^j+(1-zeta)*2^j-1, 0<=j<=m.              (2.1)\n\nIndeed the formula is zero at j=0 and its next-step difference is\n1+zeta(c-2)c^j=1+a*c^j. This proves(2.1) by induction.\n\nIndex the physical steps of H_m from0 through n-1. The FIRST occurrence\nof b_j is at t_j=2^j-1, or physical step number2^j in one-based\nnotation. This follows by induction from the concatenation defining\nH_(j+1). The word before t_j is H_j. Between t_j and t_(j+1) is H_j,\nof length2^j-1; after t_(m-1) is H_(m-1).\n\nFor J=J_m<m, the exact decomposition selected by(1.5) consequently is\n\n prefix H_J, checkpoint b_J,\n gap H_J, checkpoint b_(J+1),\n gap H_(J+1), checkpoint b_(J+2), ...,\n gap H_(m-2), checkpoint b_(m-1), tail H_(m-1).        (2.2)\n\nEquivalently, checkpoint j=J has incoming sum S_J, every checkpoint\nj>J has incoming sum S_(j-1), and the tail sum is S_(m-1).\nThere are k=m-J checkpoints. The ordinary lengths add to n-k;\nadding those k original steps gives exactly2^m-1. All unselected\nrecords and all their physical copies remain ordinary steps.\n\nThe ceiling in(1.5) gives a*c^J>=R_m. Since c>1, every selected\ncheckpoint satisfies simultaneously\n\n b_j>=2*(1+epsilon^(-2)),\n epsilon*b_j>=epsilon+A>A.                           (2.3)\n\nThus both original thresholds hold at EVERY selected physical position.\nThe maximum in F5 is exactly epsilon*b_j, not an asymptotic replacement.\n\n## 3. Exact all-depth products, including the exceptional first factor\n\nFor J<m, substituting(2.2),(2.3) into(1.4) gives the exact identities\n\n F5 = 1/(1+2*S_(m-1))\n       *[a*c^J/(chi*S_J+epsilon*(1+a*c^J))]^2\n       *product_(j=J+1)^(m-1)\n          [a*c^j/(chi*S_(j-1)+epsilon*(1+a*c^j))]^2,   (3.1)\n\n F5a=1/[2*(1+S_(m-1))]\n       *[(1+a*c^J)/(chi*S_J+2*A+epsilon*(1+a*c^J))]^2\n       *product_(j=J+1)^(m-1)\n          [(1+a*c^j)/(chi*S_(j-1)+2*A\n                                      +epsilon*(1+a*c^j))]^2. (3.2)\n\nProducts with no factors are1. In particular these formulas include\nJ=m-1, a single checkpoint with equal prefix and terminal sums.\nThe numerator in(3.1) is exactly b_j-1 throughout.\n\nFor useful normalized forms put\n\n delta_l=[(1-zeta)*2^l-1]/(zeta*c^l),\n u_rc_j=1/(a*c^j),\n D_first=chi*(1+delta_J)+epsilon*(c-2)*(1+u_rc_J),\n D_j=chi*(1+delta_(j-1))+epsilon*v*(1+u_rc_j), j>J.\n\nThen(3.1) is exactly\n\n F5=(c-2)^2*v^[2*(k-1)]\n       /[(1+2*S_(m-1))*D_first^2*product_(j>J)D_j^2]. (3.3)\n\nFor(3.2), replace D_first by D_first+2A/(zeta*c^J),\nreplace D_j by D_j+2A/(zeta*c^(j-1)), change the terminal factor\n1+2S to2(1+S), and multiply the numerator by\nproduct_(j=J)^(m-1)(1+u_rc_j)^2. These are exact identities.\n\nThere is only ONE factor c-2 in the unsquared numerator; the later\nfactors are v=c(c-2). Treating the first incoming block as H_(J-1)\nwould falsely multiply the eventual answer by lambda^2.\n\n## 4. The activation phase, including its logarithmic correction\n\nThe allowance in(1.3) has a convenient exact identity:\n\n A=epsilon + sqrt(1+epsilon^2)/[epsilon*(1-kappa)]\n                *[exp(Theta/kappa)-kappa*exp(kappa*Theta)].   (4.1)\n\nTo verify it, 1+(1+kappa)T_sp=(exp(dTheta/kappa)-kappa)/(1-kappa)\nand chi*exp(dTheta/kappa)=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)exp(Theta/kappa).\nThis calculation uses only the definitions of numbers.\n\nWith kappa=K/m and epsilon=E/m, the integral formula for arctan gives\nTheta=pi/2-E/m+O(m^(-3)). Hence(4.1) gives\n\n log A=pi*m/(2K)+log m-log E-E/K+O(1/m),\n log(A/epsilon)=pi*m/(2K)+2log m-2log E-E/K+O(1/m).     (4.2)\n\nFor example, factor exp(Theta/kappa) out of the square bracket in\n(4.1). Its relative correction is exponentially small in m, uniformly\nfor these fixed K,E; the remaining factors have ordinary Taylor\nremainders O(1/m). The positive exponential term also dominates the\nseparate additive epsilon. This justifies taking the logarithm.\n\nThe other term1+2/epsilon^2 in R_m grows polynomially, whereas\nA/epsilon has positive exponential rate. Therefore it is the latter\nthat equals R_m for all sufficiently large m. Since\nlog c=log lambda+tau/m+O(m^(-2)) and a->1/lambda, the ceiling gives\n\n J_m=(nu/K)*m+[2/log lambda]*log m+O(1).              (4.3)\n\nThe bound O(1) includes rounding and all fixed tau,K,E contributions.\nIn particular:\n\n * K<nu gives J_m>m eventually;\n * K=nu ALSO gives J_m>m eventually, due to the positive log m term;\n * K>nu gives 1<=J_m<m eventually and k/m->1-nu/K.\n\nThus the borderline cannot be declared an active chain by dropping\nthe polynomial prefactor of A. The activation allowance is fully\npaid in the selected phase theta=nu/K, even though individual\nactivation corrections later disappear from the product.\n\n## 5. Uniform accumulated errors in the nonempty case\n\nSuppose K>nu. The constants in this section may depend on the fixed\ntau,K,E but not on m or the selected index. In particular c stays in\na compact subset of(2,infinity), a,zeta stay bounded away from0,\n1-zeta=O(1/m), and J grows linearly with m.\n\nFor every l>=J the exact mass error obeys\n\n |delta_l| <= [(1-zeta)/zeta]*(2/c)^l+(1/zeta)*c^(-l).\n\nConsequently\n\n sum_(l>=J)|delta_l|\n <= [(1-zeta)/zeta]*(2/c)^J/(1-2/c)\n       +(1/zeta)*c^(-J)/(1-1/c),\n sum_(j>=J)u_rc_j=c^(-J)/[a*(1-1/c)].                  (5.1)\n\nThese are bounds on the WHOLE growing index set, by extending it to\nan infinite geometric series with nonnegative terms. They tend to0\nexponentially. There is no multiplication of an uncontrolled\npointwise o(1) by the checkpoint count.\n\nSet d_first=chi+epsilon*(c-2), d_bulk=chi+epsilon*v. Both are at least\nchi>=1. The relative difference D_first/d_first-1 is bounded in\nabsolute value by |delta_J|+epsilon*(c-2)*u_rc_J; the corresponding\nbulk difference is bounded by |delta_(j-1)|+epsilon*v*u_rc_j.\nThe sum of all these bounds tends to0 by(5.1), with delta_J counted\ntwice when needed. They are eventually at most1/2 individually.\nThe integral identity for log implies |log(1+t)|<=2|t| for |t|<=1/2.\nIt follows that the total error in replacing their logarithms by\nlog d_first and log d_bulk tends to0, with the explicit control(5.1).\n\nIt remains to pay the different numerator and ADDITIVE activation\nin F5a. Divide(3.2) by(3.1), and for its exact incoming sums s_j put\nV_j=2A/(chi*s_j+epsilon*b_j). Then\n\n F5a/F5=(1+2*S_(m-1))/[2*(1+S_(m-1))]\n              *product_(j=J)^(m-1)[(1+u_rc_j)/(1+V_j)]^2. (5.2)\n\nFor all sufficiently large m, S_l>=zeta*c^l/2 for l>=J by(5.1).\nBecause A*c^(-J)<=epsilon*a from the EXACT cutoff,\n\n sum_j V_j\n <=[4A/(zeta*chi)]*c^(-J)*[1+1/(1-1/c)]\n <=[4epsilon*(c-2)/chi]*[1+1/(1-1/c)].                (5.3)\n\nThe first term in the bracket pays the exceptional prefix, and the\ngeometric sum pays every later block, including the repeated H_J.\nUsing log(1+t)<=t for t>=0 in(5.2) yields\n\n |log(F5a/F5)|\n <=1/(1+2*S_(m-1)) + 2*c^(-J)/[a*(1-1/c)]\n       +[8epsilon*(c-2)/chi]*[1+1/(1-1/c)] ->0.      (5.4)\n\nThis is O(1/m) apart from exponentially small terms. The maximum\nactivation in F5 is inactive EXACTLY by(2.3); the separate activation\nin F5a has been summed explicitly. Neither assertion is made for\nearlier physical records excluded by(1.5).\n\n## 6. Leading constants and the benchmark limit\n\nThe elementary expansions needed for the growing product are\n\n log v=gamma*tau/m+O(m^(-2)),\n log chi=pi*K/(2m)+O(m^(-2)),\n log(chi+epsilon*v)=(pi*K/2+E)/m+O(m^(-2)).           (6.1)\n\nThe remainders are uniform on the finite selected index set because\nthe quantities in(6.1) depend only on m, not the checkpoint index.\nMultiplying an O(m^(-2)) remainder by at most m factors gives O(1/m).\nCombining(3.3),(5.1),(6.1),\n\n F5=(c-2)^2/[ (1+2*S_(m-1))*d_first^2 ]\n           *[v/(chi+epsilon*v)]^[2*(k-1)]*exp(o(1)).   (6.2)\n\nNow d_first->1, (c-2)^2->lambda^(-2), and(2.1) gives\n\n lambda^m/(1+2*S_(m-1)) -> lambda*exp(-tau)/2.        (6.3)\n\nFor(6.3), divide S_(m-1) by zeta*c^(m-1); its error tends to0 by\n(5.1), zeta->1, and (lambda/c)^m=(1+tau/m)^(-m)->exp(-tau).\nThe product exponent in(6.2) tends to\n\n              (q_rc*tau-pi*K-2E)*(1-nu/K).             (6.4)\n\nEquations(1.1),(6.2)--(6.4) prove(1.7) for F5; (5.4) proves the\nsame limit for F5a. The factor Z_1/(2lambda) includes BOTH the\nexceptional first record and the terminal H_(m-1). Neither can be\nremoved. The beta parameter cancels from each large-scale transfer\nbecause a/zeta=c-2; it has not been approximated independently in\nm different factors.\n\nFor an explicit convergence description, (4.3) changes the product\nlogarithm by O(log(m)/m), (6.1) contributes O(1/m), and(5.1),(5.4)\ngive the displayed summable errors. Accepted04219554e41d9750 also gives\n\n 0<=Z_1-lambda^(-m)B_(2^m-1)\n <=8sqrt(2)*2^(-m)+(3/25)*2^(rho/2)*lambda^(-m/2)\n                              +(9/10000)*lambda^(-m),\n rho=log_2(lambda).\n\nThus the claimed O(log(m)/m) logarithmic convergence follows; Z_1\nis bounded away from0. No optimization-limit interchange is used.\n\nFor K<=nu the selection is eventually empty by(4.3). Equation(2.1)\ninstead gives lambda^m/(1+2*S_m)->exp(-tau)/2, and the ratio between\nthe two empty-selection terminal factors tends1. The tangent addition formula shows\n4 arctan(1/5)-arctan(1/239)=pi/4: the tangent is1 and the angle is\nin(0,pi/2). For example tan(4 arctan(1/5))=120/119 and subtraction\nof1/239 gives tangent1. We will also use exp(1)>8/3, obtained by retaining\none additional positive term in its power series.\n\n## 8. Two regimes, exact success conditions, and no global optimality claim\n\n### 8A. Regime I: the present parameter restriction\n\nWrite p_rc=pi+2 and b_rc=p_rc*nu. For K>nu and E>=K, the exponent increment\nin(1.7) obeys\n\n (q_rc*tau-pi*K-2E)*(1-nu/K)\n       <=(q_rc*tau-p_rc*K)*(1-nu/K)\n       <=[(sqrt(q_rc*tau)-sqrt(b_rc))_+]^2,                (8.1)\n\nwhere x_+=max(x,0). Regime II: a conditional decoupled target with an exact margin\n\nFreeze, without numerical tuning,\n\n K=14/5, E=1/100,\n theta=5pi/(28log lambda), P_rc=14pi/5+1/50.\n\nThese satisfy E<K and therefore are OUTSIDE the frozen geometric\ndomain. Hence\n\n -1+(q_rc-P_rc)*(1-theta)\n >-1+(23941/4950)*(123/343)\n =1246893/1697850>73/100. (8.11)\n\nFinally q_rc*(1-theta)-1>13*(123/343)-1=1256/343>0.\nThe logarithm in(8.9) is therefore strictly increasing with tau.\nEquation(8.11) holds for every fixed tau>=1. For all tau>0 the exact\ncrossing is\n\n tau_dec=[P_rc*(1-theta)+log(2lambda/Z_1)]\n                               /[q_rc*(1-theta)-1],\n                         0<tau_dec<1.                (8.12)\n\nThis is a definite positive scalar diagnostic, NOT a proof that\nW_n is bounded below by it in Regime II. In particular the constraint\nepsilon>=kappa has not silently been erased from the frozen lemma.\n\n## 9. (9.2)\n\nIf necessary one may include1 in the maximum without changing any\nasymptotic assertion. Here a,c are still the exact enclosing-word\nparameters, not geometric costs. The bounds(9.1) imply\nJ_m/m->theta=alpha/log lambda in(0,1), and every selected physical\nstep meets b_j>=T_m and epsilon_m*b_j>A_m. (9.3)\n\n      Thus any norm, gluing, or overlap losses must be included in\n      the EFFECTIVE chi_m,epsilon_m,A_m or the SUMMED eta losses.\n      Separate local trajectories without their global realization\n      do not satisfy this requirement.\n\n (G3) An actual terminal value bound with the original smoothness L,\n\n      2*(f_m(x_n)-min f_m)>=L*D_rc_k^2/(1+2*S_(m-1)).    (9.4)\n\n (G4) The TRUE squared initial distance satisfies\n\n      dist(x_0,argmin f_m)^2<=r_rc_m*D_rc_0^2,\n                       r_rc_m->r_rc in(0,infinity).         (9.5)\n\n      A declared unit input coordinate alone does not establish(9.5).\n      An exhibited minimizer with this distance bound suffices;\n      otherwise the actual closest-minimizer argument is required.\n\nThese hypotheses are enough: multiply the ACTUAL inequalities(9.3),\nuse(9.4),(9.5), and normalize by L and the true radius. Then\n\n W_n(H_(m,tau)) >= F5/[r_rc_m*product_i eta_(m,i)^2]. A function with\nLipschitz gradient at most L has the required L-smoothness. If the\nconstruction changes its smoothness normalization, that change must\nalready be reflected in (G1)--(G3), not dropped after this step.\n\nThe proof of Sections2--6 applies to these costs as well. (9.10)\n\nIf this scalar expression is below the right side, this PARTICULAR\nguarantee is insufficient; it does not upper-bound the constructed\nfunction's actual error and does not prove that the geometry cannot\ndo better. Complete scalar definition and result\n\nFor 0<kappa<=epsilon<=1/2 set\n\n    Theta=arctan(1/epsilon),\n    C=c_sp=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(kappa*Theta),\n    d_sp=1-kappa^2,\n    T_sp=(exp(d_sp*Theta/kappa)-1)/d_sp,\n    H_sp=C*(1+(1+kappa)*T_sp)-1,\n    A=A_sp=epsilon+(H_sp+1)/epsilon.\n\nHere C and A are local abbreviations for the source's explicit constants;\nC is not the benchmark sequence C_n. The source is frozen finding\ncdd7f057606aeb18 at\n/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/shared_evidence/cdd7f057606aeb18.md,\nSHA256366cd6a12c8e4f56cf3c875c104f3e9bfdc185bf26c955e4f7e83d5bef7b607c.\nThat hash was read back and matched. No extension of the parameter range\nor change in the constant formula is made here.\n\nFor a finite nonnegative physical word h, a selected subset is written in\nits actual chronological order as\n\n    h=(block_0,b_0,block_1,b_1,...,block_(k-1),b_(k-1),block_k).\n\nThe ordinary sums s_i are sums of exactly those intervening unselected\nsteps. Each selected checkpoint must satisfy b_i>=1+epsilon^(-2).\nDefine Q(b)=max{epsilon*b,A} and\n\n    Phi(h;epsilon,kappa,selection)\n      =1/(1+2*s_k)*product_(i=0)^(k-1)\n          [(b_i-1)/(C*s_i+Q(b_i))]^2.                 (1)\n\nEmpty blocks are allowed. For k=0 this is exactly1/(1+2*sum h).\nLet L_sp(h) be the supremum of(1) over all these subsets and all\n0<kappa<=epsilon<=1/2, including k=0. No geometry is required to define it.\n\nPut lambda=1+sqrt(2). For a fixed real tau>0 and an integer m>tau set\n\n    beta=1-tau/m, c=lambda*(1+tau/m), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.       (2)\n\nEvery child in(2) keeps the SAME enclosing(m,tau). Write H_(m,tau)=H_m,\nn_m=2^m-1, and S_j=sum H_j. These are exactly the words specified in\nTASK.md. Only tau=1 is the original diagnostic word; the values used below\nare new explicitly specified words in the permitted family.\n\nCLAIM. For EVERY real0<tau<=1/4 and EVERY integer m>=1536,\n\n    L_sp(H_(m,tau))=1/(1+2*S_m).                      (3)\n\nMore strongly, at EACH individual allowed parameter pair, every eligible\nselection is bounded above by the right side of(3). Thus the full parameter\nsupremum, including parameters depending arbitrarily on the whole word and\nhorizon, is controlled by a finite statement, not an interchange of a limit\nand a supremum.\n\nRetain the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark\n\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4*x*y))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2*x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8*x*y)/2,\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)).\n\nAll split indices are integers, and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. Accepted\nfact04219554e41d9750 establishes the positive finite ray limit\n\n    B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m -> Z_1.\n\nFor every fixed0<tau<=1/4, the resulting exact comparison is\n\n    lim_(m->infinity) B_(n_m)*L_sp(H_(m,tau))\n                   =Z_1/(2*exp(tau)).               (4)\n\nIn particular the single explicit choice tau=1/4 gives\n\n    lim B_(n_m)*L_sp(H_(m,1/4))<237/250<1.            (5)\n\nEvery fixed tau in[1/5,1/4] also gives a limit below79/80. These strict\nconstants use only the already certified benchmark enclosure, restated\nand checked from its saved endpoints in Section7. No new benchmark value,\ndynamic program, numerical experiment, parameter scan, solver or\nmathematical program is evaluated.\n\n## 2. Exact sums and the complete children next to a selected minimum\n\nFor the fixed enclosing(m,tau), let\n\n    v=a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)],\n    M_j=1+S_j,\n    d_j=a*c^j,\n    b_j=1+d_j,\n    u_j=d_j/M_j,\n    M_max=M_(m-1), r=c(c-2).\n\nThe subscript j here is TREE LEVEL, not the physical time of a particular\noccurrence. Every occurrence of b_j has an immediate complete H_j child\non each side. The physical length of H_j is2^j-1.\n\nDirect induction in(2), starting from M_0=1, gives\n\n    M_j=v*c^j+(1-v)*2^j.                             (6)\n\nIndeed the sum recurrence is S_(j+1)=2*S_j+1+a*c^j, and therefore\nM_(j+1)=2*M_j+a*c^j. Substituting(6) and using a=v(c-2) proves the next\ncase. This is a symbolic identity, not an executed recurrence.\n\nWith x=tau/m>0 and gamma=4+2*sqrt(2),\n\n    r=1+gamma*x+lambda^2*x^2>1>beta>0.\n\nThus0<v<1 and c>lambda>2. Formula(6) yields\n\n    1<=M_j<=c^j,\n    d_j/M_j<=a/v=c-2,\n    M_j<=M_max for0<=j<m.                            (7)\n\nThe last inequality also follows directly from the positive sum recurrence.\n\nNow take ANY nonempty selected subset and choose one of its checkpoints\nwith globally minimum tree level j. Neither adjacent H_j child contains\na selected checkpoint, since every step in those children has level<j.\nConsequently the ordinary sums s and t immediately before and after this\nchosen checkpoint satisfy\n\n    s>=S_j, t>=S_j.                                 (8)\n\nIf it is the first selected checkpoint, s means the actual initial block;\nif it is the last, t means the actual terminal block. Both still contain\nthe complete adjacent child. Tied minimum levels cause no difficulty:\ntheir complete children contain strictly smaller levels. This observation\nuses no assumption about records, greedy selection, independence, or an\noptimal split of a different recurrence.\n\n## 3. An exact deletion lemma, retaining the next checkpoint cost\n\nHere suppose2<c<=5/2, and suppose C>=1,A>=C are fixed for the whole word.\nKeep Q(b)=max{epsilon*b,A}; for this lemma epsilon may be any nonnegative\nnumber. The proof does not change any remaining checkpoint or its parameter.\n\nFor a selected minimum-level checkpoint b=b_j=1+d, put\n\n    M=M_j, S=M-1, u=d/M.\n\nBy(7),0<u<=c-2<=1/2. The costs Q(b), and Q of any next checkpoint,\nare at least A.\n\nFirst suppose there IS a next selected checkpoint b', with R=Q(b').\nAll earlier and later factors in(1) cancel when b is deleted. The exact\nlocal comparison of the positive, unsquared factors is\n\n       d/(C*s+Q(b)) * (b'-1)/(C*t+R)\n    <= (b'-1)/(C*(s+b+t)+R)                          (9)\n\nif and only if\n\n    d*[C*(s+b+t)+R] <= (C*s+Q(b))*(C*t+R).           (10)\n\nThis pays for the entire deleted physical step b, including its added1.\nIt also keeps the ACTUAL next checkpoint cost R, even if that step is huge.\n\nTo prove(10), subtract its left side from its right side. The difference\nis increasing in Q(b). Its derivative with respect to R is C*s+Q(b)-d.\nIts derivatives with respect to s and t are respectively\nC*(C*t+R-d) and C*(C*s+Q(b)-d). All are positive on the required region:\nby(8), C*s+Q(b)>=C*(M-1)+A>=C*M>=M>=2*d, and the same holds for C*t+R.\nTherefore the smallest difference occurs at\n\n    s=t=S, Q(b)=R=A.\n\nDefine z=(A-C)/M>=0. At these values the difference, divided by M^2, is\n\n    E(C,u,z)=(C+z)^2-u*[C*(2+u)+z]\n            =C^2-C*u*(2+u)+z*(2*C-u)+z^2.            (11)\n\nThis proves that either of the following WHOLE-WORD conditions suffices\nfor deleting every selected minimum-level interior checkpoint:\n\n    (I)  C>=c(c-2);\n    (II) A>=2*C*M_max.                               (12)\n\nFor(I), u*(2+u)<=c(c-2)<=C; the first two terms of(11) have nonnegative\nsum, and the other terms are nonnegative because2*C-u>0.\n\nFor(II), M<=M_max and M_max>=1 imply\n\n    z=(A-C)/M>=C*(2*M_max-1)/M>=C.\n\nUsing u<=1/2, C>=1, z>=C, and2*C-u>0 in(11) gives\n\n    E(C,u,z)>=C^2-(5/4)*C+C*(2*C-1/2)+C^2\n              =4*C^2-(7/4)*C>0.                     (13)\n\nThus(9) holds in either case. Squaring preserves the comparison, all\nunchanged factors are nonnegative, and deleting b cannot lower Phi.\n\n## 4. The last selected checkpoint has a different exact comparison\n\nIf the minimum-level checkpoint b is LAST, deletion changes the terminal\nHuber denominator. Its exact comparison is\n\n       d^2/[(C*s+Q(b))^2*(1+2*t)]\n    <= 1/[1+2*(s+b+t)].                             (14)\n\nSince C>=1 and A>=C, C*s+Q(b)>=s+1. It suffices to bound\n\n    d^2*[1+2*(s+b+t)]/[(s+1)^2*(1+2*t)]<=1.          (15)\n\nFor fixed s>=0, the fraction without d^2 equals\n\n    [1+2*(s+b)/(1+2*t)]/(s+1)^2,\n\nso it is decreasing in t. Its derivative with respect to s, with the\npositive constant1+2*t suppressed, is\n\n    -2*(s+2*b+2*t)/(s+1)^3<0.\n\nIt is therefore largest at s=t=S=M-1, by(8). At that endpoint(15) has\nleft side\n\n    u^2*[(4+2*u)*M-1]/(2*M-1)\n      <=u^2*(3+2*u)<=1,                             (16)\n\nbecause M>=1 and0<u<=1/2. For the first inequality the difference after\nmultiplying by2*M-1 is2*(1+u)*(M-1)>=0. For the second, u^2*(3+2*u)\nis increasing on[0,1/2] and equals1 at1/2.\n\nUnlike the interior case, this needs neither condition(I) nor(II).\nIt handles the sole selected checkpoint and the final stage of pruning.\n\nUnder either condition in(12), repeatedly choose a currently selected\nminimum level and apply Section3 or Section4. The number of selections\nstrictly decreases, so after finitely many TEXTUAL deletion steps the\nselection is empty and its payoff has not decreased. This is an argument\nfor an arbitrary finite subset, not an executed enumeration or algorithm.\nIn particular, for EACH fixed parameter pair satisfying(12),\n\n    Phi<=1/(1+2*S_m).                               (17)\n\nNo eligibility is lost by deletion: all remaining checkpoints and both\nparameters stay unchanged. Threshold equalities are retained. At level0\nthe children are empty, S_0=0 and M_0=1; all denominators and inequalities\nabove remain defined, with no division by S_j. The target words have no\nzero physical steps, and no argument treats a zero step as positive.\n\n## 5. The frozen spiral constants force the deletion dichotomy uniformly\n\nWe now prove(12) for EVERY allowed epsilon,kappa whenever\n0<tau<=1/4 and m>=1536.\n\nFirst, c<5/2. Indeed sqrt(2)<10/7, so lambda<17/7, and for m>=9,\n\n    c<=lambda*(1+1/(4*m))\n      <(17/7)*(37/36)=629/252<5/2. Arc, exact vertical extremum, and every supporting normal\n\nFor this report only, abbreviate theta_sp by theta_*, c_sp by c,\nR_sp(theta) by R(theta), and the vectors e_sp,t_sp,n_sp by e,t,n.\nThese are scoped aliases, not changes to the project glossary. Set\n\n    theta_*=arctan(1/epsilon),\n    c=sqrt(1+epsilon^2) exp(kappa theta_*),\n    e(theta)=(cos theta,-sin theta),\n    t(theta)=(-sin theta,-cos theta),\n    v(theta)=R(theta)e(theta), R(theta)=c exp(-kappa theta),\n    n(theta)=e(theta)+kappa t(theta),\n    K=conv({0} union {v(theta):0<=theta<=theta_*}).\n\nThus v(0)=(c,0), v(theta_*)=(epsilon,-1), and K is compact and convex.\nWriting v=(p,-q), differentiation gives\n\n    p'=-R(kappa cos theta+sin theta)<0,\n    q'=R(cos theta-kappa sin theta).\n\nConsequently epsilon<=p<=c, q>=0, and q/p=tan theta<=1/epsilon.\nIf kappa<=epsilon then theta_*<=arctan(1/kappa), so q attains its\nmaximum1 at theta_*. Otherwise its sole interior maximum is at\ntheta_c=arctan(1/kappa), and evaluation gives exactly\n\n    qmax_sp=sqrt((1+epsilon^2)/(1+kappa^2))\n              exp(kappa[theta_*-arctan(1/kappa)]).\n\nAt kappa=epsilon this expression equals1, so the piecewise definition\nmatches at equality. In the overshoot case define g(epsilon)=log qmax_sp\nwith kappa fixed. Then g(kappa)=0 and\ng'(epsilon)=(epsilon-kappa)/(1+epsilon^2). Integration gives\n\n    log qmax_sp=integral_epsilon^kappa (kappa-t)/(1+t^2) dt.\n\nIt is strictly positive, bounded below by\n(kappa-epsilon)^2/[2(1+kappa^2)] and above by\n(kappa-epsilon)^2/2. No step later silently replaces qmax_sp by1.\n\nFor delta=phi-theta the normalized support pairing is\n\n    <n(theta),v(phi)>/R(theta)\n      =exp(-kappa delta)(cos delta+kappa sin delta).\n\nIts derivative is -(1+kappa^2)exp(-kappa delta)sin delta. Since\n|delta|<=theta_*<pi/2, the expression increases up to delta0 and\ndecreases thereafter, with maximum1. The origin has strictly smaller\npairing0. Thus n(theta) supports the entire K, not just nearby arc\npoints. At theta0 the vector (0,1) also supports K because q>=0.\n\nThe horizontal component cos theta-kappa sin theta has derivative\n-sin theta-kappa cos theta<0. It becomes negative precisely after\ntheta_c when theta_c<theta_*. The vertical component of n is\n-sin theta-kappa cos theta<0. These statements are valid for all\npositive kappa, even when the arc dips below vertical height-1.\nThey prove the full claimed support geometry without importing the\npublished circular geometry.\n\n## 4. Actual backward projections, including all boundary cases\n\nLet alpha_1,...,alpha_m be the ordered nonnegative ordinary steps and\nr_j=(u_j,-y_j). Start at r_m=v(theta_*)=(epsilon,-1). At an uncapped\nstage retain\n\n    r_j=R(theta_j)[(1+t_j)e(theta_j)+kappa t_j t(theta_j)], t_j>=0,\n    u_j>=epsilon, y_j>=1, y_j/u_j<=1/epsilon.             (A1)\n\nHere the scalar t_j is the normal coefficient, not the vector t(theta).\nThe endpoint starts with t_m=0. Assume the induction at j and put\nalpha=alpha_j.\n\nIf y_j<=kappa[u_j+(alpha-1)c], put mu=u_j+(alpha-1)c. Then\nmu>=y_j/kappa>0, and at r_old=r_j+alpha(c,0),\n\n    r_old-(c,0)=mu n(0)+(0,kappa mu-y_j).\n\nBoth terms belong to supporting normal rays at (c,0). Their sum\ncertifies its Euclidean projection: its inner product with every\nv-(c,0), v in K, is nonpositive. Also\ny_old<=kappa(u_old-c). Increasing u_old by any further alpha c preserves\nthe cap inequality for every alpha>=0. The cap is permanent backward,\nincluding equality in the cap test; there is no unexplained endpoint\nswitch.\n\nOtherwise the current point cannot already be capped. Define\n\n    F_j(theta)=<r_j,e(theta)-kappa^(-1)t(theta)>/R(theta).\n\nEquation(A1) gives F_j(theta_j)=1. The failed cap test gives\nF_j(0)=(u_j-y_j/kappa)/c<1-alpha. Direct differentiation gives\n\n    F_j'(theta)=(kappa+kappa^(-1))\n                  (u_j cos theta+y_j sin theta)/R(theta)>0.\n\nTherefore precisely one theta in (0,theta_j] satisfies F_j(theta)=1-alpha.\nThis is a mathematical existence argument, with no executed root search.\nSince u_j,y_j>0, the clockwise polar angle of r_j lies in (0,pi/2).\nIn(A1) the radial coefficient is positive and the tangent coefficient\nnonnegative, so that polar angle is at least theta_j. It follows that\n<r_j,t(theta)>=0 for the selected theta<=theta_j.\n\nLet w=r_j+(alpha-1)v(theta). The root equation says\n<w,e(theta)>=kappa^(-1)<w,t(theta)>, while\n<w,t(theta)>=0. Hence w is a nonnegative multiple of n(theta).\nThis proves projection of r_old=r_j+alpha v(theta) onto K at v(theta),\nand gives(A1) with the new nonnegative coefficient.\n\nThe coordinate invariants follow by addition of alpha(p,-q): both u,y\nincrease weakly, and y<=u/epsilon is preserved since q<=p/epsilon.\nThe induction is therefore closed for every finite ordinary word.\nIf alpha=0 at an uncapped point, the root is exactly theta_j and the\nstate and gradient repeat. If alpha=0 at a cap, the capped state repeats.\nThe uncapped endpoint can also repeat when trailing ordinary steps are0.\nNo physical entry is removed. Empty ordinary blocks need no induction.\n\nThe vertical assertion that replaces q<=1 is an identity:\n\n    y_j-q_j=R(theta_j)t_j(sin theta_j+kappa cos theta_j)>=0. (A2)\n\nIt holds at the initial endpoint with equality, at every repeated\nuncapped endpoint, and at every interior point. At a cap q_j=0 and\ny_j>=1. This checks all normal cases, not just generic interior ones.\n\nFor the horizontal displacement, if n_1(theta_j)>=0 then(A1) gives\nu_j-p_j>=0. In the negative-normal part, theta_j>theta_c and p decreases,\nso p_j<=p(theta_c)=kappa qmax_sp. Together with u_j>=epsilon this gives\n\n    u_j-p_j>=epsilon-kappa qmax_sp>=-N_sp,\n    N_sp=max(0,kappa qmax_sp-epsilon).                    (A3)\n\nAt a cap u_j-c>=y_j/kappa>0. Thus(A3) covers that case as well. The\nargument proves a sufficient upper bound on a deficit. It does not\nidentify N_sp as the exact largest deficit, and neither the draft nor\nthis audit relies on such an identification.\n\n## 5. Turning bounds, displacements, and thresholds\n\nFor two consecutive uncapped angles theta_old<=theta_new set\nDelta=theta_new-theta_old. Resolving the exact backward update along\nt(theta_old) cancels the added gradient term. It yields\n\n    t_old=B(Delta)+A(Delta)t_new,\n    A(Delta)=exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),\n    B(Delta)=exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta/kappa.\n\nAll coefficients are nonnegative because 0<=Delta<=theta_*<pi/2.\nPut d=1-kappa^2. Direct differentiation gives\n\n    A'-(d/kappa)A=-(1+kappa^(-2))exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta<=0.\n\nSince A(0)=1, A<=exp(d Delta/kappa). Always B<=Delta/kappa.\nFor kappa<1, d>0 and\n1+dB<=1+d Delta/kappa<=exp(d Delta/kappa). Therefore\n\n    1+d t_old<=exp(d Delta/kappa)(1+d t_new).\n\nThe sum of backward angle decrements is at most theta_*, and the\nendpoint coefficient is0. Telescoping proves\n\n    t_j<=[exp(d(theta_*-theta_j)/kappa)-1]/d<=T_sp.\n\nFor kappa>=1, the same differential estimate gives A<=1. Using\nt_old<=Delta/kappa+t_new instead proves\nt_j<=(theta_*-theta_j)/kappa<=theta_*/kappa=T_sp.\nNo division by d at kappa=1 or multiplication of an inequality by a\nnegative d occurs in this branch.\n\nT_sp is positive and finite for each allowed pair. For fixed epsilon,\nlim_(kappa->1-) [exp(d theta_*/kappa)-1]/d=theta_*;\nthe right branch theta_*/kappa has the same limit and value at1.\nContinuity does not require matching derivatives. Also c>1, so\nH_sp=c[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]-1>0 and\nA_sp=epsilon+(H_sp+1)/epsilon>0. All subsequent positive scales have\nstrictly positive finite denominators. No uniform boundedness as\nkappa->0 or epsilon->0 is claimed.\n\nAt an uncapped point,\n\n    y_j=R(theta_j)[(1+t_j)sin theta_j+kappa t_j cos theta_j]\n       <=c[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]=1+H_sp.\n\nThe transition into a cap and every subsequent capped step preserve y.\nThus the bound holds everywhere. Summing the actual updates gives\nr_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q), and\n\n    0<=P<=c S, 0<=Q<=H_sp, P>=epsilon Q,\n\nwhere S=sum_j alpha_j. These are uniform in the finite word length and\nindividual sizes; there is no small-step assumption.\n\nFor the unchanged-threshold rectangle, when kappa<=epsilon we have\nN_sp=0. Otherwise the derivative in epsilon computed in Section3 is\nnegative. Letting epsilon decrease to0 yields\n\n    qmax_sp<=exp(kappa arctan kappa)/sqrt(1+kappa^2).\n\nThe logarithmic derivative of this right side in kappa is arctan kappa.\nFor 0<kappa<=1 it is bounded by exp(pi/4)/sqrt(2)<2. The source's\nelementary numerical-constant proof is valid: pi<4,\ne=1+1+1/2+sum_(j>=3)1/j!<=5/2+(1/6)/(1-1/4)=49/18,\nand (49/18)^2<8 by 2401<2592. These are textual inequalities, not\nexecuted arithmetic. Therefore N_sp<2<=1/epsilon and\n\n    B_sp=1+max(epsilon^(-2),N_sp/epsilon)=1+epsilon^(-2).\n\nThis includes kappa1 and epsilon1/2. For arbitrary kappa>0 the same\nequality follows whenever epsilon N_sp<=1, including equality in that\ncondition. Outside it the displayed larger B_sp supplies exactly the\nhorizontal bound used below. No unproved larger-domain claim is needed.\n\n## 6. Finite local objective and proximal buffers\n\nFix a local block, a checkpoint b>=1+epsilon^(-2), and sigma>0 with\n\n    sigma(P+epsilon b)<=1,\n    sigma[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=1.\n\nSet z_circ=(1,0)-sigma(epsilon+P,-1-Q),\nU=1-sigma(P+epsilon b)>=0, Y=sigma Q, and\n\n    V_fin={0,v(0),v_0,...,v_m},\n    phi(z)=sigma max_(v in V_fin)<v,z-z_circ>.\n\nThe set V_fin is finite; its hull lies in K and contains every v_j.\nEvery projection inequality from Section4 therefore remains true on this\nsmaller hull. At x_j=z_circ+sigma r_j and z_j=x_j-sigma v_j,\n\n    <r_j-v_j,v-v_j><=0 for every v in V_fin.\n\nConsequently the plane with slope sigma v_j is active at z_j and\nsigma v_j belongs to the subdifferential of phi there. The strongly\nconvex proximal objective is uniquely minimized at z_j. This identifies\nthe actual envelope gradient, with all local competing planes retained.\n\nFor every nonzero v=(p,-q) in V_fin,\n\n    <v,-z_circ>\n      =-p+sigma[p(epsilon+P)+q(1+Q)]\n      <=-p+sigma p[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=0.\n\nSince p>0 and q>=0, all planes are nonpositive on z_1<=0,z_2>=0.\nThe zero plane shows phi=0 there. At(U,Y),\n\n    <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>=sigma[(1-b)epsilon p+q]<=0\n\nbecause q<=p/epsilon and b>=1+epsilon^(-2). Increasing the second\ncoordinate can only decrease these plane values. Thus phi(U,y)=0\nfor y>=Y. A nonnegative envelope has exactly the same zero set:\nzero value forces both the nonnegative original value and the quadratic\ndistance at its attained proximal point to vanish. This proves both\nrequired zero regions for phi and its envelope, including equalities.\n\nThe local path starts at(1,0), uses every ordinary alpha_j, and reaches\n(1-sigma P,sigma Q)=(U+sigma epsilon b,Y), with endpoint gradient\nsigma(epsilon,-1). The checkpoint reaches(U,Y+sigma b). Along the\nordinary part, the first physical coordinate decreases and the second\nincreases, so x_1>=U and 0<=x_2<=Y.\n\nAt local proximal points the second coordinate instead satisfies\n\n    (z_j)_2=sigma(1+Q-y_j+q_j).\n\nBackward monotonicity gives 1<=y_j<=1+Q, proving its nonnegative\nlower bound. Identity(A2) proves the upper bound\n\n    0<=(z_j)_2<=sigma(1+Q).                              (A4)\n\nThis is exact despite qmax_sp>1; no extra buffer error is accumulated.\nFor the first proximal coordinate, direct subtraction gives\n\n    (z_j)_1-U=sigma[epsilon(b-1)+u_j-p_j]\n       >=sigma[epsilon(b-1)-N_sp]>=0                    (A5)\n\nwhen b>=B_sp. This is the only place the additional general-kappa\nthreshold is used. The source-format assembly below uses physical\ncoordinates and does not need(A5).\n\n## 7. One global max-affine envelope and every actual row\n\nFor the main realization set\n\n    M_i=P_i+max(epsilon b_i,epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon), sigma_i=1/M_i,\n    D_0=1, ell_0=0,\n    D_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(b_i-1), ell_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i).\n\nAll D_i are positive because b_i>=B_sp>1. Define\n\n    Phi_i(z)=D_i^2 phi_i((z_i-ell_i)/D_i,z_(i+1)/D_i), i<k,\n    delta_tail=D_k/(1+2s_k),\n    Phi_k(z)=delta_tail max(z_k-ell_k,0),\n    J_sp=max_(0<=i<=k)Phi_i, f=env_1 J_sp.\n\nEach Phi_i is a finite affine maximum with a zero plane. Thus J_sp\nis a single finite affine maximum, fixed from the entire supplied word\nbefore GD starts. Its construction does not change along the path.\nIt is nonnegative and vanishes at0 by the pre-activation zero regions.\n\nFor completeness, the envelope properties follow directly. A proximal\npoint z exists uniquely since J_sp is continuous, convex, nonnegative,\nand its addition to ||x-z||^2/2 is coercive and strictly convex. Write\ng=x-z in partial J_sp(z). For another input x', monotonicity of the two\nsubgradients gives\n\n    ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>.\n\nHence g is 1-Lipschitz. The trial point z at x+w gives the upper bound\nf(x+w)<=f(x)+<g,w>+||w||^2/2. Using the actual proximal point z' at\nx+w and the subgradient inequality at z gives the lower bound\n\n    f(x+w)>=f(x)+<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2.\n\nThe two estimates prove differentiability with gradient g; the lower\nsupporting inequality proves convexity. Minimum0 is attained at0.\n\nAt the start of block i the candidate state has frozen coordinates\nx_j=ell_j+D_j U_j for j<i, current coordinate x_i=ell_i+D_i,\nand x_j=0 for j>i. Apply the local path in coordinates i,i+1. At its\ncandidate global proximal point z, subtraction of its proposed gradient\nchanges only those two coordinates. The following checks establish that\nthe local path is the global one:\n\n* The current Phi_i has the proposed scaled local subgradient at z.\n* For Phi_(i-1), when it exists, its first input remains U_(i-1).\n  By(A5), z_i>=ell_i+D_i U_i>=ell_i, so its second input is at least\n  ell_i/D_(i-1)=sigma_(i-1)(1+Q_(i-1))>=Y_(i-1). It is zero.\n* Any Phi_j with j<=i-2 sees both its coordinates frozen. Its first\n  input is U_j and its second is at least ell_(j+1)/D_j>=Y_j.\n  Those earlier pieces remain zero.\n* For Phi_(i+1), when it exists, (A4) gives\n  z_(i+1)<=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i)=ell_(i+1). Its first input is\n  nonpositive and its second is0. It is zero.\n* Every later local piece has first input -ell_j/D_j<=0 and second\n  input0. The terminal hinge is also zero before activation; if it is\n  the immediately next piece, the same(A4) bound applies to it.\n\nThe current Phi_i is nonnegative, so it attains J_sp at this point even\nif its value is0 and several pieces tie. A subgradient of an active\npiece is a subgradient of their maximum: its supporting affine bound\nlies below that piece and hence below J_sp everywhere. Therefore the\ncandidate z satisfies the global proximal condition. Uniqueness proves\nthe claimed gradient is the actual global gradient. No inactive plane\nhas been deleted.\n\nAt the checkpoint the current coordinate freezes at ell_i+D_i U_i.\nThe next is D_i(Y_i+sigma_i b_i)=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1). This proves the\ninduction across all original physical steps, including consecutive\ncheckpoints and empty or all-zero blocks.\n\nDuring the terminal block let u be the sum already executed. It lies in\n[0,s_k], and the moving coordinate is ell_k+D_k-u delta_tail. Subtracting\nthe proposed gradient leaves it at least ell_k because\n\n    D_k-(u+1)delta_tail\n      =D_k(2s_k-u)/(1+2s_k)>=0.\n\nAll previous pieces are zero at this proximal point. The terminal\ngradient is delta_tail e_k even at a boundary tie, since the terminal\nhinge admits that subgradient at0. This proves the actual terminal\ngradient and value, including the unqueried last sample:\n\n    f(x_n)=delta_tail D_k-(s_k+1/2)delta_tail^2\n          =D_k^2/[2(1+2s_k)].\n\nFor every ordered pair of samples a,b, including any minimizer and the\nterminal sample, let z_a=x_a-g_a. The exact envelope identities give\n\n    Q_ab=J_sp(z_a)-J_sp(z_b)+<g_a,z_b-z_a><=0.\n\nIndeed, the norm and gradient cross terms cancel upon substituting\nf_a=J_sp(z_a)+||g_a||^2/2 and x_a=z_a+g_a. The remaining inequality\nis the subgradient inequality at z_a. It proves each orientation, all\nremote rows, both optimum orientations, and all terminal rows. At a\nminimizer g_star=0, J_sp(z_star)=0; repeated samples from zero steps\ncause no exception. The gradients and positions are explicit vectors\nin R^(k+1), so their Gram matrix is PSD on the actual data.\n\nAt k0 use J_sp(z)=delta max(z,0), delta=1/(1+2s_0), from x_0=1.\nThe same terminal argument holds, including an empty physical word.\n\n## 8. Exact main radius and both main payoffs\n\nFor k>=1 define x_star with coordinates\n\n    (x_star)_0=1-sigma_0(epsilon+P_0),\n    (x_star)_1=sigma_0(1+Q_0)=ell_1,\n    (x_star)_j=0 for j>=2.\n\nThe first local component is at its center. The next local component,\nif any, sees(0,0), and all later components are in their zero regions.\nThe terminal hinge is zero, also when k1 and its coordinate equals\nell_1. Hence J_sp(x_star)=0. Nonnegative attained proximal terms imply\nargmin f={J_sp=0}.\n\nBoth endpoint planes of the first local component are active at x_star.\nThe exact vector identity is\n\n    e_0-x_star\n      =[(P_0-epsilon Q_0)/c] sigma_0(c,0,...)\n         +(1+Q_0) sigma_0(epsilon,-1,0,...).\n\nBoth coefficients are nonnegative. Every minimizer z has nonpositive\ninner product with each active endpoint slope after subtracting x_star.\nThus <e_0-x_star,z-x_star><=0. Expansion gives\n\n    ||e_0-z||^2>=||e_0-x_star||^2+||z-x_star||^2.\n\nThis proves uniqueness of the closest minimizer and exactly\n\n    dist(e_0,argmin f)^2\n      =sigma_0^2[(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2]>0.\n\nDividing the terminal value by this radius cancels the first sigma_0^2\nand gives exactly formula(6), with its first numerator(b_0-1)^2 and\nsubsequent factors[(b_i-1)/M_i]^2. This is not merely a chosen-minimizer\nbound.\n\nFor epsilon<=1,\n\n    sqrt((epsilon+P)^2+(1+Q)^2)\n      <=epsilon+P+(1+Q)/epsilon<=M.\n\nAlso M_i<=c s_i+max(epsilon b_i,A_sp). Therefore the exact payoff is\nat least the mass-only expression(7). Equivalently the first radius is\nat most1, as it must be because0 is another actual minimizer. Each\ninequality has the direction needed for a lower payoff bound. At k0\nthe closest minimizer is0 and the exact payoff is1/(1+2s_0).\n\n## 9. Separate source-format function and separate exact radius\n\nUse b_i>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)) and, for each ordinary block, rerun the\nbackward construction with steps divided by2. Thus the P_i,Q_i in this\nsection are half-step displacements; they are not the main construction's\ndisplacements. Put beta_i=b_i/2 and\n\n    sigma'_i=1/[c s_i/2+epsilon b_i/2+A_sp].\n\nThis satisfies both local scale conditions because P_i<=c s_i/2 and\nepsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon<=A_sp. Define local envelopes f_i=env_1 phi'_i\nand their U'_i,Y'_i as in Section6 with beta_i. Set\n\n    G_i=sigma'_i beta_i=b_i/[c(s_i+a_sp)+epsilon b_i], a_sp=2A_sp/c,\n    D'_0=1, ell'_0=0,\n    D'_(i+1)=D'_i G_i, ell'_(i+1)=D'_i Y'_i,\n    delta'=D'_k/(1+s_k).\n\nThe objective is the single fixed function\n\n    F(x)=1/2 sum_(i<k) (D'_i)^2\n           f_i((x_i-ell'_i)/D'_i,x_(i+1)/D'_i)\n         +1/2 Hub_(delta')(x_k-ell'_k).\n\nEach scaled local envelope is convex and 1-smooth on its two\ncoordinates. In the sum of its quadratic upper inequalities, each\ncoordinate occurs at most twice. The outer factor1/2 therefore gives\nF(x+w)<=F(x)+<grad F(x),w>+||w||^2/2. For clarity this bound indeed\nimplies 1-smoothness here: subtract <grad F(y),z> to obtain a convex\nfunction V minimized at y; apply the upper bound at x with\nw=-grad V(x), and compare its value to V(y). This gives\n\n    F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>\n      >=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.                      (A6)\n\nExchanging x,y and adding gives cocoercivity, and Cauchy--Schwarz\ngives a 1-Lipschitz gradient. The function is nonnegative and F(0)=0.\nThus0 is a specified actual minimizer, not an assumed normalization.\n\nAt a physical query in block i, the local first input is at least\nU'_i>=0 and the second lies in[0,Y'_i]. The previous component sees\nfirst input U'_(i-1) and second at least\nell'_i/D'_(i-1)=Y'_(i-1); it is zero. Earlier components have frozen\nzero-region coordinates. The next component has first input<=0 and\nsecond0; all later ones and the unactivated hinge are zero. Every zero\ncomponent has gradient0 because it is differentiable and nonnegative.\nOnly the current half-weighted term contributes to the gradient.\n\nThe chain rule makes a physical step h exactly a local step h/2.\nThe selected physical b_i similarly uses beta_i. Its transition leaves\nnext coordinate ell'_(i+1)+D'_(i+1), establishing the full induction.\nThis argument checks physical points only. It uses neither(A5) nor\nu_j>=p_j, which is why every kappa>0 is allowed at the doubled old\nthreshold. No factor is lost to a presumed proximal coordinate bound.\n\nIn the terminal block the physical gradient is delta'/2. At partial\nsum u<=s_k, the final coordinate above ell'_k is\nD'_k-u delta'/2>=delta'(1+s_k/2)>=delta'. Hence the Huber term stays\nin its affine region through the unqueried last sample. Substitution\ngives F(x_n)=(D'_k)^2/[4(1+s_k)]. Initial point e_0 has distance1\nfrom0, so its exact payoff about this minimizer is\n\n    2F(x_n)=1/[2(1+s_k)] product_i\n                [b_i/(c(a_sp+s_i)+epsilon b_i)]^2.\n\nThis verifies formula(8) with no discarded epsilon b_i term. At k0,\nF=Hub_(1/(1+s_0))/2 from1 gives the same formula. Inequality(A6),\nwith x=x_b,y=x_a, proves every original Q_ab<=0, including all\nminimizer and terminal rows. Dimension and physical length are unchanged\nby the local half-steps. For either realization, multiplying the whole\nobjective by L>0 and using physical steps h_t/L preserves the\ntrajectory and normalized payoff.\n\nIt remains to check the different closest radius. Let c_(i,1),c_(i,2)\nbe the two coordinates of the local center z'_circ,i. Since every slope\n(p,-q) has the decomposition\n\n    (p,-q)=[(p-epsilon q)/c](c,0)+q(epsilon,-1),\n\nwith nonnegative coefficients, the endpoint inequalities alone describe\nthe local zero set:\n\n    z_1<=c_(i,1), epsilon(z_1-c_(i,1))-(z_2-c_(i,2))<=0.\n\nThey are also necessary because both endpoint slopes are actually\nincluded. The local envelope has the same zero set. A sum of the\nnonnegative terms vanishes exactly when each vanishes. Thus the full\nminimum set C_rad is exactly\n\n    x_i<=zeta_i,\n    epsilon x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon zeta_i-D'_i c_(i,2), i<k,\n    x_k<=ell'_k,\n    zeta_i=ell'_i+D'_i c_(i,1).\n\nIt contains0 and is a nonempty closed polyhedron. Let A_rad x<=t_rad\nbe these 2k+1 inequalities, precisely with row vectors e_i and\nepsilon e_i-e_(i+1), followed by e_k. For independent row subsets S\nwrite A_S for that submatrix, r_S=A_S e_0-(t_rad)_S,\nK_S=A_S A_S^T, and lambda_S=K_S^(-1)r_S. Independence makes K_S\npositive definite. The source's finite formula is\n\n    rho_source^2=max_(S independent,lambda_S>=0) r_S^T K_S^(-1)r_S,\n\nincluding the empty subset with value0. No extra feasibility condition\non e_0-A_S^T lambda_S is needed: every entry is a dual lower bound,\nand an optimal independent active subset occurs in the list.\n\nHere is a direct verification. For x in C_rad and any lambda>=0 on S,\nput w=A_S^T lambda. Then <w,e_0-x>>=lambda^T r_S. Completing the\nsquare gives\n\n    ||e_0-x||^2>=2lambda^T r_S-||w||^2.\n\nFor lambda=lambda_S the right side equals the listed entry. Thus\neach listed entry is at most the true squared distance.\n\nConversely take the unique projection x_* of e_0 onto C_rad and set\nw_*=e_0-x_*. If a direction d has nonpositive pairing with all active\nrows, x_*+t d remains feasible for sufficiently small t>0: active\nconstraints do not increase and the finitely many inactive constraints\nhave positive slack. The projection inequality gives <w_*,d><=0.\nIt follows that w_* lies in the cone of active rows. To justify this\nwithout an imported optimization theorem, remove linear dependence from\nany nonnegative generator representation by subtracting a suitable\nmultiple of an oriented dependence, maintaining nonnegativity until a\ncoefficient vanishes. An independent representation results. The cone\nis therefore a finite union of images of closed orthants under injective\nlinear maps, and is closed. If w_* were outside it, its residual after\nprojection onto that closed cone would have nonpositive pairing with\nevery generator but strictly positive pairing with w_*, a contradiction.\nThe residual assertion follows from the cone projection inequality and\northogonality to its projected vector, obtained by testing0 and twice\nthat vector.\n\nChoose an independent active subset representing w_*=A_S^T lambda\nwith lambda>=0. Activity implies r_S=A_S w_*=K_S lambda; hence\nlambda=lambda_S. Its listed entry equals||w_*||^2, proving equality.\nThe empty-subset case covers a zero residual, although the present\ninitial point actually has positive distance. For k>=1 its first\nconstraint would require\n1<=1-sigma'_0(epsilon+P_0), which is false. For k0 the set is the\nnonpositive half-line. Thus 0<rho_source^2<=1, the upper bound using\n0 in C_rad, and the exact closest-distance payoff is(8)/rho_source^2.\nThe main radius from Section8 has not been borrowed.\n\n## 10. Finite equal-angle witness, paid cost, and uniformity\n\nFix positive kappa and 0<epsilon<=1/2. For a positive integer M set\nDelta=theta_*/M, theta_l=theta_*-l Delta, and use the A,B of Section5\nat Delta, denoted A_D,B_D. Define\n\n    t_0=0, t_l=B_D+A_D t_(l-1),\n    r_l=R(theta_l)[(1+t_l)e(theta_l)+kappa t_l t(theta_l)],\n    alpha_l=1-exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta+B_D\n                   +(1+kappa^2)B_D t_(l-1).\n\nEvery alpha_l>=0. Resolving r_(l-1) in the basis at theta_l makes\nits tangent component R(theta_l)kappa t_l; the radial difference from\nr_l is exactly alpha_l R(theta_l). Hence\nr_l-r_(l-1)=alpha_l v(theta_l), with no approximation. The support\nnormal proves projection at every r_l. The actual chronological word\n(alpha_M,...,alpha_1) therefore runs from r_M to r_0=(epsilon,-1).\nAt its last backward state theta_M=0 the cap inequality is equality;\nthis is one of the valid cap cases in Section4. Thus this is an actual\nfinite family in the construction, not a continuous path asserted to\napproximate one.\n\nLet S_M=sum alpha_l, P_M=(r_M)_1-epsilon and\nQ_M=-(r_M)_2-1. At theta_M=0,\n\n    P_M=c(1+t_M)-epsilon, 1+Q_M=c kappa t_M.              (A7)\n\nFor kappa!=1 put d=1-kappa^2 and\n\n    T_eq=[exp(d theta_*/kappa)-1]/d,\n    S_eq=(1+kappa^2)(T_eq-kappa theta_*)/d.\n\nFor kappa1 use T_eq=theta_*, S_eq=2theta_*+theta_*^2.\nThe distinction matters: when kappa>1, T_eq is an exact family limit,\nwhile the sufficient T_sp is theta_*/kappa.
