(14)\n\nInitially a_N=0. In (11) let mu=U_j+(alpha-1)*chi. Then mu>0 and\nr_(j-1)-v(0)=mu*n(0)+(0,kappa*mu-Y_j). Both are nonnegative\nsupporting-normal combinations, proving projection to v(0). The\ninequality becomes Y_(j-1)<=kappa*(U_(j-1)-chi), so all earlier\nbackward entries are capped too.\n\nIf (11) fails, the point is not already capped. The left side of\n(12) is1 at u_j by (14), less than1-alpha at0, and has derivative\n\n    (kappa+kappa^(-1))*(U_j*cos u+Y_j*sin u)/R(u)>0.\n\nThis gives exactly one root, including alpha=0 with root u_j.\nRepresentation (14) shows that the polar angle of r_j is at least\nu_j, so <r_j,t(u)>=0 at that root. Equation (12) says that\nr_j+(alpha-1)*v(u) is a nonnegative multiple of n(u): its t-component\nis kappa times its e-component. This proves (14) for j-1 and the\nprojection property. Adding alpha*(p,-q) preserves (13) by (10).\nFinally, if x-v has nonpositive dot product with every w-v in K_arc,\nthen ||x-w||^2>=||x-v||^2; thus all the supporting-normal arguments\nindeed prove v_j=Pi_(K_arc)(r_j).\n\nDefine\n\n    P=sum_(j=1)^N alpha_j*p(u_(j-1)),\n    Q=sum_(j=1)^N alpha_j*q(u_(j-1)), S=sum_j alpha_j.\n\nThen r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q), 0<=P<=chi*S and P>=epsilon*Q.\nTo bound Q independently of the word, consider two consecutive\nuncapped angles u_old<=u_new and Delta=u_new-u_old. Taking the\nt(u_old) component of r_old=r_new+alpha*v(u_old) gives exactly\n\n    a_old=B_Delta+A_Delta*a_new,\n    A_Delta=exp(-kappa*Delta)*(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),\n    B_Delta=exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin Delta/kappa.          (15)\n\nBoth coefficients are nonnegative. With d=1-kappa^2>0,\n\n    A_Delta'-(d/kappa)*A_Delta\n       =-(1+kappa^(-2))*exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin Delta<=0.\n\nHence A_Delta<=exp(d*Delta/kappa), while B_Delta<=Delta/kappa and\n1+d*B_Delta<=exp(d*Delta/kappa). It follows that\n1+d*a_old<=exp(d*Delta/kappa)*(1+d*a_new). Telescoping from a_N=0\ngives a_j<=T. By (14),\n\n    Y_j=R(u_j)*[(1+a_j)*sin u_j+kappa*a_j*cos u_j]\n       <=chi*[1+(1+kappa)*T]=1+H.\n\nA cap leaves Y unchanged. Consequently\n\n    0<=Q<=H, 0<=P<=chi*S, P>=epsilon*Q.               (16)\n\nThe needed geometry has now been proved without the false restriction\nq<=1, without nonnegative horizontal normals, and without any per-step\nunpriced error. The half-sum assembly below uses physical zero regions;\nit does not require bounds on horizontal proximal coordinates.\n\n## 4. Finite local functions and their zero regions\n\nFor a physical ordinary block of sum s and its physical checkpoint b,\napply Section3 to its entries divided by2. Write N for its length and\nP,Q,r_j,v_j for that HALF-step construction. Set b_loc=b/2 and\n\n    sigma=1/(chi*s/2+epsilon*b/2+A),\n    z_circ=(1,0)-sigma*(epsilon+P,-1-Q),\n    U=1-sigma*(P+epsilon*b_loc), Y=sigma*Q,\n    V_fin={0,v(0),v_0,...,v_N},\n    phi(z)=sigma*max_(v in V_fin)<v,z-z_circ>,\n    f_loc(x)=min_z [phi(z)+||x-z||^2/2].              (17)\n\nBy (16), P<=chi*s/2 and Q<=H. Thus U>=0 and\n\n    sigma*[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=1.               (18)\n\nThe function phi is a finite nonnegative convex maximum of affine\nfunctions. The minimizing objective defining f_loc is coercive and\nstrictly convex, so its proximal point z exists uniquely. If g=x-z,\nthe minimizing condition is g in partial phi(z). To justify both\ndirections directly, this subgradient inequality makes the cost at\nany other w at least its cost at z plus ||w-z||^2/2. Conversely,\noptimality at z, tested against z+t*(w-z) for0<t<1, and convexity of\nphi imply phi(w)-phi(z)>=<g,w-z>-t*||w-z||^2/2. Letting t decrease\nto0 proves the subgradient inequality. For two such pairs,\nthe two subgradient inequalities imply\n||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>. Moreover using z as trial point at x+w gives\n\n    f_loc(x+w)<=f_loc(x)+<g,w>+||w||^2/2.\n\nUsing the actual new proximal point z' and the subgradient inequality\nat z gives\n\n    f_loc(x+w)>=f_loc(x)+<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2.\n\nThese bounds prove differentiability with gradient g, global convexity,\nand a1-Lipschitz gradient. Also f_loc>=0 and {f_loc=0}={phi=0}.\n\nAt x_j=z_circ+sigma*r_j, put z_j=x_j-sigma*v_j. Since the convex hull\nof V_fin is contained in K_arc and includes v_j, its projection\ninequality implies that sigma*v_j is an active subgradient of phi at\nz_j. Thus grad f_loc(x_j)=sigma*v_j. In particular the original\nordered half-steps run exactly from x_0=(1,0) to\n(U+sigma*epsilon*b_loc,Y); the step b_loc then reaches\n(U,Y+sigma*b_loc).\n\nFor every nonzero v=(p,-q) in V_fin, (10) and (18) give\n\n    <v,-z_circ>\n      =-p+sigma*[p*(epsilon+P)+q*(1+Q)]<=0.\n\nTherefore phi=f_loc=0 on {z_1<=0,z_2>=0}. Also b_loc>=1+epsilon^(-2)\nby (9), so\n\n    <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>=sigma*[(1-b_loc)*epsilon*p+q]<=0.\n\nIncreasing the second coordinate decreases this inner product. Hence\n\n    f_loc(U,y)=0 for every y>=Y.                      (19)\n\nAlong the actual local path the first coordinate decreases and the\nsecond increases. Before its checkpoint they obey x_1>=U and\n0<=x_2<=Y. These are physical coordinate statements, valid independently\nof the sign of the supporting normal. Zero steps, if present, retain\ntheir original index and cause no exception to the construction.\n\n## 5. One finite-dimensional static function and every physical update\n\nFirst suppose J<m. Apply Section4 separately in advance to each actual\nincoming block Aword_i and its checkpoint b^(i), i=0,...,k-1. All its symbols receive\nthe subscript i: sigma_i,P_i,Q_i,phi_i,f_i,U_i,Y_i,z_circ,i.\nDefine positive scales and nonnegative offsets by\n\n    D_0=1, ell_0=0,\n    G_i=sigma_i*b^(i)/2\n       =b^(i)/(chi*s_i+2*A+epsilon*b^(i)),\n    D_(i+1)=D_i*G_i,\n    ell_(i+1)=D_i*Y_i,\n    delta_tail=D_k/(1+s_k).                           (20)\n\nThe basis e_0,...,e_k is the standard orthonormal basis of R^(k+1).\nFor any delta>0 define the one-sided Huber function\n\n    Hub_delta(z)=0                     if z<=0,\n                 z^2/2                 if0<=z<=delta,\n                 delta*z-delta^2/2      if z>=delta.\n\nIts continuous derivative is max{0,min{z,delta}}, which is nondecreasing\nand1-Lipschitz. The ACTUAL function used in the theorem is\n\n    F_m(x)=(1/2)*sum_(i=0)^(k-1) D_i^2\n                    *f_i((x_i-ell_i)/D_i,x_(i+1)/D_i)\n           +(1/2)*Hub_(delta_tail)(x_k-ell_k).         (21)\n\nThis is a single function fixed before running GD. The finite lists\nof planes, implicit backward roots, scales and shifts are mathematical\ndefinitions determined by the entire prescribed word. No trajectory\nis replayed or numerically solved to define (21). Its dimension is\nexactly k+1=m-J+1 in this nonempty case, finite at every m. Each local\nmaximum has only finitely many planes even though the auxiliary arc is continuous.\n\nThe function is nonnegative and convex. Every ell_i>=0, so at x=0\neach local input is in {z_1<=0,z_2>=0}, and the terminal term vanishes.\nTherefore F_m(0)=min F_m=0. Each scaled local term before its factor1/2\nhas the1-smooth upper inequality on its two coordinates. Summing,\n\n    F_m(x+w)<=F_m(x)+<grad F_m(x),w>\n          +(1/4)*[sum_(i<k)(w_i^2+w_(i+1)^2)+w_k^2]\n       <=F_m(x)+<grad F_m(x),w>+||w||^2/2.             (22)\n\nEvery coordinate is counted at most twice. This pays the overlap cost\nonce in the explicit coefficient1/2; no dimension factor remains.\nFor completeness, (22) implies1-Lipschitz gradient here: fix y and\napply (22) to V(x)=F_m(x)-<grad F_m(y),x> with w=-grad V(x).\nConvexity makes y a minimizer of V. Consequently\n\n    F_m(x)-F_m(y)-<grad F_m(y),x-y>\n       >=||grad F_m(x)-grad F_m(y)||^2/2.              (23)\n\nAdd the reversed inequality and apply Cauchy--Schwarz to obtain the\nLipschitz bound. This argument does not assume a least smoothness\nconstant or an extra rescaling of the steps.\n\nAt the start of block i the claimed physical state is\n\n    x_j=ell_j+D_j*U_j for j<i,\n    x_i=ell_i+D_i,\n    x_j=0 for j>i.                                    (24)\n\nIt is true initially at e_0. Follow the scaled local path in coordinates\ni,i+1, leaving the other coordinates fixed. Before its checkpoint,\n\n    x_i>=ell_i+D_i*U_i>=ell_i,\n    0<=x_(i+1)<=D_i*Y_i=ell_(i+1).\n\nFor a previous component j<i, its first input is U_j and its second\nis at least ell_(j+1)/D_j=Y_j. Thus (19) makes that component zero.\nFor a future component j>i, its first input is nonpositive and its\nsecond input is0, so the other zero region makes it zero. The terminal\nterm is also zero until its activation. A differentiable nonnegative\nfunction has zero gradient at any zero, since every directional\nderivative there must vanish. Thus only the current component contributes.\n\nIts global gradient is D_i/2 times the local gradient. A physical\nstep h_t therefore induces exactly the local step h_t/2. This proves\nthat the candidate local path is the actual GD path of (21) for every\nordinary step, in its given order. The physical checkpoint b^(i) then\nleaves\n\n    x_i=ell_i+D_i*U_i,\n    x_(i+1)=D_i*(Y_i+sigma_i*b^(i)/2)\n           =ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1).\n\nThis proves (24) for the next block. No component is removed or changed\nat a transition; earlier components remain zero by the same inequality.\nAll steps, including all unselected occurrences of large levels, have\nbeen included. Empty blocks and zero steps require no deletion.\n\nDuring the terminal block let u be the sum of its executed physical\nsteps. Since0<=u<=s_k, its shifted moving coordinate is\n\n    x_k-ell_k=D_k-delta_tail*u/2\n       >=D_k-delta_tail*s_k/2\n        =delta_tail*(1+s_k/2)>=delta_tail.            (25)\n\nEvery earlier component remains zero. The actual gradient equals\n(delta_tail/2)*e_k at every terminal query and at the terminal point.\nHence direct substitution into (21) gives\n\n    F_m(x_(n_m))=D_k^2/[4*(1+s_k)].                   (26)\n\nEquations (20), (26), s_k=S_(m-1), and ||e_0-0||=1 prove EXACTLY (4).\nFor arbitrary L>0 set f_m=L*F_m. It is L-smooth and GD with physical\nsteps h_t/L has the identical path, while the normalization cancels L.\n\nFor the literal empty-selection case J>=m use k=0,D_0=1,ell_0=0,\nand (21) reduces to F_m=Hub_(1/(1+S_m))/2 on R, starting at1.\nThe same terminal calculation gives normalized payoff1/[2*(1+S_m)].\nThus the exact selection rule also has a defined realization at every\nearlier m>=6. No empty-case asymptotic is used in the final theorem.\n\n## 6. All original interpolation conditions and the actual radius\n\nFor every ordered pair a,b of the actual query points, the terminal\npoint, and ANY minimizer, write g_a=grad F_m(x_a), f_a=F_m(x_a).\nInequality (23) with x=x_b,y=x_a yields\n\n    Q_ab=f_a-f_b+<g_a,x_b-x_a>+||g_a-g_b||^2/2<=0.     (27)\n\nThus every original ordered smooth-convex interpolation row holds,\nincluding both orientations of optimum and terminal rows. It is a\nglobal function inequality, so no untested nonlocal pair or cycle\nremains. For f_m=L*F_m the final squared-gradient term is divided by\n2L, as required. The update equations were proved in Section5. Any\nGram matrix of these points and gradients is positive semidefinite\nbecause they are actual vectors in R^(k+1): its quadratic form is\nthe squared norm of their indicated linear combination. Zero steps\ngive equal actual jets but still retain their physical positions.\n\nThe exact minimizer used in (4) is0, already proved to minimize F_m;\nits radius is exactly ||e_0||=1. The true closest radius is also\naccounted for explicitly. Write z_circ,i=(c_(i,1),c_(i,2)). The zero\nset of the local phi_i, and hence f_i, is exactly\n\n    z_1<=c_(i,1),\n    epsilon*(z_1-c_(i,1))-(z_2-c_(i,2))<=0.           (28)\n\nThe endpoint planes make these inequalities necessary. They suffice\nbecause each other arc slope (p,-q) is\n\n    [(p-epsilon*q)/chi]*(chi,0)+q*(epsilon,-1),\n\na nonnegative combination by (10). Since (21) is a sum of nonnegative\nfunctions, its whole minimum set is the explicit nonempty polyhedron\n\n    C_min={x: x_i<=zeta_i,\n                  epsilon*x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon*zeta_i-D_i*c_(i,2)\n                    for0<=i<k;\n               x_k<=ell_k},\n    zeta_i=ell_i+D_i*c_(i,1).                         (29)\n\nIt contains0. For k>=1 the initial point e_0 violates the first inequality,\nbecause zeta_0=1-sigma_0*(epsilon+P_0)<1. It is therefore outside this\nclosed minimum set. For k=0 the set is the nonpositive half-line and\nthe same conclusion holds from initial1. Its exact closest radius is\n\n    rho_m=dist(e_0,C_min), 0<rho_m<=1.                 (30)\n\nAttainment follows by minimizing squared distance on the nonempty\nclosed set after restricting to a sufficiently large compact ball;\nstrict convexity makes the projection unique. The radius about the\nspecified minimizer is1, while the true normalized value is exactly\nP_m/rho_m^2>=P_m. Thus using the closest minimizer strengthens the final\nbound; there is no assumption that0 is the closest minimizer.\n\n## 7. Distinguishing the two geometric formulas and their radius costs\n\nDefine, on precisely the same selected positions, the scalar reference\n\n    P5_m=1/(1+2*s_k)*product_(i<k)\n               [(b^(i)-1)/(chi*s_i+max{epsilon*b^(i),A})]^2. (31)\n\nThis is the geometry draft's main mass-only formula(7), and the scalar\ndraft's F5. In that different one-envelope realization it is a lower\nestimate on a payoff normalized by its closest-minimizer radius. Its\nfinite factors have b-1, a maximum allowance and terminal1/(1+2*s_k).\n\nThe ACTUAL function in this document is instead the half-sum (21).\nIts specified-minimizer payoff P_m is the geometry draft's formula(8),\nequivalently the scalar draft's F5a, with a_sp=2*A/chi. Its factors\nhave b, the additive allowance2*A and terminal1/[2*(1+s_k)]. Its\nspecified radius1 and closest radius(30) are properties of this same\nactual function, proved above. The one-envelope radius formula from\nthe other draft is not assigned to (21).\n\nFor the remaining calculation (31) is just an explicitly defined\nscalar reference. No assertion about the other draft's realization\nis used as a premise. In particular one must not replace the ACTUAL\nfinite value P_m by P5_m. Section9 proves their ratio tends1 with\nall accumulated losses paid. The source-format construction itself\nhas already been derived jointly in Sections3--6.\n\n## 8. The exact activation phase and its logarithmic correction\n\nThe cost A in (2) satisfies the useful exact identity\n\n    A=epsilon+sqrt(1+epsilon^2)/[epsilon*(1-kappa)]\n           *[exp(theta_sp/kappa)-kappa*exp(kappa*theta_sp)]. (32)\n\nIndeed 1+(1+kappa)*T=(exp(d*theta_sp/kappa)-kappa)/(1-kappa),\nand chi*exp(d*theta_sp/kappa)=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(theta_sp/kappa).\n\nWith fixed K=14/5,E=1/100, the integral defining arctan gives\ntheta_sp=pi/2-E/m+O(m^(-3)). Factoring exp(theta_sp/kappa) from the bracket\nin (32), its relative subtracted term is exponentially small in m.\nThe separate additive epsilon is also exponentially smaller than the\nremaining positive term. The logarithms of sqrt(1+epsilon^2) and\n1/(1-kappa) have bounded Taylor remainders of orders m^(-2) and m^(-1).\nIt follows, with constants independent of m, that\n\n    log A=pi*m/(2*K)+log m-log E-E/K+O(1/m),\n    log(A/epsilon)=pi*m/(2*K)+2*log m-2*log E-E/K+O(1/m). (33)\n\nFor explicit remainder justification, the arctan integral differs\nfrom epsilon by at most epsilon^3/3; the log expansion follows from\nintegrating1/(1+t) on a fixed interval about0. After some fixed m,\ntheta_sp is bounded below by a positive constant, so the exponential\nrelative remainders are bounded by a fixed multiple of exp(-c_0*m)\nfor some c_0>0. Taking their logarithms is then justified by the\nsame integral bound. These estimates require no evaluated exponential.\n\nThe other quantity1+2/epsilon^2 in R_cut has polynomial growth, so\nR_cut=A/epsilon for every sufficiently large m. Since\nlog c=log lambda+1/m+O(m^(-2)) and a->1/lambda, the exact ceiling gives\n\n    J=theta*m+(2/log lambda)*log m+O(1),\n    theta=pi/(2*K*log lambda)=5*pi/(28*log lambda).    (34)\n\nThe O(1) includes the exact rounding in (3); it is not an alteration\nof the chosen cutoff. Section11 proves0<theta<220/343<1. Hence\n1<=J<m for every sufficiently large m and k/m->1-theta>0.\nThe excluded first theta*m levels and the logarithmic correction are\nthe activation payment. They are retained in the exponent below.\n\n## 9. Exact products and control of the entire growing index set\n\nFor J<m, (8)--(9) give the exact finite identities\n\n    P5_m=1/(1+2*S_(m-1))\n       *[a*c^J/(chi*S_J+epsilon*(1+a*c^J))]^2\n       *product_(j=J+1)^(m-1)\n          [a*c^j/(chi*S_(j-1)+epsilon*(1+a*c^j))]^2,  (35)\n\n    P_m=1/[2*(1+S_(m-1))]\n       *[(1+a*c^J)/(chi*S_J+2*A+epsilon*(1+a*c^J))]^2\n       *product_(j=J+1)^(m-1)\n          [(1+a*c^j)/(chi*S_(j-1)+2*A\n                                    +epsilon*(1+a*c^j))]^2. (36)\n\nThe maximum in (31) is EXACTLY epsilon*b^(i), by (9). Products with\nno factors are1. In particular a single selected checkpoint retains\nthe same prefix and tail H_(m-1).\n\nDefine the exact scalar corrections\n\n    delta_l=[(1-zeta)*2^l-1]/(zeta*c^l),\n    xi_j=1/(a*c^j),\n    D_first=chi*(1+delta_J)+epsilon*(c-2)*(1+xi_J),\n    D_bulk,j=chi*(1+delta_(j-1))+epsilon*v_mass*(1+xi_j),\n    d_first=chi+epsilon*(c-2), d_bulk=chi+epsilon*v_mass.\n\nThe denominators D_first,D_bulk,j in this section are scalar\nnormalizations, distinct from the physical scales D_i in Section5.\nUsing a/zeta=c-2 and a*c/zeta=v_mass, (35) becomes\n\n    P5_m=(c-2)^2*v_mass^[2*(k-1)]\n       /[(1+2*S_(m-1))*D_first^2*product_(j>J)D_bulk,j^2]. (37)\n\nThe exceptional first factor is c-2, not v_mass. Replacing its prefix\nby H_(J-1) would introduce an erroneous factor tending to lambda^2.\n\nHere c stays in a compact subset of(2,infinity), a and zeta are bounded\naway from0, and1-zeta=O(1/m). The EXACT errors obey\n\n    sum_(l>=J)|delta_l|\n      <=[(1-zeta)/zeta]*(2/c)^J/(1-2/c)\n                         +(1/zeta)*c^(-J)/(1-1/c),\n    sum_(j>=J)xi_j=c^(-J)/[a*(1-1/c)].                (38)\n\nThese sums extend to infinity only to upper-bound nonnegative error\nterms; no infinite-dimensional function is constructed. By (34) the\nbounds vanish exponentially in m. Since d_first,d_bulk>=chi>=1,\n\n    |D_first/d_first-1|<=|delta_J|+epsilon*(c-2)*xi_J,\n    |D_bulk,j/d_bulk-1|<=|delta_(j-1)|+epsilon*v_mass*xi_j.\n\nThe sum of these bounds, with delta_J counted twice if necessary,\nvanishes by (38). Each is eventually at most1/2. The integral formula\nfor log gives |log(1+t)|<=2|t| there. Therefore replacing all normalized\ndenominators simultaneously by d_first,d_bulk incurs a total logarithmic\nerror tending0, explicitly controlled by (38). This is a bound on the\nwhole product, not a pointwise o(1) multiplied by k without control.\n\nIt remains to pay every difference between the actual P_m and P5_m.\nFor each selected level j, let s^(j)=S_J when j=J and S_(j-1) otherwise,\nand put\n\n    V_j=2*A/(chi*s^(j)+epsilon*b_j),\n    tau_tail=(1+2*S_(m-1))/[2*(1+S_(m-1))] in(0,1).\n\nThe exact ratio is\n\n    P_m/P5_m=tau_tail\n                   *product_(j=J)^(m-1)[(1+xi_j)/(1+V_j)]^2. (39)\n\nFor every sufficiently large m, (38) gives S_l>=zeta*c^l/2 for l>=J.\nThe EXACT cutoff (3) gives A*c^(-J)<=epsilon*a. Thus\n\n    sum_j V_j\n      <=[4*A/(zeta*chi)]*c^(-J)*[1+1/(1-1/c)]\n      <=[4*epsilon*(c-2)/chi]*[1+1/(1-1/c)].           (40)\n\nThe separate1 in the bracket pays the first prefix; the geometric\nseries pays every later gap, including the repeated H_J. Since\n-log tau_tail=log[1+1/(1+2*S_(m-1))]<=1/(1+2*S_(m-1)) and\n0<=log(1+t)<=t for t>=0, (38)--(40) give\n\n    |log(P_m/P5_m)|\n      <=1/(1+2*S_(m-1))\n        +2*c^(-J)/[a*(1-1/c)]\n        +[8*epsilon*(c-2)/chi]*[1+1/(1-1/c)] ->0.      (41)\n\nThis pays the source-format additive2*A, b versus b-1, and its distinct\nterminal factor. Its last term is O(1/m); the other terms decay\nexponentially. No checkpoint loss is silently dropped.\n\n## 10. The exact limit, with the first record and terminal block retained\n\nSet gamma=4+2sqrt(2), q_rc=2*gamma. Elementary expansions with uniform\nremainders, obtained from the log integral as in Section8, give\n\n    log v_mass=gamma/m+O(m^(-2)),\n    log chi=pi*K/(2*m)+O(m^(-2)),\n    log(chi+epsilon*v_mass)=(pi*K/2+E)/m+O(m^(-2)).   (42)\n\nThe quantities depend only on m, not on the selected level. There\nare at most m factors, so summing all O(m^(-2)) remainders gives\nO(1/m). Equations (37)--(38) therefore give\n\n    P5_m=(c-2)^2/[(1+2*S_(m-1))*d_first^2]\n                 *[v_mass/d_bulk]^[2*(k-1)]*exp(o(1)). (43)\n\nThe o(1) is bounded by the explicit sums in (38). Moreover\nd_first->1, c-2->lambda^(-1), zeta->1, and (7) gives\n\n    lambda^m/(1+2*S_(m-1)) -> lambda*exp(-1)/2.        (44)\n\nTo verify (44), divide S_(m-1) by zeta*c^(m-1); its error delta_(m-1)\ntends0 by (38). Then use (lambda/c)^m=(1+1/m)^(-m)->exp(-1).\nThe logarithm of the growing power in (43) tends, by (34),(42), to\n\n    (q_rc-pi*K-2*E)*(1-theta).                       (45)\n\nThe replacement k=(1-theta)*m+O(log m) contributes O(log(m)/m) to\nthis logarithm. Combined with (1), (41), and (43)--(45), this proves\n\n    lim B_(n_m)*P_m\n      =Z_1/(2*lambda)*exp[-1+(q_rc-pi*K-2*E)*(1-theta)]\n      =L_joint.                                     (46)\n\nEvery geometric/product error has been summed: it is O(log(m)/m)\napart from exponentially decaying terms before applying the accepted\nbenchmark convergence. Only the already accepted convergence in (1)\nis needed, so no new convergence rate for B_n is assumed or computed.\nThe factor1/(2*lambda) includes the exceptional first record and the\ntrue terminal child. The enclosing beta cancels exactly through\na/zeta=c-2; it was not separately approximated in each of k factors.\n\n## 11. (10)\n\nFor every fixed0<tau<=1/4 the further claim is\n\n    lim_(m->infinity) B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,tau))\n                         =Z_1/(2*exp(tau)).            (11)\n\nIn fact, the strict benchmark deficits already hold at EVERY m>=1536:\n\n    B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,1/4))<237/250<1,\n    B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,tau))<79/80 for1/5<=tau<=1/4.  (11a)\n\nThe corresponding limits are strictly below the same constants. These\nclaims use only the accepted benchmark enclosure, rederived from its\nsaved endpoint in Section8. No new benchmark calculation is performed.\n\n## 3. Joint hierarchy identities and the selection mechanism\n\nFor fixed(m,tau), put\n\n    r=c*(c-2), v=a/(c-2)=beta/r,\n    M_j=1+S_j, d_j=a*c^j, b_j=1+d_j,\n    u_j=d_j/M_j, M_max=M_(m-1).                         (12)\n\nWriting x=tau/m and gamma=4+2*sqrt(2), direct expansion gives\n\n    r=1+gamma*x+lambda^2*x^2>1>beta>0.                 (13)\n\nThus0<v<1 and c>lambda>2. The sum recurrence is\nM_(j+1)=2*M_j+a*c^j with M_0=1. Substitution, using a=v*(c-2), proves\nby induction that\n\n    M_j=v*c^j+(1-v)*2^j, 0<=j<=m.                     (14)\n\nIt follows that\n\n    1<=M_j<=c^j,\n    0<u_j<=c-2,\n    M_j<=M_max for0<=j<m.                              (15)\n\nFor the first two inequalities use c>2 and M_j>=v*c^j. For the last,\nthe sum recurrence has positive added terms and M_j>0.\n\nChoose ANY currently selected checkpoint having minimum selected level\nj. Its immediate complete H_j children contain only levels<j, so neither\nchild contains a selected checkpoint. If s and t are the actual ordinary\nsums immediately before and after this checkpoint, then\n\n    s>=S_j, t>=S_j.                                   (16)\n\nThis includes a first or last selected checkpoint: its adjacent ordinary\nblock is then the true initial or terminal block. Tied minimum levels\ncause no problem because each child has strictly smaller levels.\n\nDeleting a checkpoint below means deleting it from the selected subset,\nnot from the physical word. Its full physical step b is reclassified as\nordinary, so the two ordinary blocks merge to the sum s+b+t. All other\nphysical indices, step values, and the one parameter pair are retained.\nEligibility of any remaining checkpoint is unchanged.\n\nWe prove one minimum-level deletion scheme for both channels under the\nsame whole-word alternatives\n\n    (I)  C>=r=c*(c-2),\n    (II) A>=2*C*M_max,                                (17)\n\nassuming C>=1, A>=2*C and2<c<=5/2. Section7 proves these assumptions\nand the dichotomy for the ENTIRE enlarged parameter domain. The two\nchannel comparisons below are separate; neither is inferred from a\npointwise ordering of(3) and(4).\n\n## 4. Mass channel: exact interior and terminal comparisons\n\nLet b=b_j=1+d be a selected minimum-level checkpoint, with\nM=M_j, S=M-1, u=d/M. Then0<u<=1/2 and s,t>=S.\nFor this channel write Q_M(b)=max{epsilon*b,A}.\n\nIf a next selected checkpoint b' exists, put R=Q_M(b'). Cancelling its\npositive numerator b'-1 and all unchanged factors shows that deletion\ndoes not lower Phi_M exactly when\n\n    d*[C*(s+b+t)+R] <= (C*s+Q_M(b))*(C*t+R).           (18)\n\nThe right side minus the left is increasing in Q_M(b). Its derivatives\nwith respect to R,s,t are, respectively,\n\n    C*s+Q_M(b)-d,\n    C*(C*t+R-d),\n    C*(C*s+Q_M(b)-d).\n\nThey are positive throughout s,t>=M-1 and Q_M(b),R>=A: indeed\nC*(M-1)+A>=C*M>=M>=2*d. Thus its minimum is at\ns=t=M-1 and Q_M(b)=R=A. Put z_M=(A-C)/M>=0. Dividing that minimum by\nM^2 gives the exact expression\n\n    E_M=C^2-C*u*(2+u)+z_M*(2*C-u)+z_M^2.              (19)\n\nUnder(I), u*(2+u)<=c*(c-2)<=C, so every grouped term in(19) is\nnonnegative. Under(II),\n\n    z_M=(A-C)/M>=C*(2*M_max-1)/M>=C,\n\nbecause M<=M_max and M_max>=1. Therefore\n\n    E_M>=C^2-(5/4)*C+C*(2*C-1/2)+C^2\n        =4*C^2-(7/4)*C>0.                            (20)\n\nHere u*(2+u)<=5/4 and2*C-u>0 justify each replacement. This proves\nthe interior deletion comparison in either branch of(17).\n\nIf b is the LAST selected checkpoint, the exact comparison is instead\n\n    d^2*[1+2*(s+b+t)]\n      <=(C*s+Q_M(b))^2*(1+2*t).                      (21)\n\nSince C*s+Q_M(b)>=s+1, it suffices to bound\n\n    d^2*[1+2*(s+b+t)]/[(s+1)^2*(1+2*t)].              (22)\n\nFor fixed s this decreases in t, because the fraction without d^2 is\n[1+2*(s+b)/(1+2*t)]/(s+1)^2. Its s derivative after suppressing the\npositive constant1+2*t is -2*(s+2*b+2*t)/(s+1)^3<0. Hence(22) is\nlargest at s=t=M-1. There it equals\n\n    u^2*[(4+2*u)*M-1]/(2*M-1)\n      <=u^2*(3+2*u)<=1.                              (23)\n\nFor the first inequality, the difference of the cross-multiplied\nnumerators is2*(1+u)*(M-1)>=0. For the second, u^2*(3+2*u) increases\non[0,1/2] and equals1 at1/2. This terminal comparison needs no\nadditional choice between(I) and(II).\n\nRepeatedly deleting a currently selected minimum level strictly reduces\nthe number of selections. The comparisons keep Phi_M nondecreasing\nuntil the empty-selection value1/(1+2*S_m) is reached. This proves\nthe first inequality in(7) under(17). It is a proof for an arbitrary\nfinite subset, not an executed recurrence or enumeration.\n\n## 5. Source-format channel: the numerator shift is paid explicitly\n\nKeep b=1+d, M=M_j, u=d/M, and s,t>=M-1 from Section4. Now define\n\n    Q_S(b)=2*A+epsilon*b.\n\nIts numerator is b itself. If a next selected checkpoint b' exists,\nwrite R=Q_S(b'). The exact interior deletion comparison, after cancelling\nthe unchanged positive b', is\n\n    b*[C*(s+b+t)+R] <= (C*s+Q_S(b))*(C*t+R).           (24)\n\nThe difference is increasing in Q_S(b), and its R,s,t derivatives are\n\n    C*s+Q_S(b)-b,\n    C*(C*t+R-b),\n    C*(C*s+Q_S(b)-b),\n\nrespectively. All are positive on the larger comparison region\ns,t>=M-1, Q_S(b),R>=2*A. In fact,\n\n    C*(M-1)+2*A-b\n      >=C*M+3*C-(M/2+1)>0,                           (25)\n\nusing A>=2*C, C>=1, M>=1 and d<=M/2. Therefore the smallest difference\nin(24) occurs at s=t=M-1 and Q_S(b)=R=2*A. Define\n\n    q=2*A-C>=3*C.\n\nThe minimum is EXACTLY\n\n    F_S=(C*M+q)^2-(d+1)*[C*(2*M+d)+q]\n       =M^2*[C^2-C*u*(2+u)]\n         +M*[q*(2*C-u)-C*(2+u)]+q^2-q.               (26)\n\nFor the first equality, note that the merged physical sum is\n2*(M-1)+(d+1)=2*M+d-1, so adding R=2*A yields\nC*(2*M+d)+q. The extra1 in b=d+1 is retained in every term of(26).\n\nUnder(I), the M^2 coefficient in(26) is nonnegative. Also\n\n    q*(2*C-u)-C*(2+u)\n      >=3*C*(2*C-u)-C*(2+u)\n       =C*(6*C-4*u-2)>=2*C>0,                       (27)\n\nand q^2-q>=0 because q>=3*C>=3. Thus(24) holds in branch(I).\n\nUnder(II), use the first expression in(26). Put z_S=q/M. Since\n\n    q=2*A-C>=C*(4*M_max-1)>=3*C*M,\n\none has z_S>=3*C. Moreover(d+1)/M<=3/2 and(2*M+d)/M<=5/2. Consequently\n\n    F_S/M^2\n      >=(C+z_S)^2-(3/2)*[(5/2)*C+z_S]\n       =C^2-(15/4)*C+z_S*(2*C-3/2)+z_S^2\n      >=16*C^2-(33/4)*C>0.                          (28)\n\nThe last step uses C>=1, so2*C-3/2>0 and substitution z_S>=3*C has\nthe claimed direction. Its final expression is positive for C>=1.\nThis proves the interior source-format comparison for both branches.\n\nFor a LAST selected checkpoint, deletion replaces the terminal factor\n1/[2*(1+t)] by1/[2*(1+s+b+t)]. The exact ratio of the selected\nlocal factor to the deleted local factor is\n\n    R_tail=b^2*(1+s+b+t)/[(C*s+Q_S(b))^2*(1+t)].       (29)\n\nBecause Q_S(b)>=2*A>=4*C and C>=1, its denominator satisfies\nC*s+Q_S(b)>=s+4. The resulting upper bound for(29) is\n\n    b^2*(1+s+b+t)/[(s+4)^2*(1+t)].                   (30)\n\nFor fixed s, the fraction without b^2 equals\n[1+(s+b)/(1+t)]/(s+4)^2 and decreases with t. Its derivative in s,\nwith the positive constant1+t suppressed, is\n\n    (2-s-2*b-2*t)/(s+4)^3<0,\n\nsince b=1+d>1. Thus(30) is largest at s=t=M-1, where\n\n    R_tail\n      <=(d+1)^2*(2*M+d)/[(M+3)^2*M]\n      <=(5/2)*[(M/2+1)/(M+3)]^2\n      <=5/8.                                       (31)\n\nFor the last two inequalities use d<=M/2 and\nM/2+1<=(M+3)/2. This is a uniform quantitative terminal contraction.\nIt has not been replaced by the mass-channel terminal comparison.\n\n## 6. Finite pruning and the two different empty-selection values\n\nFor the source channel only, define the empty-product bookkeeping value\nto be1/[2*(1+S_m)]. This is the value of its own displayed formula after\nall checkpoints are removed from the subset. It is a comparison sink;\nthe functional(5) retains its actual Huber baseline1/(1+2*S_m).\n\nStart with any nonempty selected subset, and repeatedly choose a minimum\ncurrently selected level. At an interior deletion use(24); at a terminal\ndeletion use(31). Every remaining subset still has the complete children\nproperty(16). At least one deletion is terminal, since the last remaining\ncheckpoint is terminal. All factors outside the local comparison are\nunchanged and positive. Multiplying the comparisons therefore gives\n\n    Phi_S<=(5/8)*1/[2*(1+S_m)]\n         =5/[16*(1+S_m)].                            (32)\n\nIf additional deletions are terminal, each contributes another factor\nat most5/8; only one such factor was needed in(32). The mass pruning in\nSection4 gives its own bound1/(1+2*S_m). Finally\n\n    5/[16*(1+S_m)]<1/(1+2*S_m),                      (33)\n\nbecause cross multiplication reduces to5*(1+2*S_m)<16*(1+S_m).\nThus under the hypotheses and dichotomy(17), both channels are bounded\nby the Huber baseline, and that baseline attains L_wide.\n\nNo pointwise comparison between Phi_S and Phi_M was used. The extra1\nin the source numerator is absorbed in the q-dependent terms(26)--(28),\nand the terminal factor is handled by(29)--(31). In particular, merely\ncopying the old b-1 proof would not prove the source-channel assertion.\n\nThe proof also covers level0, whose children are empty and M_0=1,\nand arbitrary ties between selected levels. The bounds were proved on\nthe larger class of all nonempty subsets of this positive word, so there\nis no lost threshold case, including B_sp at kappa>1 or equality at\neither actual eligibility threshold. No ordinary block is replaced by\nan envelope, alternative ordering, or child with a new parameter m.\n\n## 7. The deletion dichotomy holds on the entire enlarged domain\n\nAssume0<tau<=1/4 and m>=1536. First2<c<5/2. To give rational bounds,\nsqrt(2)<10/7 implies lambda<17/7; for m>=9,\n\n    c<=lambda*(1+1/(4*m))\n      <(17/7)*(37/36)=629/252<5/2.                   (34)\n\nAlso gamma=4+2*sqrt(2)<7 and lambda^2=3+2*sqrt(2)<6, so m>=2 and(13)\ngive\n\n    r-1<7/(4*m)+3/(8*m^2)\n       <=31/(16*m)<2/m.                              (35)\n\nFor EVERY pair0<epsilon<=1/2,kappa>0, one has C>1. Both branches of\nT_sp in(1) are positive. Therefore, without any restriction kappa<1,\n\n    A=epsilon+(C/epsilon)*[1+(1+kappa)*T_sp]\n      >=C/epsilon>=2*C.                              (36)\n\nThis includes kappa=1 and every kappa>1. The source's piecewise T_sp\nand threshold price remain unchanged.\n\nIf C>=r, alternative(I) applies immediately. Consider ANY remaining\npair with C<r. Since C>=sqrt(1+epsilon^2), equations(35) and m>=2 imply\n\n    epsilon^2<=C^2-1<r^2-1\n      <4/m+4/m^2<=6/m<=1/256.                       (37)\n\nThus epsilon<1/16. Using pi>3 and arctan(epsilon)<epsilon gives\n\n    theta_sp=pi/2-arctan(epsilon)>23/16>1.            (38)\n\nFor completeness pi>3 follows by comparing the unit circle circumference\nwith its inscribed regular hexagon, and arctan(epsilon)<epsilon follows\nby integrating1/(1+t^2)<1 for positive t. Neither uses a decimal value.\n\nThe SAME assumption C<r yields, directly from the formula for C,\n\n    kappa*theta_sp<=log C<log r<r-1<2/m.              (39)\n\nSince theta_sp>1 and m>=1536, this implies\n\n    kappa<2/m<=1/768<1/16.\n\nIn particular kappa<1 has now been proved in precisely the branch\nwhere the exponential expression for T_sp will be used. Put\n\n    X=(1-kappa^2)*theta_sp/kappa.\n\nFrom(38)--(39),\n\n    X>((1-kappa^2)*theta_sp^2/2)*m\n      >(255/256)*(529/256)*(m/2)\n       =(134895/131072)*m>m.                         (40)\n\nFor0<kappa<1, the formula in(1) simplifies exactly to\n\n    1+(1+kappa)*T_sp=(exp(X)-kappa)/(1-kappa)\n                    >=exp(X).                       (41)\n\nIndeed subtracting exp(X) gives\nkappa*(exp(X)-1)/(1-kappa)>=0. Combining(37),(40),(41) yields\n\n    A>=C*exp(X)/epsilon>16*C*exp(m).                  (42)\n\nThe positive exponential series gives exp(1)>1+1+1/2=5/2>c.\nTogether with(15),\n\n    M_max<=c^(m-1)<exp(m),\n    A>16*C*exp(m)>2*C*M_max.                         (43)\n\nThis is alternative(II). Hence every parameter pair is covered by\n(I) or(II), and Sections4--6 prove(7)--(8).\n\nThis is a finite uniform argument: no limits or optimized constants\nK=m*kappa and E=m*epsilon were assumed. It includes every horizon-\ndependent scaling, oscillatory choices, fixed positive curvature, both\nsides of kappa=epsilon, both sides of kappa=1, and epsilon=1/2. Within\nthe stated small-tau family there is no remaining parameter region.\n\nThe precise changes from the old-domain proof are now visible. Its\nsmall-C branch used kappa<=epsilon<1/16 to bound1-kappa^2. Here(39)\nforces kappa<1/16 directly. It also used its exponential formula for A\nthroughout the old domain. Here the universal bound(36) replaces that\nuse outside the small-C branch, and(39) independently justifies the\nexponential branch wherever(41) is used. No ordering of kappa and epsilon\nenters any deletion comparison. The new source-channel comparisons in\nSection5 additionally pay its different numerator and terminal factor.\n\n## 8. Review, retrieval, and exact scope\n\nThe following stress tests were checked symbolically in this ONE joint\ncandidate, without mathematical computation or a separate verifier:\n\n* Every original nonempty subset is addressed by its own minimum selected\n  level; no record-chain optimality is assumed.\n* A checkpoint can be first, last, sole, tied in level, or at level0.\n  Initial and terminal blocks still contain the stated complete children.\n* Removing a selection merges s+b+t and keeps the full physical b=1+d.\n  Interior next-checkpoint costs remain their ACTUAL values until the\n  proved monotonicity lowers them to the comparison threshold.\n* Both interior differences were expanded with their own numerators.\n  The source-format extra1 gives the negative terms visible in(26);\n  it is not discarded as a negligible large-level error.\n* The two terminal denominators remain separate. Finite\n  inequalities(37)--(43) cover the entire enlarged parameter domain.\n* The parameters of every child remain the enclosing(m,tau). At u=0, the vector(0,1) supports this hull as well.\nThis tests remote arc pairs, rather than only neighboring directions.\n\nFor any finite list of nonnegative local steps alpha_1,...,alpha_N,\nthe terminal backward state is r_N=v_N=(epsilon,-1). Write each state\nr_j=(U_j,-Y_j). Invariants are U_j>=epsilon, Y_j>=1 and\nY_j/U_j<=1/epsilon. For an uncapped state at angle u_j,\n\n    r_j=R(u_j)[(1+a_j)e(u_j)+kappa*a_j*t(u_j)], a_j>=0.\n\nFor step alpha=alpha_j, the cap condition is\nY_j<=kappa[U_j+(alpha-1)chi]. With mu=U_j+(alpha-1)chi, this implies\nmu>0 and\n\n    r_(j-1)-v(0)=mu*n(0)+(0,kappa*mu-Y_j),\n    r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha*v(0).\n\nBoth coefficients describe supporting normals, so v(0) is a\nprojection. The resulting inequality Y_(j-1)<=kappa(U_(j-1)-chi)\nmakes the cap condition true at every earlier nonnegative step. Its\nvertical coordinate stays fixed.\n\nIf the cap condition fails, the state cannot already be capped. Put\n\n    F_j(u)=<r_j,e(u)-t(u)/kappa>/R(u).\n\nOne has F_j(u_j)=1,\nF_j(0)=(U_j-Y_j/kappa)/chi<1-alpha, and\n\n    F_j'(u)=(kappa+kappa^(-1))(U_j*cos u+Y_j*sin u)/R(u)>0.\n\nThus F_j(u)=1-alpha has exactly one solution in(0,u_j], including\nu=u_j if alpha=0. To check the normal coefficient, the representation\nof r_j gives, for 0<=u<=u_j,\n\n    <r_j,t(u)>=R(u_j)[(1+a_j)sin(u_j-u)\n                              +kappa*a_j*cos(u_j-u)]>=0.\n\nAt the root, F_j(u)=1-alpha is equivalent to\n\n    r_j+(alpha-1)v(u)=(<r_j,t(u)>/kappa)n(u).\n\nThis is a nonnegative supporting normal. Adding v(u) establishes the\nrequired representation and projection at the previous state. Adding\nalpha(p,-q) preserves all three invariants because p>=epsilon*q.\nThe elementary expansion of squared distances confirms that a\nsupporting-normal inequality implies Euclidean projection. No inverse\nGD map was assumed globally invertible.\n\nFor two successive uncapped angles with Delta=u_new-u_old>=0, their\nt(u_old) components give\n\n    a_old=B_Delta+A_Delta*a_new,\n    A_Delta=exp(-kappa*Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),\n    B_Delta=exp(-kappa*Delta)sin Delta/kappa.\n\nBoth are nonnegative. With d=1-kappa^2>0, differentiating A_Delta\ngives A_Delta'-(d/kappa)A_Delta<=0, so A_Delta<=exp(d*Delta/kappa).\nAlso B_Delta<=Delta/kappa and 1+dB_Delta<=exp(d*Delta/kappa). Hence\n\n    1+d*a_old<=exp(d*Delta/kappa)(1+d*a_new).\n\nTelescoping from a_N=0 over a total angle no greater than theta_sp\nyields a_j<=T. Consequently the positive vertical coordinate is at\nmost chi[1+(1+kappa)T]=1+H. Caps do not increase it. If\nP=sum alpha_j*p(u_(j-1)) and Q=sum alpha_j*q(u_(j-1)), then\n\n    r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q),\n    0<=P<=chi*sum alpha_j, 0<=Q<=H, P>=epsilon*Q.\n\nThese estimates are uniform in the number and size of the local steps.\nThis is the point that lets the construction include every ordinary\nstep in each long physical gap without a cumulative geometric error.\n\n## 6. Finite local function, static global assembly, and exact payoff\n\nFor a physical block of sum s followed by checkpoint b, use the above\nbackward construction with its actual entries divided by2, so its local\nsum is s/2. Put b_loc=b/2 and\n\n    sigma=1/(chi*s/2+epsilon*b/2+A),\n    z_circ=(1,0)-sigma(epsilon+P,-1-Q),\n    U=1-sigma(P+epsilon*b_loc), Y=sigma*Q,\n    V_fin={0,v(0),v_0,...,v_N},\n    phi(z)=sigma*max_(v in V_fin)<v,z-z_circ>,\n    f_loc(x)=min_z[phi(z)+||x-z||^2/2].\n\nHere sigma>0, U>=0, Y>=0. The allowance gives\n\n    sigma[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=1.\n\nThe minimum in f_loc exists uniquely: phi is finite, convex and\nnonnegative, while the quadratic makes the objective coercive and\nstrictly convex. If z is the minimizer and g=x-z, the subgradient\ncondition is g in partial phi(z). It is sufficient by the completed\nsquare. For necessity, test the minimum at z+t(w-z), use convexity,\ndivide by t and let t decrease to 0. Thus no unstated envelope theorem\nis required.\n\nFor two such pairs, adding subgradient inequalities gives\n||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>. The trial point z at x+w gives the upper\nvalue bound f_loc(x+w)<=f_loc(x)+<g,w>+||w||^2/2. Subgradient support\nat z evaluated at the actual new minimizer z' gives the lower bound\n\n    f_loc(x+w)>=f_loc(x)+<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2.\n\nThese inequalities establish differentiability, global supporting\naffines and convexity; the first displayed inequality gives the\n1-Lipschitz gradient. They also prove f_loc>=0 and\n{f_loc=0}={phi=0}.\n\nFor x_j=z_circ+sigma*r_j and z_j=x_j-sigma*v_j, the projection inequality\nmakes v_j maximize <v,z_j-z_circ> over V_fin. Therefore sigma*v_j is\nan active subgradient of phi and grad f_loc(x_j)=sigma*v_j. Since\nV_fin is finite, the objective is finite even though its construction\nwas described with a continuous arc. The half-step ordinary run starts\nat(1,0), ends before its checkpoint at(U+sigma*epsilon*b_loc,Y),\nand the local checkpoint ends at(U,Y+sigma*b_loc).\n\nFor each nonzero v=(p,-q) in V_fin,\n\n    <v,-z_circ>=-p+sigma[p(epsilon+P)+q(1+Q)]<=0,\n    <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>=sigma[(1-b_loc)epsilon*p+q]<=0.\n\nThe first uses q/p<=1/epsilon and the allowance; the second uses\nb_loc>=1+epsilon^(-2). Decreasing the first coordinate or increasing\nthe second preserves the needed signs. This proves exactly the two\nphysical zero regions used by the assembly. It never asserts a\nnonnegative horizontal proximal coordinate.\n\nApply this construction in advance to each incoming block i and use\nsubscripts i for its local quantities. Define\n\n    D_0=1, ell_0=0,\n    G_i=sigma_i*b^(i)/2=b^(i)/(chi*s_i+2A+epsilon*b^(i)),\n    D_(i+1)=D_i*G_i, ell_(i+1)=D_i*Y_i,\n    delta_tail=D_k/(1+s_k).\n\nAll D_i are positive and finite for any fixed m; all ell_i are\nnonnegative. Let Hub_delta(z) equal0 for z<=0, z^2/2 for 0<=z<=delta,\nand delta*z-delta^2/2 for z>=delta. Its derivative is the clipped\ncoordinate max(0,min(z,delta)). With coordinates numbered0,...,k,\nthe actual normalized objective is\n\n    F_m(x)=1/2 sum_(i<k) D_i^2\n              f_i((x_i-ell_i)/D_i,x_(i+1)/D_i)\n           +1/2 Hub_(delta_tail)(x_k-ell_k).\n\nThis is one static function on R^(k+1), fixed by the word before GD.\nAt 0 each local first input is nonpositive and second input is 0, so\nF_m(0)=0. Every term is nonnegative and convex. Scaling a local\n1-smooth function by D_i^2 and its coordinates by1/D_i preserves its\nsmoothness on that coordinate pair. The complete quadratic upper\nremainder is\n\n    1/4[sum_(i<k)(w_i^2+w_(i+1)^2)+w_k^2]<=||w||^2/2.\n\nInterior coordinates and the terminal coordinate are counted twice;\nthe first coordinate is counted once. This proves the precise global\nconstant1, not a constant growing with dimension. Applying the upper\nbound to F_m(x)-<grad F_m(y),x>, whose minimum is attained at y,\nwith increment-(grad F_m(x)-grad F_m(y)), gives\n\n    F_m(x)-F_m(y)-<grad F_m(y),x-y>\n                 >=||grad F_m(x)-grad F_m(y)||^2/2.\n\nAdding its reverse proves the1-Lipschitz gradient everywhere. A smaller\npossible optimal Lipschitz constant is immaterial: the objective meets\nthe required bound1 without any subsequent step rescaling.\n\nAt the beginning of block i, the actual claimed coordinates are\nx_j=ell_j+D_jU_j for j<i, x_i=ell_i+D_i, and x_j=0 for j>i. This\nholds initially at e_0. Throughout the current ordinary local path,\nx_i>=ell_i+D_iU_i>=ell_i and 0<=x_(i+1)<=D_iY_i=ell_(i+1).\nFor every earlier component, its first input is its frozen U_j and\nits second is at least Y_j. For the immediately preceding component\nthis last assertion follows from x_i>=ell_i; for older components\nit follows from their already frozen coordinates. For every future\ncomponent the first input is nonpositive and the second is 0. Thus all\nthese components vanish; their gradients vanish because they are\ndifferentiable nonnegative functions at a minimum. The terminal Huber\nterm likewise vanishes until its activation.\n\nOnly the current local component contributes a gradient. Its gradient\non physical coordinates is D_i/2 times the local one. Therefore a\nphysical step h_t becomes precisely h_t/2 after dividing coordinates\nby D_i. This establishes every ordinary update in its original order.\nThe physical checkpoint leaves\n\n    x_i=ell_i+D_iU_i,\n    x_(i+1)=D_i(Y_i+sigma_i*b^(i)/2)=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1).\n\nThe induction advances without changing any old function. During the\nnext block x_(i+1) remains at least ell_(i+1), so the old function\nstays zero. This explicitly rules out a hidden fresh-child reset.\n\nIn the terminal block let u be the sum of executed physical steps.\nIts shifted coordinate equals\n\n    D_k-delta_tail*u/2\n       >=D_k-delta_tail*s_k/2=delta_tail(1+s_k/2)>=delta_tail.\n\nThus every terminal query and the terminal point stay in the affine\nHuber region. Earlier components remain zero. Substitution gives\n\n    F_m(x_(n_m))\n       =1/2[delta_tail*D_k-delta_tail^2*s_k/2-delta_tail^2/2]\n       =D_k^2/[4(1+s_k)].\n\nHence2F_m(x_(n_m))=P_m exactly. For f_m=L*F_m and steps h_t/L, all\niterates are identical and the L in the payoff cancels. At k=0 the\nsame proof is the single function Hub_(1/(1+S_m))/2, starting at 1.\n\n## 7. Every directed row and both radius conventions\n\nFor actual query/terminal/minimum data let g_a=grad F_m(x_a) and\nf_a=F_m(x_a). For L*F_m the last coefficient becomes\n1/(2L). The GD equations were proved physically, and the Gram matrix\nis PSD because these are actual vectors. Both endpoint slopes are present.\nConsequently its local zero set is exactly\n\n    z_1<=c_(i,1),\n    epsilon(z_1-c_(i,1))-(z_2-c_(i,2))<=0.\n\nBecause the entire objective is a sum of nonnegative terms, its\nminimizer set is the closed convex polyhedron\n\n    C_min={x: x_i<=zeta_i,\n              epsilon*x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon*zeta_i-D_i*c_(i,2)\n                    for 0<=i<k; x_k<=ell_k},\n    zeta_i=ell_i+D_i*c_(i,1).\n\nThe scale factors and the ell_i shifts in this formula are correct:\nmultiply each local inequality by D_i before substituting the inputs.\nThis set contains 0 by the established zero-region argument. At i=0,\n\n    zeta_0=1-sigma_0(epsilon+P_0)<1.\n\nThus e_0 lies outside C_min. In fact the first halfspace already gives\ndist(e_0,C_min)>=sigma_0(epsilon+P_0)>0. Nonemptiness and closedness\ngive attainment of the closest distance; convexity gives a unique\nprojection. Since0 is available, the exact closest distance rho_m\nsatisfies0<rho_m<=1. At k=0 the minimum set is the nonpositive half-line\nand rho_m=1.\n\nIt follows that the payoff about0 is exactly P_m, while the payoff\nabout the closest minimizer is exactly P_m/rho_m^2>=P_m. This audit\ndoes NOT infer that rho_m=1 or that the latter payoff has limit L_joint.\nThe asserted exact limit concerns the specified-minimizer value only.\nFor the lower bound on W_n either minimizer choice is permissible.\n\n## 8. Exact activation phase and simultaneous product errors\n\nThe exact identity behind the activation estimate is\n\n    A=epsilon+sqrt(1+epsilon^2)/[epsilon(1-kappa)]\n                    [exp(theta_sp/kappa)-kappa*exp(kappa*theta_sp)].\n\nIt follows by simplifying1+(1+kappa)T and multiplying by chi. For the\nfixed positive K,E, the identity theta_sp=pi/2-arctan(epsilon) and\nthe elementary integral remainder for arctan give\ntheta_sp=pi/2-E/m+O(m^-3). The bracket is exp(theta_sp/kappa) times\n1 minus an exponentially small nonnegative quantity. The separate\nadditive epsilon is exponentially negligible relative to the positive\nmain term. The elementary logarithm integral bounds then give\n\n    log A=pi*m/(2K)+log m-log E-E/K+O(1/m),\n    log(A/epsilon)=pi*m/(2K)+2log m-2log E-E/K+O(1/m).\n\nThese estimates have constants independent of m. The other alternative\n1+2/epsilon^2 in R_cut grows only polynomially, so eventually R_cut\nequals A/epsilon exactly. Since log c=log lambda+1/m+O(m^-2) and\nlog a=-log lambda+O(1/m), division and the ceiling yield\n\n    J=theta*m+(2/log lambda)log m+O(1),\n    theta=pi/(2K log lambda)=5pi/(28log lambda).\n\nThe rounding contributes at most1 and is retained in O(1). The\nexplicit rational bound theta<220/343<1 is checked below. Thus the\nselection is eventually nonempty and k/m tends1-theta. This correctly\npays both the linear number of discarded levels and their logarithmic\ncorrection; it does not replace the original finite cutoff.\n\nDefine exact errors, with zeta=a/(c-2),\n\n    delta_l=[(1-zeta)2^l-1]/(zeta*c^l),\n    xi_j=1/(a*c^j),\n    D_first=chi(1+delta_J)+epsilon(c-2)(1+xi_J),\n    D_bulk,j=chi(1+delta_(j-1))+epsilon*v_mass(1+xi_j),\n    d_first=chi+epsilon(c-2), d_bulk=chi+epsilon*v_mass.\n\nThe D_first and D_bulk,j here are scalar denominators; they are not\nthe physical scales D_i. Directly normalizing the exact product gives\n\n    P5_m=(c-2)^2*v_mass^[2(k-1)]\n           /[(1+2S_(m-1))*D_first^2\n                         *product_(j=J+1)^(m-1)D_bulk,j^2].\n\nThe first factor differs because its incoming sum is S_J. There is\nno extra c in its numerator. This is essential for the factor 1/lambda\nin the final constant.\n\nAs m grows, c stays bounded away from 2, a and zeta stay bounded away\nfrom 0, and 1-zeta=O(1/m). The following are bounds for the ENTIRE sets\nof errors, not estimates at a fixed level:\n\n    sum_(l>=J)|delta_l|\n       <=[(1-zeta)/zeta]*(2/c)^J/(1-2/c)\n                         +c^(-J)/[zeta(1-1/c)],\n    sum_(j>=J)xi_j=c^(-J)/[a(1-1/c)].\n\nSince J grows linearly with m, these bounds decay exponentially.\nDividing D_first and D_bulk,j by their positive leading terms gives\nrelative errors bounded respectively by\n\n    |delta_J|+epsilon(c-2)xi_J,\n    |delta_(j-1)|+epsilon*v_mass*xi_j.\n\nCounting delta_J twice covers both the first factor and the first\nbulk factor. The sum tends0, so each error eventually has magnitude\nat most1/2. Integrating1/(1+t) gives |log(1+t)|<=2|t| on that interval.\nThis proves a simultaneous o(1) logarithmic error over the whole\ngrowing product. The infinite sums are only scalar upper estimates;\nthe constructed function remains finite-dimensional.\n\nTo compare the actual P_m with this reference, put s^(J)=S_J and\ns^(j)=S_(j-1) for j>J, and define\n\n    V_j=2A/(chi*s^(j)+epsilon*b_j),\n    tau_tail=(1+2S_(m-1))/[2(1+S_(m-1))].\n\nThe exact ratio is\n\n    P_m/P5_m=tau_tail product_(j=J)^(m-1)\n                                      [(1+xi_j)/(1+V_j)]^2.\n\nFor all large m, S_l>=zeta*c^l/2 for every l>=J. The first term has\nincoming level J; every later term has incoming level j-1. Summing\ntheir geometric upper estimates gives\n\n    sum_j V_j\n       <=4A*c^(-J)/(zeta*chi) [1+1/(1-1/c)]\n       <=4epsilon(c-2)/chi [1+1/(1-1/c)].\n\nThe last inequality is precisely A*c^(-J)<=epsilon*a from the\nunchanged cutoff. The separate1 pays the exceptional first record;\nthe geometric series pays the repeated H_J and every subsequent gap.\nAlthough A grows exponentially, its total relative product penalty is\nonly O(epsilon)=O(1/m). An incorrect pointwise argument could have\nlost a constant here; the displayed sum avoids that error.\n\nUsing0<=log(1+t)<=t for t>=0 and\n-log tau_tail<=1/(1+2S_(m-1)), one obtains\n\n    |log(P_m/P5_m)|\n       <=1/(1+2S_(m-1))\n         +2c^(-J)/[a(1-1/c)]\n         +8epsilon(c-2)/chi [1+1/(1-1/c)] ->0.\n\nThus the additive2A, the replacement b-1 by b, and the exact terminal\nfactor have all been paid simultaneously. The finite products need\nnot satisfy P_m>=P5_m, and the source makes no such claim.\n\n## 9. Exact limiting constant and optional interface losses\n\nLet gamma=4+2sqrt(2), q_rc=2gamma=8+4sqrt(2). The elementary logarithm\nexpansions give, with uniform remainders depending only on m,\n\n    log v_mass=gamma/m+O(m^-2),\n    log chi=pi*K/(2m)+O(m^-2),\n    log d_bulk=(pi*K/2+E)/m+O(m^-2).\n\nThere are at most m factors, so their combined second-order remainder\nis O(1/m).
