Actual metric, local O(1) small-angle activation, all directed rows, overlap and accumulated-cost scope are paid; no all-word theorem.", "evidence": "# Correlated Euclidean stages: a finite calibration and an exact remote cycle\n\nStatus: JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical draft, max61. No assertion below is a\nnew accepted fact. This is one frame mechanism and one explicit terminal\ncorrection, within TASK.md and guidance42998ccb0a64c7b8; the later guidance\n2915a98c60a7ec96 leaves this allocation unchanged. No mathematical program,\nnumerical experiment, solver, executed recurrence, formal submission,\ninstallation, consult or sub-agent was used.\n\nThe result is a complete six-step, two-checkpoint static-function\ncalibration with changing gradients on BOTH incoming ordinary blocks,\nfollowed by a value-independent three-query obstruction to the natural\nrepeated gluing rule. The calibration is not an all-word checkpoint lemma.\nThe three-query obstruction does not exclude every correlated realization.\n\nAll displayed calculations are joint proofs in this draft, not deductions\nfrom unverified shared claims. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is used for the\nproject's directed-row convention and scope. We also prove the required\nenvelope and row identities directly. Throughout the payoff is W=2R:\n\n    2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L dist(x_0,argmin f)^2).\n\n## 1. Actual frame, primal metric, dual metric, and support arcs\n\nLet e_0,...,e_k be an orthonormal basis and 0<=r<1. Write\ns=sqrt(1-r^2), u_0=e_0 and u_(i+1)=-r*u_i+s*e_(i+1).\nHere r is the task's r_corr, not a covariance law for queried gradients.\nInduction, using the new coordinate e_(i+1), gives\n\n    ||u_i||=1, <u_i,u_j>=(-r)^|i-j|.                     (1)\n\nThus, for arbitrary real coefficients a_i,\n\n    ||sum_i a_i u_i||^2\n      =sum_i a_i^2+2 sum_(i<j) (-r)^(j-i) a_i a_j.      (2)\n\nThe coefficients are NOT orthonormal coordinates. If b_i=<u_i,x>,\nthe orthonormal coordinates of x satisfy\n\n    <e_0,x>=b_0,\n    <e_i,x>=(b_i+r*b_(i-1))/s, i>=1,\n    ||x||^2=b_0^2+sum_(i=1)^k (b_i+r*b_(i-1))^2/s^2. (3)\n\nFor k>=1 the inverse of the Gram matrix in (1) is tridiagonal:\nits two end diagonal entries are 1/s^2, its interior diagonal entries\nare (1+r^2)/s^2, and each adjacent off-diagonal entry is r/s^2.\nThis follows by expanding (3), since b=U^T x for U with columns u_i.\nFor k=0 both matrices are [1]. In particular constant positive inner\nproducts b_i=d require EXACTLY\n\n    ||x||^2=d^2[1+k*(1+r)/(1-r)].                       (4)\n\nThis is a conditional radius cost for that prescribed array, not a\nclaim that every correlated witness must pay it.\n\nAbsolute row sums of the Gram matrix and its inverse give\n\n    (1-r)/(1+r)<=lambda_min(Gram)\n       <=lambda_max(Gram)<=(1+r)/(1-r).                (5)\n\nFor fixed r and growing k both extremes are approached: insert the\nconstant and alternating-sign coefficient columns in (2), divide by\nk+1, and sum the resulting geometric series. The Rayleigh quotients\ntend (1-r)/(1+r) and (1+r)/(1-r), respectively. Consequently the\nconditioning can approach ((1+r)/(1-r))^2. If k*(1-r) tends to0,\nthe alternating-sign Rayleigh quotient is (k+1)(1+o(1)); an interior\ndiagonal entry of the inverse also implies\nlambda_min(Gram)<=s^2/(1+r^2) for k>=2. Both types of degeneration\nare relevant, depending on the joint limit.\n\nFix epsilon>0 and define the endpoint and its actual Euclidean data by\n\n    w_i=epsilon*u_i-u_(i+1)\n       =(epsilon+r)*u_i-s*e_(i+1),\n    omega=sqrt(1+epsilon^2+2*epsilon*r),\n    theta=arctan(s/(epsilon+r)).                        (6)\n\nIn the ORTHONORMAL plane (u_i,e_(i+1)), put\n\n    E_i(t)=cos(t)*u_i-sin(t)*e_(i+1),\n    T_i(t)=-sin(t)*u_i-cos(t)*e_(i+1),\n    c=omega*exp(kappa*theta),\n    V_i(t)=c*exp(-kappa*t)*E_i(t), 0<=t<=theta,\n    N_i(t)=E_i(t)+kappa*T_i(t), kappa>0.               (7)\n\nThe endpoints are c*u_i and w_i. If delta=z-t, then\n\n    <N_i(t),V_i(z)>/[c*exp(-kappa*t)]\n      =exp(-kappa*delta)*(cos(delta)+kappa*sin(delta)).\n\nIts derivative in delta is\n-(1+kappa^2)*exp(-kappa*delta)*sin(delta). As |delta|<pi/2,\nthe expression has its maximum1 at0; its inner product with the\nadditional slope0 is strictly smaller. Thus N_i(t) supports the\nconvex hull of0 and the whole arc at V_i(t). At t=0, e_(i+1) is\nalso a supporting normal. This verifies the actual Euclidean arc.\n\nWriting V_i(t)=p*u_i-q*u_(i+1) gives\n\n    p=c*exp(-kappa*t)*(cos(t)-r*sin(t)/s),\n    q=c*exp(-kappa*t)*sin(t)/s,\n    p>=epsilon*q>=0, p<=c.                            (8)\n\nFor t>0 the first inequality follows from cot(t)>=(epsilon+r)/s;\nat t=0 it follows from p=c,q=0.\nFor two such slopes in consecutive stages one has EXACTLY\n\n    <p*u_i-q*u_(i+1), p'*u_(i+1)-q'*u_(i+2)>\n       =-(r*p+q)*(p'+r*q').                           (9)\n\nFor stage separation d>=1 the right side is multiplied by\n(-r)^(d-1). This follows by substituting (1) into the four terms.\nIn particular adjacent slopes have negative inner products, while\nslopes two stages apart have positive ones when r>0. No assertion\nabout Markov dependence of all gradients follows from (9).\n\n## 2. Two genuinely changing ordinary steps and their exact values\n\nThis section specifies a finite block without root finding. Let\nDelta=theta/2 and define\n\n    A_D=exp(-kappa*Delta)*(cos(Delta)+sin(Delta)/kappa),\n    B_D=exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin(Delta)/kappa,\n    t_0=0, t_1=B_D, t_2=B_D*(1+A_D),\n    theta_l=theta-l*Delta, l=0,1,2,\n    R_l=c*exp(-kappa*theta_l),\n    v_(i,l)=R_l*E_i(theta_l),\n    r_(i,l)=R_l*((1+t_l)*E_i(theta_l)\n                            +kappa*t_l*T_i(theta_l)),\n    alpha_l=1-exp(-kappa*Delta)*cos(Delta)+B_D\n                            +(1+kappa^2)*B_D*t_(l-1), l=1,2. (10)\n\nBoth alpha_l are strictly positive. Resolving radial and tangent\ncomponents in the basis (E_i(theta_l),T_i(theta_l)) proves\n\n    r_(i,l)-r_(i,l-1)=alpha_l*v_(i,l), l=1,2.          (11)\n\nIndeed, cancellation of the tangent component is exactly\nt_l=B_D+A_D*t_(l-1), and the remaining radial coefficient is alpha_l.\nThe vector r_(i,l)-v_(i,l)=R_l*t_l*N_i(theta_l) supports the\nfinite slope hull conv{0,v_(i,0),v_(i,1),v_(i,2)} at v_(i,l).\nHence the chronological ordinary word (alpha_2,alpha_1) has gradients\nv_(i,2),v_(i,1), followed at its checkpoint query by v_(i,0)=w_i.\nThese three gradients have different directions when r<1.\n\nUse the following exact coefficients of its initial state:\n\n    r_(i,2)=A*u_i-B*u_(i+1),\n    B=c*kappa*t_2/s,\n    A=c*(1+t_2)-r*B,\n    Z=A+r*B=c*(1+t_2),\n    R0^2=||r_(i,2)||^2\n         =c^2*((1+t_2)^2+kappa^2*t_2^2).              (12)\n\nBy (11), r_(i,2)=w_i+alpha_1*v_(i,1)+alpha_2*v_(i,2).\nConsequently A>=epsilon, B>=1 and A-epsilon*B>=0, despite the\nsubtraction in the formula defining A. The same positive combination\nalso shows ||r_(i,l)||<=R0: all arc pairs have positive inner product,\nand the backward additions in (11) strictly increase the norm.\n\nFor any scale a>0 and center C define the finite local nonsmooth piece\n\n    phi_(i,a,C)(y)=max{0, a*<v_(i,l),y-C>:l=0,1,2}.   (13)\n\nAt x=C+a*r_(i,l), its envelope with parameter1 has\n\n    g=a*v_(i,l), y=x-g,\n    phi_(i,a,C)(y)=a^2*R_l^2*t_l,\n    f_local(x)=a^2*R_l^2*(t_l+1/2).                  (14)\n\nThese are retained physical values and proximal heights. They will\nnot be reselected when the pieces are assembled below.\n\n## 3. Complete two-checkpoint calibration, including the terminal correction\n\nFor this calibration restrict sqrt(2/3)<=r<1 and 0<epsilon<=1/2, and take\n\n    kappa=2*s/r,\n    ell=1+epsilon*r,\n    beta=ell/(epsilon+r),\n    M=A+beta*B=Z+s^2*B/(epsilon+r),\n    a=ell/M, C_0=0, C_1=-w_0-a*r_(1,2).              (15)\n\nThe first local scale is1, the second is a. Put\n\n    Hpref=omega+(1+2*a)*(R0+c).                       (16)\n\nChoose ANY real d strictly larger than the following explicit finite\nmaximum (this is a definition, not a parameter scan):\n\n    max{2*c, 8*c*r*epsilon, sqrt(8*c*r*a*Z),\n        c*r/epsilon,\n        [1+2*(epsilon+c)/r]/epsilon,\n        [Hpref+r*ell]/(epsilon*r)}.                  (17)\n\nSet b_0=2, b_1=1+d/a and choose the actual six-step word\n\n    h=(alpha_2,alpha_1,b_0,alpha_2,alpha_1,b_1).        (18)\n\nThe proposed prefix and terminal positions are\n\n    x_0=r_(0,2), x_1=r_(0,1), x_2=w_0,\n    x_3=-w_0=C_1+a*r_(1,2),\n    x_4=C_1+a*r_(1,1), x_5=C_1+a*w_1,\n    x_6=C_1-d*w_1.                                   (19)\n\nThe prefix gradients at indices0,...,5 are, in that order,\n\n    v_(0,2),v_(0,1),w_0,a*v_(1,2),a*v_(1,1),a*w_1.  (20)\n\nEquations (11), b_0=2 and b_1=1+d/a prove every GD update\nx_(j+1)=x_j-h_j*g_j, including both checkpoints.\n\nDefine a terminal center and ONE static max-affine function by\n\n    C_T=x_6-d*u_2,\n    J(y)=max{phi_(0,1,0)(y),phi_(1,a,C_1)(y),\n                                      d*<u_2,y-C_T>},\n    F(x)=min_y [J(y)+||x-y||^2/2].                    (21)\n\nThere are seven nonzero affine planes and the plane0, all fixed before\nthe run. We next prove that (19)-(20), with exactly (14), are the\nactual prefix jets of F and compute its actual terminal jet.\n\n### 3.1 Future stage planes at all first-block proximal points\n\nA nonzero first-block normal r_(0,l)-v_(0,l) is a positive multiple\nof N_0(theta_l). Directly,\n\n    <u_1,N_0(t)>=-r*cos(t)-s*sin(t)\n                          +kappa*(r*sin(t)-s*cos(t))<0, (22)\n\nbecause tan(t)<=s/(epsilon+r)<s/r. Every stage1 slope has component\np'+r*q'>0 along u_1 and its other component along e_2. It therefore\nhas nonpositive inner product with every first-block proximal point.\nMoreover, for any stage1 slope v=p'*u_1-q'*u_2,\n\n    <v,-C_1>=-ell*(p'+r*q')\n                    +a*((p'+r*q')*A+(r*p'+q')*B)<=0. (23)\n\nHere (r*p'+q')/(p'+r*q')<=beta, by (8), and a*M=ell.\nEquations (22)-(23) prove that every future stage1 plane is\nnonpositive throughout the first block, including its checkpoint.\n\n### 3.2 Every old plane at all second-block proximal points\n\nLet an old slope be v=p*u_0-q*u_1. At the second-block endpoint\nproximal point C_1 its inner product is\n\n    <v,C_1>=-epsilon*(p+r*q)-(r*p+q)\n                                     +a*(r*p+q)*Z.  (24)\n\nThe ratio (p+r*q)/(r*p+q) is at least\n(epsilon+r)/ell. Thus (24) is nonpositive provided\na*Z<=omega^2/ell. This holds because\n\n    a*Z=ell*Z/M<ell, omega^2-ell^2=epsilon^2*s^2>0.   (25)\n\nAt an earlier second-block proximal point, its displacement from\nC_1 is a*(r_(1,l)-v_(1,l)). The old slope has inner product\n\n    <v,r_(1,l)-v_(1,l)>\n      =-(r*p+q)*R_l*t_l*(cos(theta_l)-kappa*sin(theta_l)).\n\nThe stated restriction r>=sqrt(2/3) gives\n\n    kappa*tan(theta)<=1.                              (26)\n\nIndeed (26) is equivalent to 2*(1-r^2)<=r*(epsilon+r).\nConsequently the cosine difference and t_l are nonnegative, so the\nold plane value at every earlier proximal point is at most its\nvalue at C_1. Thus every old plane is nonpositive and the old\npiece, which includes the plane0, equals0 there. This pays the\nactual normal geometry in the second-block maximum.\n\n### 3.3 The terminal plane is inactive throughout the prefix\n\nEvery prefix proximal point has norm at most Hpref. For the first\nblock use ||r_(0,l)-v_(0,l)||<=R0+c. For the second use\n||C_1||<=omega+a*R0 and then add a*(R0+c); (16) bounds both.\nFrom (19)-(21),\n\n    <u_2,C_T>=-r*ell+a*(r*A+B)+epsilon*r*d>Hpref,     (27)\n\nby (17) and A,B>0. Hence the terminal plane is negative at every\nprefix proximal point and at0. Equations (23)-(25) also show the\nsecond piece is zero at0; the first piece is zero there by definition.\nThus J(0)=0, min J=0, and every claimed prefix proximal point has\nthe indicated local plane active in the global maximum. Its local\ngradient in (20) is a subgradient of J there, so (14) gives the\nactual prefix values and heights of F. No earlier plane, value or\nheight has been removed.\n\n### 3.4 The direct terminal jet fails, and its exact correction\n\nThe tempting pure new-direction terminal jet would be\n\n    g_6^ideal=d*u_2, f_6^ideal=d^2/2,\n    y_6^ideal=C_T, proximal height0.                   (28)\n\nLet\n\n    K0=a*Z+epsilon*d-1,\n    V=c*(r*K0-epsilon),\n    Dmix=c^2+d^2-2*c*d*r^2.                           (29)\n\nThe first cap plane at C_T has value V. Its original interpolation\nrow with the retained first jet and (28) is EXACTLY\n\n    Q_(0,6)^ideal=V>0.                                (30)\n\nThis follows either by inserting (14) into Q, or by canceling the\nfirst cap's affine height to obtain c*<u_0,C_T>. The fifth entry\nin (17) makes K0>2*(epsilon+c)/r, which proves V>0.\nThis is a failure of the specified terminal jet, not a\nvalue-independent cycle. For six steps it is an incoming terminal\nrow; using that jet to begin any further ordinary block makes it\na required queried-prefix row. The full-function request cannot\nignore it. We do not claim that (30) alone excludes value reselection.\n\nThe ONE correction in this calibration is to keep J unchanged and\nuse its actual mixed terminal gradient. Put\n\n    eta=V/Dmix,\n    g_6=(1-eta)*d*u_2+eta*c*u_0,\n    y_6=x_6-g_6.                                     (31)\n\nWe prove 0<eta<1 and that these are the actual terminal data. First,\nDmix>=(d-c)^2>d^2/4, while\n\n    V<c*r*a*Z+c*r*epsilon*d<d^2/4\n\nby the first three entries in (17). Thus eta is strictly between0\nand1. For every 0<=t<=1 set y(t)=C_T+t*(d*u_2-c*u_0).\nThe old cap minus the terminal plane at y(t) is V-t*Dmix, so they\ntie at t=eta.\n\nTo check ALL other planes, project y(t) into (u_0,e_1). Its coordinates\nare\n\n    (r*(K0+t*d*r)-epsilon-t*c, -s*(K0+t*d*r)).        (32)\n\nSince kappa*r-s=s and K0>2*(epsilon+c)/r, its first coordinate\nis positive and its downward second magnitude is at most kappa\ntimes that first coordinate. Therefore (32) is a nonnegative\ncombination of N_0(0)=u_0-kappa*e_1 and e_1. Both support the\nfirst-stage hull at c*u_0. The cap dominates every first-stage\nplane, and its value is positive.\n\nFor any second-stage slope v=p'*u_1-q'*u_2, direct expansion gives\n\n    <v,y(t)-C_1>\n       =-d*epsilon*(p'+r*q')-t*d*(r*p'+q')\n                                     +t*c*r*(p'+r*q')<=0, (33)\n\nbecause d*epsilon>c*r. Thus no second-stage plane intervenes.\nAt y_6 the old cap and terminal plane tie above all remaining\nplanes, and their convex combination in (31) belongs to partial J(y_6).\nAs x_6-y_6=g_6, this proves the proximal condition exactly.\n\nEvaluating the envelope yields\n\n    F(x_6)=d^2/2+V^2/(2*Dmix).                        (34)\n\nFor an explicit check, the envelope value associated to the mixture\nt is d^2/2+t*V-t^2*Dmix/2: at the tie, use the weighted average\nof the two active affine values at x_6 and subtract half the squared\nnorm of their averaged slope. Substitution of eta gives (34).\nThis changes only the illegal proposed terminal jet (28); every\nprefix value and every prefix proximal height in (14) is unchanged.\n\n### 3.5 Global smoothness, every directed row, and the exact radius\n\nThe function J is a finite convex maximum, nonnegative and zero at0.\nFor every x, J(y)+||x-y||^2/2 is coercive and strictly convex, hence\nhas a unique minimizer y. The optimality condition is x-y in partial J(y):\nthe forward implication follows by testing y+t(z-y) and letting t\ndecrease to0, and the reverse follows by completing the squared norm.\nFor two pairs write g=x-y and g'=x'-y'. The two subgradient\ninequalities imply\n\n    ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>.\n\nUsing y as a trial point at x+w gives the upper estimate\nF(x+w)<=F(x)+<g,w>+||w||^2/2; the subgradient inequality gives\nF(x+w)>=F(x)+<g,w>. These bounds prove differentiability with\ngradient g, convexity, and a globally1-Lipschitz gradient.\nThe minimum is0 and argmin F={J=0}.\n\nFor this calibration the least global Lipschitz constant is actually1.\nAt0 the planes with slopes0,c*u_0,w_0,a*w_1 are all active; the\nlast one is active because a*M=ell makes its inequality in (23)\nan equality. The three nonzero slopes are linearly independent\nby (1),(6), so their convex hull with0 has nonempty Euclidean\ninterior and belongs to partial J(0). At every input in that\ninterior the proximal point is0 and grad F(x)=x. Thus no global\ngradient Lipschitz constant strictly below1 is possible.\n\nFor any two actual samples, terminal and minimizers included,\nwrite H_i=J(y_i), f_i=H_i+||g_i||^2/2 and x_i=y_i+g_i. Then\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =H_i-H_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i><=0.                  (35)\n\nThis proves every old/new directed interpolation row, in both\norientations, without a matrix of unchecked remote entries.\n\nFinally x_0=r_(0,2)=w_0+alpha_1*v_(0,1)+alpha_2*v_(0,2)\nis a nonnegative combination of first-stage slopes active at0.\nEvery z with J(z)=0 has nonpositive inner product with each of\nthese slopes, hence <x_0,z><=0. Consequently\n\n    ||x_0-z||^2>=||x_0||^2+||z||^2,\n    dist(x_0,argmin F)^2=R0^2,                       (36)\n\nand0 is its unique closest minimizer. The complete finite conclusion is\n\n    W_6(h)>=[d^2+V^2/Dmix]/R0^2.                     (37)\n\nThe function L*F has smoothness bound L, uses precisely the same\npositions for physical steps h_i/L, and gives the same normalized\npayoff. There is no half-sum, preconditioning or concealed step rescaling.\n\n## 4. The first remote cycle of the repeated pure-stage rule\n\nThe terminal mixture above is not the prescribed initial gradient of\nanother fresh local arc. Here is the exact failure if one instead\ncontinues the locally maximal, pure-stage zero-height gluing rule.\nIt uses the SAME frame and arcs, now in R^4 to allow u_3, not a\nnew mechanism or an attempted second correction.\n\nRetain the parameters and the two-step ordinary block (10). Choose\nT0>0 and T1>0, and define\n\n    a_0=1, a_1=T0*ell/M, a_2=T1*ell/M,\n    C_0=0,\n    C_1=-T0*w_0-a_1*r_(1,2),\n    C_2=C_1-T1*w_1-a_2*r_(2,2),\n    b_0=1+T0, b_1=1+T1/a_1.                         (38)\n\nThe proposed trajectory consists of the three ordinary blocks with\ntheir translated/scaled local data (14), with the two checkpoints\nb_0,b_1 between them. Every GD update agrees with (11),(38).\nTo keep the three decisive samples strictly in the queried prefix,\ntake the physical nine-step word\n\n    (alpha_2,alpha_1,b_0,alpha_2,alpha_1,b_1,\n                                      alpha_2,alpha_1,1). (39)\n\nThe final ordinary step1 is executed from the third endpoint query;\nit does not introduce a third selected checkpoint. The endpoint\nqueries at physical indices2,5,8 have proposed data\n\n    X_i=C_i+G_i, G_i=a_i*w_i, F_i=||G_i||^2/2,\n    i=0,1,2, corresponding respectively to indices2,5,8. (40)\n\nThese are local data, not claimed jets of a common function.\nDefine the positive quantities\n\n    z=ell*Z/M,\n    H=epsilon+r-r*z\n       =s^2*[epsilon+r*ell*B/((epsilon+r)*M)],\n    K=omega^2-ell*z\n       =s^2*[epsilon^2+ell^2*B/((epsilon+r)*M)].      (41)\n\nAll equalities follow from M-Z=s^2*B/(epsilon+r) and\nomega^2-ell^2=epsilon^2*s^2. In particular H,K>0.\nFor the original row convention the following is an EXACT\nvalue-independent cycle:\n\n    Q_(2,8)+Q_(8,5)+Q_(5,2)\n                 =-T0*K+T1*ell*H.                  (42)\n\nProof: with the convenient test values in (40), the elementary\ncancellation in Q gives Q_(i,j)=<G_i,C_j-C_i>. Now\n\n    <w_1,w_0>=-(epsilon+r)*ell,\n    <w_i,r_(i,2)>=(epsilon+r)*M,\n    <w_0,r_(1,2)>=-ell*Z,\n    <w_0,r_(2,2)>=r*ell*Z.\n\nThese follow from (6),(9),(12). Therefore\n\n    Q_(5,2)=a_1*[-T0*(epsilon+r)*ell\n                                      +a_1*(epsilon+r)*M]=0,\n    Q_(8,5)=a_2*[-T1*(epsilon+r)*ell\n                                      +a_2*(epsilon+r)*M]=0,\n    Q_(2,8)=-T0*omega^2+a_1*ell*Z\n                          +T1*(epsilon+r)*ell-a_2*r*ell*Z\n             =-T0*K+T1*ell*H.\n\nThe function values cancel in the sum of ANY three original Q rows\naround this cycle. Thus calculating it with (40) proves (42) for\nEVERY possible reassignment of all values on these same positions\nand gradients. Translation of all positions also leaves it unchanged.\nThere is no minimizer in this cycle.\n\nIn particular, take the explicit positive choice\n\n    T1=2*T0*K/(ell*H).                                (43)\n\nThen the cycle equals T0*K>0. At least one of its three original\nqueried-prefix rows is positive, independently of the entire value\narray, of any choice or relocation of the minimizer, and of all\nunqueried terminal data. No convex1-smooth function realizes these\njets. This is stronger than the single failed terminal row (30).\n\nThe rejected hypothesis is precise: identical chosen local arcs and\nordinary blocks, local endpoint proximal heights0, Euclidean GD,\nand maximal adjacent scales a_(i+1)=T_i*ell/M as in (38), with\nT1/T0>K/(ell*H). It is not a theorem about all scale choices, all\nheight-bearing interpolants, arbitrary arcs, or all correlated frames.\nThe local blocks themselves remain legal. Their pairwise gluing\ndoes not extend to the third block by that rule.\n\nThe six-step calibration in Section3 is not advertised as a repair\nof the nine-step word (39). It gives the exact static function and\nactual mixed terminal jet BEFORE demanding a third pure local block.\nNo part of the failed third block is silently discarded from a claim\nabout that same longer word. No second correction of (42) is attempted.\n\n## 5. Local activation, transfer, radius, and turning costs\n\nThe following accounts for the apparent small-angle gain and its\nlimits. It does not turn the failed repeated rule into a lower bound.\n\nFor a general ordered nonnegative ordinary word, a backward arc\ntrajectory can be constructed in the actual orthonormal plane.\nStart at w=(epsilon+r,-s). Suppose the current backward point is\n(X,-Y), projected to V(theta_j), with positive X,Y. For a step alpha,\nif\n\n    Y<=kappa*[X+(alpha-1)*c],\n\nthe preceding projection is c*u and the preceding point is obtained\nby adding alpha*c*u. Put mu=X+(alpha-1)*c; its normal is\nmu*N(0)+(kappa*mu-Y)*e_new, with nonnegative coefficients.\nOtherwise choose the unique angle t in (0,theta_j] for which\n\n    <(X,-Y),E(t)-T(t)/kappa>/[c*exp(-kappa*t)]=1-alpha. (44)\n\nThe left side equals1 at theta_j. Its value at0 is\n(X-Y/kappa)/c<1-alpha, and its derivative is\n(kappa+1/kappa)*(X*cos(t)+Y*sin(t))/[c*exp(-kappa*t)]>0.\nThe root therefore exists uniquely. Resolving radial and tangent\ncomponents shows that adding alpha*V(t) gives a nonnegative\nsupporting normal at that slope. The tangent coefficient is\nnonnegative because the polar angle of the current point is at\nleast theta_j and the new angle is no larger. Backward addition\npreserves X>0,Y>0 and Y/X<=s/(epsilon+r). A capped point stays\ncapped in all earlier steps. These observations inductively prove\nthe asserted local construction, including alpha=0.\n\nAt an uncapped point use the representation R*((1+t)*E+kappa*t*T).\nFor two consecutive backward angles separated by Delta>=0, its\nnormal coefficient obeys exactly\n\n    t_old=B(Delta)+A(Delta)*t_new,\n    A(Delta)=exp(-kappa*Delta)*(cos(Delta)+sin(Delta)/kappa),\n    B(Delta)=exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin(Delta)/kappa.        (45)\n\nLet d0=1-kappa^2. Differentiation gives\nA'-(d0/kappa)*A=-(1+1/kappa^2)*exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin(Delta)<=0.\nFor kappa<1, A<=exp(d0*Delta/kappa) and\n1+d0*B<=exp(d0*Delta/kappa); the latter follows from\nB<=Delta/kappa and exp(t)>=1+t. Hence telescoping (45) yields\n\n    t<=T=[exp((1-kappa^2)*theta/kappa)-1]/(1-kappa^2).\n\nFor kappa>=1, A<=1 and B<=Delta/kappa instead give t<=T=theta/kappa.\nThese bounds also bound the vertical coordinate at the first cap,\nwhich is unchanged by subsequent backward cap additions.\n\nWrite the resulting initial point as A_b*u_i-B_b*u_(i+1), and let\nS be the physical sum of this ordinary block. Equation (8) implies\n\n    A_b=epsilon+P, B_b=1+Q,\n    0<=P<=c*S, Q>=0, P>=epsilon*Q,\n    B_b<=Bmax=c*[(1+T)/omega+(kappa/s)*T].             (46)\n\nFor the last bound use sin(t)<=sin(theta)=s/omega and R<=c in\nthe downward orthonormal coordinate R*((1+t)*sin(t)+kappa*t*cos(t)),\nthen divide by s. These are actual vector coefficients, with (2)\nstill required for all norms.\n\nIf the local trajectory starts at D*u_i, is scaled by a, and its\ncenter is D*u_i-a*(A_b*u_i-B_b*u_(i+1)), its required zero value\nat the old origin is equivalent to\n\n    D/a>=M_b:=A_b+beta*B_b.                            (47)\n\nIndeed divide each endpoint or arc plane inequality at0 by its\npositive factor p+r*q. The ratio (r*p+q)/(p+r*q) is maximized\nat the endpoint p/q=epsilon, giving beta. Thus (47) is necessary\nas well as sufficient for that zero set; it is not only a loose\ndisplacement estimate. In particular\n\n    M_b<=c*S+Aadd, Aadd=epsilon+beta*Bmax.             (48)\n\nFor the source-style buffer point which retains the coefficient\nof u_(i+1) just before a checkpoint, the plane residual is proportional to\n\n    (1-b)*epsilon*(p+r*q)+(r*p+q).\n\nConsequently that particular buffer has the exact threshold\n\n    b>=1+beta/epsilon.                               (49)\n\nThe actual post-checkpoint point, with its additional b*a*u_(i+1),\nis in the current piece's zero set already for b>=1. Equation (49)\nprices an extra zero-region buffer. It must not be imposed as a\nnecessary threshold for every correlated construction: Section3\nuses b_0=2 and proves its cross-plane conditions directly.\n\nFor consecutive stages, a preceding checkpoint with T_i=(b_i-1)*a_i\ncan supply the next scale only up to\n\n    a_(i+1)<=T_i*ell/M_(i+1)                           (50)\n\nif the next endpoint plane is to vanish at the preceding center.\nThis follows by exactly the calculation (23). When (26) holds,\nthe other direction's old-plane conditions also hold for the local\narc normals. Thus (50) is a legitimate ADJACENT transfer bound.\nThe gain ell is real Euclidean geometry and cannot be dropped or\nreplaced by an orthogonal-coordinate transfer formula.\n\nThe full local initial norm is\n\n    ||A_b*u_i-B_b*u_(i+1)||^2\n       =A_b^2+B_b^2+2*r*A_b*B_b.                    (51)\n\nFor the actual calibration (36) supplies its closest-minimizer\ninterpretation; (51) alone does not establish such an interpretation\nfor an arbitrary global gluing. If an initial state includes other\nstages, its full norm must instead use (2), or (3) for prescribed\ninner products. Neither can be replaced by a sum of stage squares.\n\n### 5.1 Limits as r tends to1\n\nKeep 0<epsilon<=1/2 fixed and kappa=2*s/r. Then\n\n    theta/s -> 1/(1+epsilon),\n    theta/kappa -> 1/[2*(1+epsilon)],\n    c -> 1+epsilon,\n    T -> exp(1/[2*(1+epsilon)])-1,\n    Bmax -> (1+T)+2*(1+epsilon)*T,\n    beta -> 1.                                      (52)\n\nThus the LOCAL activation allowance (48) stays finite: the\northogonal spiral's exponential in 1/kappa has not been imported\ninto this different Euclidean angle. This observation is paid for\nby the actual geometry, but it is not a global checkpoint theorem.\n\nFor the specific two-step ordinary block let\n\n    tau=1/[4*(1+epsilon)], Ttwo=2*tau+tau^2.\n\nEquations (10)-(12),(15) give, without executing a recurrence,\n\n    alpha_1 -> tau, alpha_2 -> tau*(1+tau), t_2 -> Ttwo,\n    B -> 2*(1+epsilon)*Ttwo,\n    M,Z -> (1+epsilon)*(1+Ttwo),\n    a -> 1/(1+Ttwo),\n    R0^2 -> (1+epsilon)^2*(1+Ttwo)^2.                 (53)\n\nThese limits are finite and positive. The threshold (17) remains\nbounded for each fixed epsilon; near r=1 it is O(1/epsilon) as\nepsilon tends to0, with constants bounded for 0<epsilon<=1/2.\nThus a fixed sufficiently large d satisfies (17) for all r close\nenough to1. For such a fixed d,\n\n    V -> (1+epsilon)*epsilon*d,\n    Dmix -> [d-(1+epsilon)]^2,\n    eta -> (1+epsilon)*epsilon*d/[d-(1+epsilon)]^2.    (54)\n\nThe correction is not identically zero in this small-angle limit.\nFor a prospective next physical step h, the actual next position\nminus the position predicted using the pure jet is EXACTLY\n\n    h*(g_6^ideal-g_6)=h*eta*(d*u_2-c*u_0),\n    norm = h*V/sqrt(Dmix).                            (55)\n\nFor large d and then r near1 this has scale h*(1+epsilon)*epsilon.\nThere is no uniform bound over arbitrary subsequent physical h.\nOne cannot carry the improved terminal value (34) into a further\nordinary block while silently restoring the pure terminal gradient.\n\n### 5.2 Accumulated factors and the exact surviving cycle cost\n\nAn adjacent-only multiplication of (48),(50), for positive ordinary\nsums S_i, would incur the squared coefficient factor (c/ell)^(2k),\nactivation factors and the checkpoint numerators b_i-1. Its exact\nlogarithmic loss relative to the purely diagnostic product using\nb_i/S_i is\n\n    2*k*log(c/ell)+2*sum_i log(1+Aadd/(c*S_i))\n                                      -2*sum_i log(1-1/b_i). (56)\n\nThis is only an accounting identity for the proposed adjacent\nestimate. Because (42) can be positive, it is NOT an actual\nlower bound for a repeated k-stage witness. When S_i=0 use the\nundivided denominators in (48); the diagnostic (56) is not defined.\n\nThe Euclidean coefficient in (56) satisfies EXACTLY\n\n    log(c/ell)=kappa*theta\n                    +(1/2)*log(1+epsilon^2*s^2/ell^2). (57)\n\nThe ell transfer cancels the leading endpoint-norm factor. In\nparticular, with epsilon fixed and r tending to1,\n\n    log(c/ell)/s^2\n      -> 2/(1+epsilon)+epsilon^2/[2*(1+epsilon)^2].   (58)\n\nKeeping this coefficient loss negligible as k grows would require\nk*s^2 small. But then r^d is near1 for all stage separations\nd<=k: none of the remote stages decouples.\n\nThis is not just a conditioning warning. Set\nzeta=2*Ttwo/(1+Ttwo). The exact remote cycle constants in (41)\nobey\n\n    H/s^2 -> epsilon+zeta,\n    K/s^2 -> epsilon^2+(1+epsilon)*zeta,\n    K/(ell*H)\n      -> [epsilon^2+(1+epsilon)*zeta]\n                          /[(1+epsilon)*(epsilon+zeta)] < 1. (59)\n\nThe strict inequality follows because the denominator exceeds the\nnumerator by epsilon. In particular even the equal-budget choice\nT1=T0 fails for every r sufficiently close to1, and\n\n    [Q_(2,8)+Q_(8,5)+Q_(5,2)]/(T0*s^2) -> epsilon>0. (60)\n\nA small positive interpolation defect is still incompatible with\nan exact common function. Equation (60) is not permission to omit\nit at each stage or to average illegal realizations. The specific\nsaturated repeated pilot is already excluded at this finite length;\nit has no legal unbounded-stage scaling to price as a payoff.\n\nFor reference, the entire old-plane memory is also explicit. With\npossibly differing local starts A_i*u_i-B_i*u_(i+1), let\nZ_i=A_i+r*B_i, T_i=(b_i-1)*a_i and retain the center update\nC_(i+1)=C_i-T_i*w_i-a_(i+1)*r_(i+1,start).\nFor every j<i,\n\n    <u_j,C_i-C_j>\n      =-(epsilon+r)*T_j\n       -sum_(t=j+1)^(i-1) (-r)^(t-j)\n                                  [a_t*Z_t+(epsilon+r)*T_t]\n       -(-r)^(i-j)*a_i*Z_i.                          (61)\n\nEvery old cap at a proposed zero-height endpoint requires the\nright side to be nonpositive. It follows by expanding the center\nrecursion with <u_j,r_(t,start)>=(-r)^(t-j)*Z_t and\n<u_j,w_t>=(-r)^(t-j)*(epsilon+r). Thus all old centers and all\nphysical displacements remain present. Absolute geometric weights\nsum to (1-r^(i-j))/(1-r); their effective number grows on the scale\nmin{i-j,1/(1-r)}. Their signs may matter; this absolute sum is not\nclaimed to be an unavoidable radius loss. In the small total-turning\nregime the full old-plane memory has order k, not order1.\n\n## 6. Smoothness overlap if a sum were substituted\n\nThe actual construction (21) uses one envelope and pays global\nsmoothness1 exactly as proved, irrespective of r. There is no\noverlap factor in (37). A half-sum argument from orthogonal stages\nwould be invalid here without a new proof.\n\nTo quantify that statement, let P_i be the orthogonal projector onto\nspan(u_i,e_(i+1)). Since these two vectors are orthonormal,\nP_i=u_i*u_i^T+e_(i+1)*e_(i+1)^T. Consequently\n\n    sum_(i=0)^(k-1) P_i+u_k*u_k^T\n       =U*U^T+sum_(j=1)^k e_j*e_j^T=:Aov,\n    lambda_max(Gram)<=lambda_max(Aov)\n                              <=lambda_max(Gram)+1. (62)\n\nFor the concrete half-sum of local quadratic functions\n\n    Fquad(x)=(1/4)*[sum_(i<k)||P_i*x||^2+<u_k,x>^2],\n\nthe TRUE least gradient Lipschitz constant is\nlambda_max(Aov)/2, because its Hessian is Aov/2. For fixed k and\nr tending to1, Aov tends\n(k+1)*e_0*e_0^T+sum_(j=1)^k e_j*e_j^T, so this constant tends\n(k+1)/2. In particular it exceeds1 when k>=2 and r is sufficiently\nnear1. This refutes the generic old half-sum bound in these spans.\nIt does not assign that least constant to our particular local\nenvelopes or claim they all attain their largest Hessians together.\nA sum construction would have to prove its own global constant\nand charge the corresponding division of every physical step.\n\n## 7. Source audit, status, and the exact missing interface\n\nThe immutable assemblies c8fcf4ced5a34046 and6be9249882fd0868 were\nread before this construction. Its fixed endpoint\nand prescribed zero-height minimum impose its own original endpoint\nrow. Changing the actual Euclidean angle changes that local row to\n(47), but does not erase the new global cycles (42).\n\nThe primary source Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, Silver Rate Is (Almost)\nOptimal for Gradient Descent, arXiv:2609.09152v2, 2026, was opened on\nthe web and its existing exact local PDF extraction was read at\nAppendix A.3. No new external theorem is a premise.\n\nThe actual rejected hypotheses of75d019fd344b8136 were checked:\nit has constant-gradient halves, its prescribed maximal reset-norm\nlaw, a particular signed carrier Gram, and its positive original\nrow(16,35), with a separate value-rigidity argument. Those are not\nour changing arc gradients or our common static max. Similarly,\n88f3db7b0a2bb506 concerns a specified Haar-direction rank-one\ngradient recurrence and its cycle on physical queries1,2,3. We did\nnot rename or re-use that recurrence. Our new cycle is the explicit\nthree-endpoint cycle (42), on queries2,5,8 of (39).\n\nAccepted0e7e78056dc6258c assumes orthogonality of the expected inner\nproducts of EVERY pair of queried gradients. Equation (9) already\nviolates that hypothesis in the present deterministic example. No\nrandomization or averaging of illegal transcripts has been used.\n\nWhat is established only at joint-draft level:\n\n* A full actual Euclidean metric calculation and local support-arc\n  construction, including activation, adjacent transfer and thresholds.\n* One complete two-checkpoint six-step static envelope, all physical\n  updates, every retained value and proximal height, every directed row,\n  a common attained minimum and its EXACT closest radius (36).\n* The sole correction's actual terminal mixture and value (31),(34).\n* An exact value-independent queried-prefix cycle excluding the\n  saturated repeated rule (38) whenever T1/T0>K/(ell*H), with its\n  precise small-angle scaling (59)-(60).\n* Full metric and overlap accounting. The simple initial norm of the\n  calibration stays bounded; the dual-frame cost (3)-(4) applies only\n  when the corresponding coordinate constraints are actually prescribed.\n\nThe missing unbounded interface is now specific. A continuation must\nconstruct one static function for arbitrarily many stages, retain the\nactual mixtures and their positive proximal heights, prove ALL original\nrows including the signed remote constraints replacing (61), and control\nthe resulting initial closest radius and the accumulated physical-step\neffects in (55). Alternatively it must change the adjacent scale/height\npolicy and give a new all-history proof. No benchmark was recomputed. The earlier scalar obstruction717b541e05f4484f was read\nin its exact reduced/specified-radius scope, and is not used as a lemma.\n\n## 1. The precise scope and conclusions\n\nLet h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be a finite nonnegative physical word. For a\nconvex differentiable function f with 1-Lipschitz gradient and attained\nminimum, the actual trajectory is x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t grad f(x_t). Its\nclosest-distance payoff is\n\n    E(f,h)=2(f(x_n)-min f)/dist(x_0,argmin f)^2.          (1)\n\nThe symbol W_n(h) denotes the supremum of this payoff over all such\nfunctions, initial points with positive distance, and finite Euclidean\ndimensions. None of the upper bounds below is an upper bound on W_n.\n\nThis manuscript analyzes the SAME two explicitly defined static spiral\nrealizations as the source, at the fixed advertised smoothness L=1.\nWe derive their full minimizer sets for every checkpoint count, their\nexact or finite-projection nearest-distance structure, and their actual\nterminal payoffs. The comparison theorem is deliberately restricted to\nZERO OR ONE selected checkpoint. It includes every eligible position,\nevery independent parameter pair kappa>0, 0<epsilon<=1/2, and every\nactual ordered block at that position. There is no assertion about the\noptimized family with two or more checkpoints.\n\nFor lambda=1+sqrt(2), fix 0<tau<=1/4 and an integer m>=1536. Define\n\n    beta=1-tau/m, c=lambda(1+tau/m), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    H_(m,tau)=H_m, n_m=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j.             (2)\n\nEvery occurrence of a child H_j uses this SAME enclosing pair (m,tau).\nLet H_base(h)=1/(1+2 sum h). The joint comparison conclusion is:\n\n    E(f,h)<(441/500) H_base(h)                         (3)\n\nfor EVERY one-checkpoint realization of EITHER kind below on\nh=H_(m,tau), using that realization's own eligibility threshold.\nThe two zero-checkpoint realizations have payoffs H_base(h) and\n1/[2(1+sum h)], respectively. Consequently the supremum over these\nactual zero-or-one-checkpoint functions, normalized by their TRUE\nclosest-minimizer distances, is exactly\n\n    L_actual,<=1(H_(m,tau))=H_base(H_(m,tau)).           (4)\n\nIn particular the normalization improvement does not repair the\nsmall-tau benchmark deficit in this precisely delimited subfamily:\n\n    B_(n_m) L_actual,<=1(H_(m,1/4))<237/250,\n    B_(n_m) L_actual,<=1(H_(m,tau))<79/80\n                              if 1/5<=tau<=1/4.       (5)\n\nFor fixed 0<tau<=1/4 the exact limit is Z_1/(2 exp(tau)).\nB_n and Z_1 are the ORIGINAL accepted benchmark quantities defined in\nSection9. These conclusions do not reuse the old reduced-channel\nestimate to upper-bound actual payoffs.\n\nTwo actual boundary tests delimit the argument. With two source-format\ncheckpoints, copying the one-checkpoint radius can overstate its square\nby a factor of five. Also two ordinary blocks with the same sum can\nhave different backward P,Q outputs. Neither test is an escape on the\nsmall-tau family or a counterexample to the full construction.\n\n## 2. Arc, backward chronology, and actual displacement\n\nFix independent 0<epsilon<=1/2 and kappa>0. Write\n\n    theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon),\n    C=c_sp=sqrt(1+epsilon^2) exp(kappa theta_sp),\n    R(theta)=C exp(-kappa theta),\n    e(theta)=(cos theta,-sin theta),\n    t(theta)=(-sin theta,-cos theta),\n    v(theta)=R(theta)e(theta)=(p(theta),-q(theta)),\n    n(theta)=e(theta)+kappa t(theta), 0<=theta<=theta_sp.\n\nC here is an arc constant, distinct from the benchmark C_n.\nThe endpoints are v(0)=(C,0) and v(theta_sp)=(epsilon,-1).\nLet K be the convex hull of 0 and this compact arc. For\ndelta=phi-theta,\n\n    <n(theta),v(phi)>/R(theta)\n         =exp(-kappa delta)(cos delta+kappa sin delta).\n\nIts derivative is -(1+kappa^2)exp(-kappa delta)sin delta, so its\nmaximum on the relevant interval (-pi/2,pi/2) is1 at delta=0.\nIts value at the extra point0 is0. Thus n(theta) supports K at\nv(theta). The vector (0,1) also supports K at v(0).\nFurthermore\n\n    p'=-R(kappa cos theta+sin theta)<0,\n    q'=R(cos theta-kappa sin theta),\n    p>=epsilon>0, q>=0, p>=epsilon q.                 (6)\n\nFor an actual chronological ordinary block alpha_1,...,alpha_r,\nalpha_j>=0, construct backward states as follows. Set\nr_r=v_r=(epsilon,-1). Given r_j=(u_j,-y_j) and its projected arc\npoint v_j=v(theta_j), let alpha=alpha_j. If\n\n    y_j<=kappa[u_j+(alpha-1)C],                        (7)\n\nput v_(j-1)=v(0), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v(0). Otherwise take the unique\ntheta in (0,theta_j] satisfying\n\n    <r_j,e(theta)-kappa^(-1)t(theta)>/R(theta)=1-alpha, (8)\n\nand put v_(j-1)=v(theta), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v(theta).\nThese are exact implicit definitions, never executed root searches.\n\nHere is a full projection and existence proof. Inductively\n\n    u_j>=epsilon, y_j>=1, y_j/u_j<=1/epsilon,           (9)\n\nand, before a cap,\n\n    r_j=R(theta_j)[(1+t_j)e(theta_j)+kappa t_j t(theta_j)],\n    t_j>=0.                                           (10)\n\nThey hold at the endpoint with t_r=0. In (7), set\nmu=u_j+(alpha-1)C. Then mu>=y_j/kappa>0 and\nr_(j-1)-v(0)=mu n(0)+(0,kappa mu-y_j), a nonnegative combination\nof supporting normals. This proves projection at the cap. The new\ninequality y_(j-1)<=kappa(u_(j-1)-C) makes every earlier step capped.\n\nIf (7) fails, the point is uncapped. The left side of (8) equals1\nat theta_j, is less than1-alpha at0, and has strictly positive\nderivative (kappa+kappa^(-1))(u_j cos theta+y_j sin theta)/R(theta).\nThus the stated root exists and is unique, including alpha=0 at the\ncurrent endpoint. The polar angle of r_j is at least theta_j by(10),\nso <r_j,t(theta)> >=0 at this earlier angle. Equation(8) makes\nr_j+(alpha-1)v(theta) a nonnegative multiple of n(theta), proving\nprojection and(10) at the preceding state. Adding alpha(p,-q), using\n(6), preserves(9). No chronological step or zero is removed.\n\nFor the actual outputs define\n\n    P=sum_j alpha_j p(theta_(j-1)),\n    Q=sum_j alpha_j q(theta_(j-1)), s=sum_j alpha_j.\n\nThen\n\n    r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q), P>=epsilon Q, 0<=P<=Cs.      (11)\n\nWe also need the uniform Q bound used to define the source's scales.\nSet d_sp=1-kappa^2 and\n\n    T_sp=(exp(d_sp theta_sp/kappa)-1)/d_sp, 0<kappa<1,\n    T_sp=theta_sp/kappa,                         kappa>=1,\n    H_sp=C[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]-1,\n    A_sp=epsilon+(1+H_sp)/epsilon.                    (12)\n\nFor consecutive uncapped angles theta_old<=theta_new with difference\nDelta, resolving the exact backward update along t(theta_old) gives\n\n    t_old=B_D+A_D t_new,\n    A_D=exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),\n    B_D=exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta/kappa.\n\nBoth are nonnegative. Differentiation gives\nA_D'-(d_sp/kappa)A_D=-(1+kappa^(-2))exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta<=0.\nThus A_D<=exp(d_sp Delta/kappa). If kappa<1, B_D<=Delta/kappa and\n1+d_sp B_D<=exp(d_sp Delta/kappa); telescope to get t_j<=T_sp.\nIf kappa>=1, use A_D<=1 and B_D<=Delta/kappa to get the same bound\nwith its second definition. Consequently\ny_j=R[(1+t_j)sin theta_j+kappa t_j cos theta_j]\n<=C[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]. A cap leaves y unchanged, so\n\n    0<=Q<=H_sp.                                      (13)\n\nThe exact maximum of q is\n\n    qmax=1, kappa<=epsilon;\n    qmax=sqrt((1+epsilon^2)/(1+kappa^2))\n         exp(kappa[theta_sp-arctan(1/kappa)]), kappa>epsilon.\n\nThis follows from the sign of q' in(6). Define\n\n    N_sp=max(0,kappa qmax-epsilon),\n    B_sp=1+max(epsilon^(-2),N_sp/epsilon).              (14)\n\nAt every projected state y_j>=q_j, by(10), or by q_j=0 at a cap.\nAlso u_j-p_j>=-N_sp: a positive horizontal normal gives a\nnonnegative displacement; with a negative normal, theta>arctan(1/kappa)\nand p_j<=kappa qmax, whereas u_j>=epsilon. At a cap u_j>=C.\n\nThe new lower bound, in the direction needed for an upper bound on\nnormalized error, is particularly simple. Since R(theta)>=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)\nand cos theta+sin theta>=1 on [0,pi/2],\n\n    p(theta)+q(theta)>=1,\n    P+Q>=s,\n    (epsilon+P)^2+(1+Q)^2\n        >=(1+epsilon+P+Q)^2/2 >=(1+epsilon+s)^2/2.     (15)\n\nThe first quadratic inequality is the square of the elementary\nEuclidean comparison (u+y)^2<=2(u^2+y^2). It uses actual P,Q,\nnot the upper bound P<=Cs. It imposes no sum-only dependence on them.\n\n## 3. Local planes, their exact zero wedge, and smooth envelopes\n\nTake a local selected step b>=1+epsilon^(-2), and any sigma>0 with\n\n    sigma(P+epsilon b)<=1,\n    sigma[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=1.                 (16)\n\nSet\n\n    z_circ=(1-sigma(epsilon+P), sigma(1+Q))=(c_1,c_2),\n    U=1-sigma(P+epsilon b), Y=sigma Q,\n    V={0,v(0),v_0,...,v_r},\n    phi(z)=sigma max_(v in V)<v,z-z_circ>.             (17)\n\nThe finite set retains the endpoint and cap slopes and every actual\nbackward projected slope. Each (p,-q) in it equals\n[(p-epsilon q)/C](C,0)+q(epsilon,-1), with nonnegative coefficients.\nTherefore its exact zero set is\n\n    Z_phi={z: z_1<=c_1,\n                 epsilon(z_1-c_1)-(z_2-c_2)<=0}.      (18)\n\nBoth endpoint constraints are necessary and the displayed conic\nrepresentation proves sufficiency for every other plane.\n\nThe function phi is nonnegative. From(16) and p>=epsilon q one has\n<v,-z_circ><=0 for every v. It follows that phi=0 on z_1<=0,z_2>=0.\nAlso <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>=sigma[(1-b)epsilon p+q]<=0, so\nphi(U,y)=0 for y>=Y. These statements keep ALL local planes.\n\nFor any nonnegative finite maximum J of affine functions having a zero,\ndefine env_1 J(x)=min_z[J(z)+||x-z||^2/2]. The proximal minimizer is\nunique by coercivity and strict convexity. If z is this point and\ng=x-z, then g belongs to the convex subdifferential of J at z.\nConversely this subgradient condition proves proximal optimality by\nexpanding the squared norm. For another input and its pair z',g',\nsubgradient monotonicity gives ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>. A trial point\nz gives the upper increment bound <g,w>+||w||^2/2; using z' and the\nsubgradient inequality at z gives the lower bound\n<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2. These prove differentiability, convexity,\ngradient g, and 1-Lipschitz continuity. Nonnegativity and attainment\nshow EXACTLY\n\n    argmin(env_1 J)={J=0}, min(env_1 J)=0.              (19)\n\nAt x_j=z_circ+sigma r_j, let z_j=x_j-sigma v_j. The projection\ninequalities proved in Section2 hold against every vector of V, hence\nsigma v_j is an active subgradient of phi at z_j. Thus these are the\nactual gradients of env_1 phi. The path starts at (1,0), follows\neach actual ordinary alpha_j, then reaches (U+sigma epsilon b,Y)\nwith gradient sigma(epsilon,-1). Its selected step b reaches\n(U,Y+sigma b). The first physical coordinate decreases and the second\nincreases, with x_(j),1>=U, 0<=x_(j),2<=Y during the ordinary block.\n\nAt the local proximal points, the more precise bounds are\n\n    0<=z_(j),2=sigma(1+Q-y_j+q_j)<=sigma(1+Q),\n    z_(j),1-U=sigma[epsilon(b-1)+u_j-p_j]>=0\n                                               if b>=B_sp. (20)\n\nThe second bound pays N_sp through(14); the first uses y_j>=q_j,\nincluding the overshooting arc. No error is dropped at a checkpoint.\n\n## 4. First realization: full minimum set, rows, and exact radius\n\nFor any k>=1 selected checkpoints, write the actual word as\n(block_0,b_0,block_1,...,b_(k-1),block_k), with block sums s_i.\nRun Section2 separately on each ACTUAL full-step block_i, i<k.\nRequire b_i>=B_sp, and use\n\n    M_i=P_i+max(epsilon b_i,epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon),\n    sigma_i=1/M_i,\n    D_0=1, ell_0=0,\n    D_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(b_i-1),\n    ell_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i).                      (21)\n\nUse (17) to define phi_i, c_(i,1),c_(i,2),U_i,Y_i and put\n\n    Phi_i(z)=D_i^2 phi_i((z_i-ell_i)/D_i,z_(i+1)/D_i), i<k,\n    delta=D_k/(1+2s_k), Phi_k(z)=delta max(z_k-ell_k,0),\n    J(z)=max_(0<=i<=k)Phi_i(z), f=env_1 J, x_0=e_0.    (22)\n\nAll D_i are positive. This is one function fixed before its run;\nall old and future pieces remain in the maximum. Conditions(16) hold.\nJ is nonnegative, and J(0)=0 by its pre-activation zero wedges.\nEquation(19) makes f convex and 1-smooth with minimum0.\n\nAt the start of block i the frozen coordinates are ell_j+D_j U_j\nfor j<i, the current coordinate is ell_i+D_i, and all later ones\nare0. At each candidate local proximal point the current piece has\nthe local subgradient. The preceding piece is zero: its first input\nis U_(i-1), and its second is at least ell_i/D_(i-1)>=Y_(i-1),\nby(20). Earlier pieces stay in their zero wedges. The next piece\nhas first input at most0 because z_(i+1)<=ell_(i+1), by(20),\nand second input0. Every further piece and the hinge are also zero.\nThus the current subgradient is a subgradient of J, even at a tie.\nThe local proximal point is the actual global proximal point.\n\nAfter b_i the new coordinate equals D_i sigma_i(Q_i+b_i)\n=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1), proving this state induction. In the tail, if u\nis its already executed mass, the moving coordinate equals\nell_k+D_k-u delta. Its candidate proximal coordinate is at least\nell_k because D_k-(u+1)delta>=0 for 0<=u<=s_k. All old pieces are\nzero there. Therefore the actual gradient is delta e_k throughout\nthe tail, including at the terminal point, and\n\n    2f(x_n)=D_k^2/(1+2s_k).                            (23)\n\nFor ALL directed pairs of samples a,b, including the initial point,\nall queried points, terminal point, and ANY minimizer, put\ng_a=grad f(x_a), z_a=x_a-g_a. Then f_a=J(z_a)+||g_a||^2/2 and\n\n    Q_ab=f_a-f_b+<g_a,x_b-x_a>+||g_a-g_b||^2/2\n        =J(z_a)-J(z_b)+<g_a,z_b-z_a><=0.               (24)\n\nThis proves every original smooth-convex interpolation row in its\nspecified orientation, without deleting optimum or terminal rows.\nThe actual Gram matrix is that of vectors in R^(k+1), hence is\npositive semidefinite. Zero steps preserve identical actual jets.\n\nThe full minimizer set, directly from (18),(19),(22), is\n\n    C_env={x: x_i<=zeta_i,\n                 epsilon x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon zeta_i-D_i c_(i,2), i<k;\n                 x_k<=ell_k},\n    zeta_i=ell_i+D_i c_(i,1).                          (25)\n\nDefine x_*=(c_(0,1),c_(0,2),0,...,0). The first component is at\nits center. Its second coordinate equals ell_1, so the next component\nhas local input(0,0), and all remaining components are in their\npre-activation zero wedges; the hinge is zero. Thus x_* is feasible.\nThe vector e_0-x_* has the active-normal representation\n\n    e_0-x_*=sigma_0[(P_0-epsilon Q_0)/C](C,0,0,...)\n                  +sigma_0(1+Q_0)(epsilon,-1,0,...).\n\nBoth coefficients are nonnegative. For every x in C_env this implies\n<e_0-x_*,x-x_*><=0. Expanding ||e_0-x||^2 proves that x_* is the\nunique projection and gives\n\n    rho_env^2=dist(e_0,C_env)^2\n      =sigma_0^2[(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2],           (26)\n    E_env=(b_0-1)^2/([(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2](1+2s_k))\n                     *product_(i=1)^(k-1)[(b_i-1)/M_i]^2. (27)\n\nIn particular at k=1,\n\n    E_env<=[2(b-1)^2]/[(1+s)^2(1+2t)],                (28)\n\nwhere s,t are its actual prefix and suffix sums. This uses (15) as\na LOWER radius bound; it never uses P<=Cs to upper-bound payoff.\n\n## 5. Second realization: full minimum set and nearest-distance structure\n\nUse the same selection, but now require b_i>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)).\nApply Section2 to the actual ordinary blocks with EVERY physical step\ndivided by2; P_i,Q_i in this section mean those HALF-step outputs.\nIn (17) use the local selected step beta_i=b_i/2 and scale\n\n    sigma'_i=1/[Cs_i/2+epsilon b_i/2+A_sp].             (29)\n\nBoth conditions(16) follow from(11),(13). Define primed local objects\nphi'_i,c'_(i,1),c'_(i,2),U'_i,Y'_i and f_i=env_1 phi'_i. Put\n\n    G_i=sigma'_i b_i/2,\n    D'_0=1, ell'_0=0,\n    D'_(i+1)=D'_i G_i, ell'_(i+1)=D'_i sigma'_i Q_i,\n    delta'=D'_k/(1+s_k).\n\nLet Hub_d(z) be0 for z<=0, z^2/2 for 0<=z<=d, and\ndz-d^2/2 for z>=d. The one static source-format function is\n\n    F(x)=(1/2)sum_(i<k)(D'_i)^2\n           f_i((x_i-ell'_i)/D'_i,x_(i+1)/D'_i)\n               +(1/2)Hub_(delta')(x_k-ell'_k), x_0=e_0. (30)\n\nIt is nonnegative with F(0)=0. Every local term is convex and\n1-smooth on its two coordinates. Their quadratic upper bounds,\nafter multiplication by1/2, count each coordinate at most twice,\nincluding the hinge. Hence F(x+w)<=F(x)+<grad F(x),w>+||w||^2/2.\nFor a differentiable convex F this implies\n\n    F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>\n                     >=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.    (31)\n\nIndeed apply that upper bound to V(z)=F(z)-<grad F(y),z> at x and\nw=-grad V(x); y minimizes V. Adding(31) and its exchanged version\nproves 1-Lipschitz continuity of grad F. Taking x=x_b,y=x_a in(31)\nis exactly Q_ab<=0 in(24)'s orientation for every original pair.\nThe optimum and terminal rows are included.\n\nAt the start of stage i, the frozen coordinates are\nell'_j+D'_j U'_j for j<i, current coordinate ell'_i+D'_i, and later\ncoordinates0. During its local PHYSICAL path, x_i>=ell'_i and\n0<=x_(i+1)<=ell'_(i+1). Thus all old terms are in post-checkpoint\nzero wedges and all future terms are in pre-activation zero wedges.\nThey have gradient0, since they are differentiable and nonnegative.\nOnly the current term acts; its coefficient1/2 turns each physical\nstep into the stipulated local half step. The selected step gives\nnext coordinate ell'_(i+1)+D'_(i+1). This proves the actual trajectory,\nwithout using a horizontal proximal-coordinate bound. In the tail\nthe physical gradient is delta'/2; the final coordinate above ell'_k\nis D'_k-delta' s_k/2>=delta'. Substitution yields\n\n    2F(x_n)=(D'_k)^2/[2(1+s_k)].                       (32)\n\nAll original pieces, translations, scales, and the 1/2 coefficient\nare retained. The specified origin is a minimizer at radius1, but\nwe now divide(32) by the ACTUAL closest distance.\n\nBecause each term is nonnegative and each envelope has zero set(18),\nthe complete minimum set is the nonempty closed polyhedron\n\n    C_source={x: x_i<=zeta'_i,\n                 epsilon x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon zeta'_i-D'_i c'_(i,2), i<k;\n                 x_k<=ell'_k},\n    zeta'_i=ell'_i+D'_i c'_(i,1).                      (33)\n\nIt contains0. For k>=1, e_0 violates its first horizontal constraint,\nso its squared distance rho_source^2 is positive and at most1.\nSince C_source is a subset of the first local zero wedge, projection\nonto that wedge by the normal argument of Section4 gives the genuine\nlower bound\n\n    rho_source^2 >= (sigma'_0)^2\n                   [(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2].       (34)\n\nIn general this is a lower bound, NOT the exact distance. Unlike the\nfirst realization, c'_(0,2)=sigma'_0(1+Q_0) is larger than\nell'_1=sigma'_0 Q_0. Later constraints must be checked.\n\nFor completeness an exact finite projection formula, with all those\nconstraints retained, is as follows. Let A_rad consist of rows\ne_i^T and (epsilon e_i-e_(i+1))^T for i<k, followed by e_k^T,\nand let t_rad consist of the right sides in(33). For each independent\nrow subset S, put A_S=(A_rad)_S, r_S=A_S e_0-(t_rad)_S,\nK_S=A_S A_S^T and lambda_S=K_S^(-1)r_S. Include the empty subset\nwith value0. Then\n\n    rho_source^2=max_(S independent,lambda_S>=0)\n                          r_S^T K_S^(-1)r_S.          (35)\n\nThis is an expression, not an executed solver. For any feasible x and\nlambda>=0 on S, completing a square gives\n||e_0-x||^2>=2lambda^T r_S-||A_S^T lambda||^2. At lambda_S this is\nthe displayed entry, proving every such lower bound. At the actual\nprojection x_*, the normal e_0-x_* lies in the nonnegative cone of\nits active constraint rows. To justify this without importing a\nduality theorem, any direction having nonpositive inner products\nwith all active rows is feasible for a sufficiently small move;\nprojection optimality forces its inner product with e_0-x_* to be\nnonpositive. A finitely generated cone is closed: remove linear\ndependences from any nonnegative representation until independent\ngenerators remain; it is a finite union of closed injective images\nof orthants. If the normal were outside that cone, subtracting its\nprojection on the cone would contradict the preceding feasible-\ndirection inequality. Choose independent active rows representing\nthe normal. Activity gives r_S=K_S lambda, and their value in(35)\nequals the squared distance. This proves equality.\n\n## 6. Exact simplification with one source-format checkpoint\n\nSuppose k=1, write s,t for the prefix and suffix sums, and omit\nprimes on the local sigma for this paragraph. P,Q are still the\nHALF-step outputs of the whole ordered prefix. Formula(33) becomes\n\n    x_0<=1-sigma(epsilon+P),\n    epsilon x_0-x_1<=epsilon-sigma(epsilon^2+epsilon P+1+Q),\n    x_1<=sigma Q.                                    (36)\n\nDefine E_0=P+epsilon+1/epsilon, a local abbreviation distinct from\nthe payoff E(f,h). The point\n\n    x_*=(1-sigma E_0,sigma Q)                         (37)\n\nsatisfies all three constraints: the second and third are equalities,\nand the first has slack sigma/epsilon. Its normal from e_0 is\n\n    e_0-x_*=sigma(E_0,-Q)\n       =sigma(E_0/epsilon)(epsilon,-1)\n          +sigma(E_0/epsilon-Q)(0,1).                 (38)\n\nThe coefficients are positive because\nE_0/epsilon-Q=(P/epsilon-Q)+1+epsilon^(-2)>0 by(11).\nThus(38), using the active second and third constraints, proves\nprojection by squared-distance expansion. We obtain the closed formula\n\n    rho_source^2=sigma^2[(P+epsilon+1/epsilon)^2+Q^2],  (39)\n    E_source=b^2/[8(1+t)((P+epsilon+1/epsilon)^2+Q^2)]. (40)\n\nBoth the first scale sigma and its activation allowance cancel\ncorrectly, but the actual P,Q remain. This is an exact payoff of the\noriginal function(30), not a new scale choice or a reset minimum.\n\nBy(15) for half steps, P+Q>=s/2. Since epsilon+1/epsilon>=5/2\non 0<epsilon<=1/2, the denominator in(39) obeys\n\n    (P+epsilon+1/epsilon)^2+Q^2\n       >=(P+Q+epsilon+1/epsilon)^2/2 >=(s+5)^2/8.\n\nConsequently the actual one-checkpoint source payoff satisfies\n\n    E_source<=b^2/[(1+t)(s+5)^2].                     (41)\n\nThis is a LOWER bound on its actual squared radius and an UPPER\nbound on its actual payoff, with both directions justified.\n\n## 7. All one-checkpoint selections on the small-tau hierarchy\n\nFor(2), put\n\n    r_h=c(c-2), v_h=a/(c-2)=beta/r_h,\n    M_j=1+S_j, d_j=a c^j, u_j=d_j/M_j.\n\nThe symbol r_h is a hierarchy constant, not a backward vector r_j.\nWith x=tau/m and gamma=4+2sqrt(2), expansion gives\nr_h=1+gamma x+lambda^2 x^2>1>beta>0. Hence0<v_h<1. From the word\nrecurrence M_(j+1)=2M_j+a c^j and M_0=1, induction gives\n\n    M_j=v_h c^j+(1-v_h)2^j,\n    v_h c^j<=M_j<=c^j,\n    a<=u_j<=c-2.                                     (42)\n\nThe simple constants needed uniformly on the assigned domain are\n\n    c<121/50, 1/4<a<=u_j<21/50.                      (43)\n\nIndeed sqrt(2)<283/200 by squaring, so lambda<483/200. Also\nc<=lambda(1+1/6144)<484/200=121/50 because483<6144.\nMoreover beta>=1-1/6144>3/4 and c<3, proving a>1/4.\n\nChoose ANY occurrence of the sole checkpoint, at level j. Its value\nis b=1+d_j. Its two immediate complete children are H_j, and neither\ncontains a selected checkpoint because this is the only selection.\nThus the ACTUAL prefix sum s and suffix sum t satisfy\n\n    X=1+s>=M_j, Y=1+t>=M_j,\n    S_m=s+b+t=X+Y+d_j-1.                             (44)\n\nThere may be arbitrarily much exterior mass on either side. No\nassumption of root, first-occurrence, or record selection is used.\n\nFor the main realization, (28) and(44) give\n\n    E_env/H_base(H_m)\n      <=2d_j^2(2X+2Y+2d_j-1)/[X^2(2Y-1)].            (45)\n\nFor fixed Y,d_j this decreases with X>0: the derivative of\n(2X+K)/X^2, where K=2Y+2d_j-1>0, is -2/X^2-2K/X^3.\nFor fixed X,d_j it decreases with Y>1/2 because the numerator\nequals (2Y-1)+2X+2d_j. Hence(45) is at most\n\n    2u_j^2(4M_j+2d_j-1)/(2M_j-1)\n      =2u_j^2[2+u_j+(1+u_j)/(2M_j-1)].               (46)\n\nMain eligibility implies b>=1+epsilon^(-2)>=5, so d_j>=4.\nBy(43), M_j=d_j/u_j>200/21>19/2, hence2M_j-1>18.\nUsing(43), the bracket in(46) is strictly less than\n\n    121/50+(71/50)/18=2249/900<5/2.\n\nThus\n\n    E_env/H_base(H_m)<5(21/50)^2=441/500.             (47)\n\nAll independent kappa,epsilon values, including kappa>1 with the\nfull N_sp threshold, have been included: their only use here was\nactual eligibility and the universally valid displacement bound.\n\nFor the source realization, (41) gives\n\n    E_source/H_base(H_m)\n       <=(1+d_j)^2(2X+2Y+2d_j-1)/[Y(X+4)^2].         (48)\n\nSource eligibility gives b>=2(1+epsilon^(-2))>=10, so d_j>=9.\nFor fixed X,d_j the ratio decreases with Y, since its varying part\nis 2+(2X+2d_j-1)/Y. For fixed Y,d_j, differentiation of\n(2X+K)/(X+4)^2 gives (8-2X-2K)/(X+4)^3<0, where\nK=2Y+2d_j-1>=19. Thus(48) is at most\n\n    [(1+d_j)/(M_j+4)]^2[4+2u_j-1/M_j].              (49)\n\nSince u_j>1/4, we have (1+d_j)/(M_j+4)<=d_j/M_j=u_j.\nEquations(43),(49) now imply\n\n    E_source/H_base(H_m)<2u_j^2(2+u_j)\n                 <5(21/50)^2=441/500.               (50)\n\nEquations(47),(50) prove(3) for EVERY eligible checkpoint position\nand EVERY parameter pair. No trajectory was changed during these\ncomparisons; no minimum-level deletion was applied to block outputs.\n\nAt k=0 the first function is the hinge envelope\nHub_(1/(1+2S_m)) from initial1, with zero set(-infinity,0]. Its\nterminal payoff is 1/(1+2S_m). The second is\nHub_(1/(1+S_m))/2, with the same closest distance1 and payoff\n1/[2(1+S_m)]<H_base(H_m). This proves(4).\n\n## 8. Exact boundary tests and the failure of the multi-stage shortcut\n\nFirst, an all-zero ordinary block has every r_j=(epsilon,-1),\nevery v_j=(epsilon,-1), and P=Q=0. The two one-checkpoint formulas\ngive respectively (b-1)^2/[(1+epsilon^2)(1+2t)] and\nb^2/[8(1+t)(epsilon+1/epsilon)^2]. The radial bounds(15),(41)\nremain valid. Such a prefix is not an eligible one-checkpoint\nselection in H_(m,tau), whose complete child has positive mass at\nevery eligible level; the hierarchy proof does not assert otherwise.\n\nFor a single ordinary step alpha, whenever\nalpha>=1+(1/kappa-epsilon)/C, condition(7) selects the cap, so\n\n    P=C alpha, Q=0,\n    ||r_0||^2=(epsilon+C alpha)^2+1.                  (51)\n\nThis is an actual arbitrarily huge-step test of(15), not a limiting\ngradient-flow substitution. An equality threshold gives the same\nprojected point; zeros before a cap leave it capped.\n\nHere is exact order dependence at a fixed total mass. Set\nepsilon=kappa=1/2, theta_*=arctan(3/4), and\n\n    R_*=C exp(-theta_*/2), p_*=(4/5)R_*, q_*=(3/5)R_*,\n    A_*=1+2(1+q_*)/C.                                (52)\n\nFor a single last ordinary step1, the cap test fails, since1>1/4.\nEquation(8) reduces to 2sin theta-(3/2)cos theta=0, whose root\nis theta_*. Equivalently n(theta_*)=(1/2,-1), verifying the\nsupport relation exactly. For chronological block(A_*,1), the\nlast step first gives r=(1/2+p_*,-1-q_*). The preceding A_* caps\nbecause its right side in(7) is\n1+q_*+1/4+p_*/2>1+q_*. Its ACTUAL outputs are therefore\n\n    P=A_* C+p_*, Q=q_*>0.                            (53)\n\nFor chronological block(1,A_*), the last A_* already caps, since\nA_*>1+2/C>1+3/(2C), and the preceding unit step stays capped.\nIts outputs are instead\n\n    P=(A_*+1)C, Q=0.                                 (54)\n\nBoth blocks have sum A_*+1, while p_*<C and q_*>0. Thus actual\nP,Q are not functions of their sums. This test invalidates that\nshortcut, not any formula for the unchanged ordered construction.\n\nFinally test extension of the exact one-source-checkpoint radius(39).\nUse TWO source checkpoints b_0=b_1=10, all three ordinary blocks\nempty (or made of zeros), epsilon=kappa=1/2. Both checkpoints are\neligible exactly at the threshold. Then P_i=Q_i=0,\n\n    sigma=1/(A_sp+5/2), 0<sigma<1/5,\n    D'_1=5sigma, D'_2=25sigma^2, ell'_1=ell'_2=0.      (55)\n\nThe strict sigma bound follows from H_sp>0 and A_sp>5/2. The local\nfinite planes are exactly 0, (C,0), and (1/2,-1) about center\n(1-sigma/2,sigma). The point\n\n    x_*=(1-sigma/2,sigma,0)                           (56)\n\nis at the first center. The second local input is(1/5,0). It is\ninside its zero wedge: its horizontal constraint has positive slack,\nand its endpoint plane evaluates, before the positive scale,\n\n    (1/2)[1/5-(1-sigma/2)]+sigma\n                    =-2/5+(5/4)sigma<-3/20<0.\n\nThe terminal hinge is zero. Thus(56) belongs to the full(33), with\nevery old/future plane retained. Its normal from e_0 is\nsigma(1/2,-1,0), an active first endpoint normal. Projection follows,\nand hence\n\n    rho_source^2=(5/4)sigma^2.                        (57)\n\nBy contrast the proposed extrapolation of(39) would give\n(25/4)sigma^2, five times the actual squared distance. The tail cap\nx_1<=sigma Q_0 used in(36) is no longer a constraint; the genuine\ntail cap is x_2<=0. This is the exact failed interface.\n\nFor additional trajectory evidence, the actual gradients along the\ntwo selected steps of(30) are\n\n    g_0=(sigma/4,-sigma/2,0),\n    x_1=(1-5sigma/2,5sigma,0),\n    g_1=(0,5sigma^2/4,-5sigma^2/2),\n    x_2=(1-5sigma/2,5sigma-25sigma^2/2,25sigma^2).\n\nAt x_2 the local terms are zero and the tail value is\n(D'_2)^2/4, so the actual closest payoff is250sigma^2, while\nusing the incorrect squared radius would give50sigma^2. Smoothness\nand all interpolation rows follow directly from the static function\nand(31). This is a boundary test outside H_(m,tau); no small-tau\nescape or benchmark comparison is inferred from it.\n\n## 9. Original benchmark and the finite small-tau deficit\n\nUse ONLY accepted fact_id04219554e41d9750 for the following inputs.\nFor nonnegative u,v define\n\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    C_n=max_(0<=i<n)Omega(C_i,C_(n-i-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=i<n)G(C_i,B_(n-i-1)).                  (58)\n\nThese are the original ALL-split denominators and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\nThe accepted ray enclosure is\n\n    lambda^(-j)B_(2^j-1)<=Z_1\n           <=lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(2^j))+3/100)^2,       (59)\n\nwhere Z_1 is their positive finite limit. (60)\n\nSince19600<19602, sqrt(2)>140/99. The binomial identity\nlambda^10=3363+2378sqrt(2) implies lambda^10>6725, because\n(3363*99+2378*140)-6725*99=82>0. Thus(59) at j=10, (60), and\nsqrt(15910)<127 give\n\n    Z_1<(15910+381/50+9/10000)/6725\n         <15918/6725<237/100.                        (61)\n\nThe last integer comparison is1591800<1593825. All these are written\nscalar deductions from the existing accepted endpoint, not a new\nbenchmark evaluation and not a claim that the old draft is a fact.\n\nFor the hierarchy, (42) gives\n\n    (1+2S_m)/lambda^m\n      =2v_h(1+tau/m)^m+2(1-v_h)(2/lambda)^m-lambda^(-m). (62)\n\nAlso r_h-beta=(5+2sqrt(2))tau/m+lambda^2 tau^2/m^2\n<8tau/m+6tau^2/m^2<9/(4m) for the stated m,tau. Since r_h>1,\n\n    v_h=beta/r_h>1-9/(4m)>255/256,\n    lambda^(-m)<1/256.                               (63)\n\nFor m>=2, binomial truncation gives\n(1+tau/m)^m>=1+tau+tau^2/4. Combining(59),(61) with(4)\nproves precisely(5), for EVERY m>=1536. No choice of benchmark\noptimizer or alternate recurrence has been substituted.\n\nFor fixed tau>0, v_h tends1, (1+tau/m)^m tends exp(tau), and\n(2/lambda)^m tends0. Hence(62) tends2exp(tau). The accepted ray\nlimit(59) proves the stated limit for L_actual,<=1. It is a limit\nfor this delimited actual family, never an upper bound on W_n.\n\n## 10. Remaining interface, scope, and record\n\nThe endpoint projection mechanism succeeds for all actual one-checkpoint\nrealizations, with a uniform strict factor(3). It fully pays the true\ninitial distance, both half-step and full-step trajectories, every\naffine plane, the terminal value, and every directed interpolation row.\nIt uses no backward-state replacement and does not collapse ordered\nblocks to sums; sums enter only through inequalities proved from each\nactual projected slope.\n\nThe same mechanism has NOT proved an all-count family obstruction.\nFor the main realization the exact remaining expression is(27), with\neach M_i computed from its actual ordered block. For the source\nrealization it is (D'_k)^2/[2(1+s_k)rho_source^2] with(33)--(35).\nThe first-wedge lower bound(34) is valid but weaker than the special\none-stage formula(39); the explicit test(55)--(57) refutes copying\nthat stronger formula when a further stage replaces the first tail.\nThe exact equal-sum test(52)--(54) also bars replacing actual P_i,Q_i\nafter deleting a checkpoint without a new comparison proof.\n\nThus the unresolved task is to control the product of ACTUAL transfer\ndenominators together with the first-block or chained source distance\nwhen k>=2 on the same H_(m,tau) family. There is no claimed counterexample\nto its all-count comparison, no normalized escape, no conclusion about\nthe unrestricted W_n, and no original-target solution.\n\nMatlas was queried for the minimizer/projection interface and the\nendpoint-displacement bound; its results were inapplicable. Numerical\nchecks will not promote this text to the fact graph.\n\nFor an integer m>=2 set lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m,\nc=lambda(1+1/m), a=beta/c, r=2/c, w_j=r^j/sum_(k<m)r^k.\nKeep this same enclosing m at every level: H_0=(),\nH_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), n=2^m-1, S=sum H_m. The initial vector is NOT scaled.\n\nThere is an orthonormal coordinate e_v for each binary word v of length\nless than m. For a leaf s of length m define\nv_s=sum_(j<m)(-1)^(s_(j+1))*sqrt(w_j)*e_(s_1...s_j).\nThe full body K_m is the convex hull of these 2^m vectors, in dimension\n2^m-1. Each leaf has squared norm sum w_j=1. The feature matrix rows\nhave zero sum and are pairwise orthogonal: disjoint subtrees have zero\nproduct; a descendant's two signs cancel on an ancestor's constant\nsign region. Every row is nonzero. Thus the matrix has rank 2^m-1 and\nkernel the constant leaf vector. The leaves are affinely independent,\ntheir average is zero, and zero is interior to K_m. In particular this\nis the CENTERED simplex without a carrier or an extra dimension.\n\nLet K_scaled=delta*K_m and\nF_scaled(x)=max_(g in K_scaled)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2).\nStrict convexity of squared distance gives a unique maximizer g, and\ncompleting the square identifies\ng=P_(delta*K_m)(x)=delta*P_(K_m)(x/delta).\nThe projection characterization is <x-g,v-g><=0 for every vertex v.\nNecessity follows by a derivative along the segment from g to v;\nsufficiency follows by expanding ||x-v||^2-||x-g||^2. Adding the two\nnormal inequalities at x,x' gives ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>, proving\n1-Lipschitz continuity. The maximizing formulas sandwich\nF_scaled(x+d)-F_scaled(x)-<g,d> between zero and\n<P_(delta*K_m)(x+d)-g,d>, bounded by ||d||^2. Hence F_scaled is convex\nand differentiable with the stated gradient and global smoothness L=1.\nInside the body the gradient equals x; an open ball is contained there,\nso no smaller global Lipschitz constant is possible. Every gradient has\nnorm at most delta by convexity and the unit leaf norms. Along t*v_left\nwith t>=delta its norm equals delta, so this cap is sharp.\n\nThe zero gradient is feasible, hence F_scaled>=0 and F_scaled(0)=0.\nFor x!=0 a sufficiently small positive multiple t*x belongs to the\ninterior body and has <x,t*x>-||t*x||^2/2>0. Thus the WHOLE minimum set\nis {0}. With x0=v_left, the unscaled all-left leaf, the true squared\nclosest-minimizer distance is exactly R^2=1. Reported V=2F_scaled/(LR^2)\ntherefore equals 2F_scaled. Projection commutes with them\nby uniqueness. A vector fixed by all these automorphisms has zero\noff-spine coordinates, since the child swap at an off-spine node reverses\nthat coordinate. Induction in the actual GD update keeps every x_i in\nthe m-dimensional spine. Group the leaves by first-right depth k<m,\nand retain the all-left leaf as group m. Uniform averaging gives b_k\nwith j coordinate sqrt(w_j) for j<k, -sqrt(w_k) for j=k<m, and zero\nfor j>k; b_m has all coordinates sqrt(w_j). Averaging representations\nproves the fixed section is exactly conv{b_0,...,b_m}. Group masses p_k\nlift uniformly over 2^(m-k-1) leaves for k<m, and p_m is the last mass.\nFor a spine residual, its inner product with every full leaf in a group\nequals the product with b_k. Thus ALL reduced vertex normals give ALL\noriginal full-leaf normals, with no full-space diagnostic sampling.\n\nFor physical x_j=sqrt(w_j) z_j and group tail masses q_j=sum_(k>=j)p_k,\nq_0=1, the gradient coordinate is\ng_j=delta*sqrt(w_j)*(2q_(j+1)-q_j).\nProjection minimizes sum_j w_j*(z_j/delta-(2q_(j+1)-q_j))^2 over\n1=q_0>=q_1>=...>=q_m>=0. It is repeated at\nevery query and has no automatic implication about future queries.\n\nFor a proposed face I choose its first vertex v0 and independent edges\nE=(v-v0). Because\n1-h_t=-beta*c^(v2(t+1)-1) and\nsum_(j=1)^i v2(j)=i-s2(i), one has\n\n  Pi_i=(-1)^i*beta^i*c^(-s2(i)).\n\nWhenever a suffix from q through n is actually inside the body, induction\nin the physical updates gives\n\n  V=||x_q||^2*beta^(2(n-q))*c^(2*s2(q)-2m).\n\nThis is a CONDITIONAL identity; it does not assert that such a suffix\nexists. The frozen records checked all suffix queries, starting at90\nfor zeta8 and at85 for zeta16. No product or new payoff was evaluated\nfrom this formula after closure. The identity explains the loss of\nterminal value in those records without extending it to untested m.\n\nThe earlier core visit at zeta16 query49 is insufficient: its next\nphysical step is1.875, and query50 is outside. In the saved query50\ninverse barycentric coordinates the last mass is about -0.19209828,\nwith squared projection residual about4.2509e-7. The actual projection\nhas support {0,1,2}. No closeness-to-boundary ambiguity explains this\nexit. This reinforces the need to keep every physical query and to\ncheck fresh membership, even for a step below2.\n\nTwo independent obstructions to a useful conclusion remain. First,\nthe best registered noncalibration value remains below the accepted\nbenchmark upper comparison and below the global Huber witness, despite\nits much larger value than the old unit-body run. Second, the records\ngive no uniform all-m bound or useful lower law for the suffix face\nsequence. The exact early bridge law above does not close that missing\ninterface. Other scales and other initial points were not scanned.\nThe original unrestricted all-word exact-profile problem remains OPEN.\n\n\n---\n\n# Full frozen preregistration\n\n# Frozen scaled centered-tree discovery, max3 round55\n\nAuthority: TASK.md and master_guidance6216f7ee407cd7e9. Physical states stay unscaled; the projection\nsolver works at X=x/delta with the unit Haar vertices for conditioning,\nthen g=delta*P_K(X) is the actual physical gradient. Compare both with the rederived radial\n   Huber identity at every query and terminal V=1/(1+2S).\n2,3,4. Exactly m8 at zeta4,8,16 respectively, once each, only after a\n   passing calibration. A failed case is invalid and cannot select zeta.\n5. Exactly m10 at the zeta with greatest RECORDED VALID m8 terminal V;\n   ties choose the smaller zeta. No interpolation or further tuning.\n6. All exits consume the full8s, with no refund.\n\nPrimary arithmetic: actual Python float significand length is reported\nfrom sys.float_info (expected53 binary bits), fsum for dot products.\nIt solves positive-definite face-edge systems by Cholesky, starts at\nthe nearest vertex and enters a maximally violated normal. Core masses\nuse the triangular q recursion. Calibration disables the reduced core\nshortcut and uses independent full augmented Gram/equality systems with\npivoted Gaussian elimination, evolving a distinct full state.\n\nPrecision arithmetic:80 decimal significant digits, ROUND_HALF_EVEN,\nOverflow and Underflow traps. Fresh constants use decimal square roots\nand arithmetic, no float-derived constant. The affine systems are the\naugmented Gram/equality equations, solved by pivoted Gaussian elimination;\nstart at vertex0, enter the first violated normal, use the explicit\ninverse barycentric core formula. The baseline never supplies its faces.\n\nFloat tolerances: KKT, complementarity and reconstruction 2e-11 times\n(1+||X||+||P_K(X)||); feasibility/sum2e-12; entering normal1e-13 times\nthe same scale; linear residual2e-11*(1+max|rhs|). Cholesky diagonal\nresidual>1e-18; Gaussian pivot magnitude>1e-20. Calibration absolute\ncomparison2e-10. Decimal: KKT/reconstruction/complementarity/linear1e-60,\nfeasibility and entering normal1e-65, both pivot cutoffs1e-75.\nThe same KKT check is repeated in physical coordinates. Fresh core\nmembership requires EVERY computed mass>=0 without tolerance. The core\ngradient is assigned x only after that test and reconstruction checks.\nAn active-set first-blocking coefficient is assigned exact zero only\nafter its preassignment discrepancy is logged and <=feasibility tolerance;\nall other negative masses are retained for the solve or invalidate it.\nNo clipping, regularization or fallback thresholding is allowed.\n\nAt every query save x, x/delta, g, g/delta, all group masses, F, physical\nstep, prefix sum, residual x-g, complete physical/normalized normals,\nstationarity, complementarity, feasibility/reconstruction errors, core\ncandidate masses, strict/tolerance supports, signed norm-defect terms,\nnorm identity residual, pivot counts and all counters. Stable F is\n||g||^2/2+<x-g,g>, or ||x||^2/2 in a freshly checked core. Also save the\nalternate dot-product formula and relative discrepancy; never subtract\ntwo large squared norms to obtain F. Save negative-excess/value flags;\nnegative F beyond KKT_tol*delta^2 or excess below\n-KKT_tol*delta^2*(1+||X||) invalidates. Check ||g||^2<=delta^2*(1+10KKT_tol).\n\nNonfinite saved scalars invalidate. Count underflows in all dot-product\nmultiplications and GD products, all finite saved scalar checks, and\nsaved subnormals; record Decimal context flags. This detection is not\nexhaustive binary arithmetic tracing or an error enclosure. Full m2\nvertices, states, chosen weights and all60 face candidates are retained.\nNo full-space diagnostics are sampled: beyond m2 the exact symmetry\nreduction covers all leaves through all m+1 normals. Raw logs include\nevery query including the terminal projection. Same physical family, full trajectories/minimum sets, no lower-mass-to-upper error, no formal acceptance.", "evidence": "# Actual closest-distance spiral payoffs at arbitrary checkpoint count\n\nStatus: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical draft, with the precise scope\nstated in Section 12. Its complementary band remains open.\n\nThe four recalled findings172014083da57ddf,59d88015afb74486,\n717b541e05f4484f,c8fcf4ced5a34046 are provenance and warnings, not proof\npremises.
